    - Rarity is weighed over the retrieved text, not only over identities, and the title counts. Templates are named by their title, and the word "information" in one NDA's title would otherwise name that NDA for every question about confidential information.
    - A "unique word" condition was dropped: it blocked "the Northwind NDA" when the Northwind MSA was also retrieved.
    - The focus choices on mini-dev's pools — the only signal used — gave every wrong naming one of two causes, and three fixes followed. Acronyms count only in capitals ("Agreement for Prospective Purchasers" had become "app", matched by "does this app sell…"). All-capitals sentences are not read as names ('ANY INFORMATION', 'BE CONFIDENTIAL'). Only capitalised question words name a document ("does the document *state* that…" had matched a party "State …").
    - Fresh-dev's pools, read only after those fixes, show no wrong naming.
  - Contamination, stated: while a dev pool was inspected, the extracted identity of one fresh-test document was seen; it was there as a distractor. No fresh-test query or result was looked at before the test run.
- **Measured, dev** (final-context character recall, bge@30 unless stated; paired, documents resampled):

  | Set | P | C1 | **C2** | C3 | C5 | FNAME | ORACLE |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Pooled (1,362 q, 359 docs) | 77.5 | +3.1 [+2.0, +4.4] | **+3.6 [+2.4, +5.1]** (55 up, 0 down) | +3.6 | +3.2 | +4.7 | +5.5 [+3.7, +7.8] |
  | Mini-dev | 77.2 | +2.5 | **+2.5 [+0.4, +5.1]** | +2.5 | +2.5 | +4.9 | +5.1 |
  | Fresh-dev | 77.6 | +3.3 | **+4.1 [+2.5, +5.8]** | +4.1 | +3.5 | +4.6 | +5.7 |
  | MiniLM@10, pooled | 70.1 | +2.2 | **+2.5 [+1.5, +3.8]** (41 up, 1 down) | +2.5 | +2.2 | +3.6 | +4.5 |

  - C2, pooled: MRR@10 +2.5 [+1.6, +3.6], doc_hit@1 +0.7 [+0.2, +1.3], recall@10 +5.5 [+3.8, +7.4].
  - C2 by sub-corpus, bge@30 (MiniLM@10): ContractNLI 75.2 +3.1 (+1.9), CUAD 83.7 +4.0 (+2.9), MAUD 56.1 +4.2 (+2.9), PrivacyQA 99.0 +0.0 (+0.0).
  - Rank-1 chunk from another document: ContractNLI 93 → 90, MAUD 41 → 35, CUAD 13 → 13. The gain is the right agreement's clauses reaching the context; the reranker still often ranks another NDA's near-identical clause first.
  - Focus choice: the fused leader was the right document for 1,148 of 1,362 queries, C2's choice for 1,291. C2 switched 146 times: 143 to the right document, 0 away from a right one, 3 between wrong ones. The right document was in the pool for 1,348 queries, and none of the other 14 would have been named had it been added, so C4 had no headroom and was not run. C5 switched 284 times, 113 of them away from the right document: its identity-only rarity named words like "privacy" and "considered".
  - The file-name variant adds 1.1 points more (many of ContractNLI's names come from file names: "CopAcc", "VELCO"); the oracle adds 1.9 more.
  - Selection: all four measured candidates are eligible. C2 and C3 tie at +3.58. C1's +3.07 lies 0.51 below, just outside the 0.5-point window, so C2 is chosen (C3 needs one more search and up to 30 more pairs for no gain).
- **Measured, fresh-test, once** (Decision 80's sealed half: 935 queries, 320 documents; the shipped code against P on the same index, P run wherever the node named another document):

  | Metric | P | Shipped | Paired change |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | bge@30 recall@context | 74.6 [71.0, 78.1] | 77.2 [73.7, 80.4] | **+2.6 [+1.3, +4.1]** (28 up, 2 down) |
  | bge@30 MRR@10 | 43.5 | 45.6 | +2.1 [+1.1, +3.2] |
  | bge@30 doc_hit@1 | 89.2 | 90.5 | +1.3 [+0.5, +2.2] |
  | MiniLM@10 recall@context | 63.0 [58.9, 67.0] | 64.5 [60.4, 68.4] | **+1.5 [+0.5, +2.7]** (18 up, 3 down) |

  - By sub-corpus, bge@30: ContractNLI 61.9 → 64.6, CUAD 83.4 → 84.5, MAUD 53.8 → 61.4. Wrong top document: ContractNLI 53 → 49, CUAD 21 → 21, MAUD 27 → 19.
  - Focus choice: right 776 → 854 times. Of 85 switches, 81 went to the right document, 1 between wrong ones, and 3 — all MAUD — away from a right one. Those 3 are both bge losses; they are not investigated, so that the test half stays untouched.
  - The refusal gate admits 935/935 either way. T1, T2 and T3 pass.
- **Gates**:
  - Golden set `--baseline`: PASS. Production fact coverage@10 94.3 / @context 97.1 / recall@10 78.7 / MRR 77.2 / nDCG 73.8, identical to main. All 15 golden queries that ran focus kept their document: the sample deal's questions name "Vertex" or "Aurora", which every retrieved document mentions.
  - Answerability dev set: identical, question by question, to the same run with `NAMED_DOCUMENT_FOCUS=0` (0/27 false vetoes, 11/28 unanswerable caught, AUC 0.801). One unanswerable question's focus moved — DataFlow Systems' settlement, to the IP and litigation schedule — with no change to its verdict or score.
  - Offline suite: 1,815 → 1,850 passed. Ruff clean.
- **Cost**:
  - No extra search: the one focus search runs on another document. On switched queries the rerank pool is smaller: on dev, bge@30 averaged 40.9 → 33.3 pairs and MiniLM@10 26.5 → 25.1; on test, 39.4 → 35.2 and 26.2 → 24.8. The named agreement's passages overlap the fused candidates more.
  - The choice is Python over the retrieved payloads: p50 17 ms, p95 22 ms per legal pass on the development laptop, most of it tokenising the retrieved text. It returns before that when no retrieved document has an identity (an index not yet backfilled) or nothing capitalised matches.
  - Ingest: regexes over 4,000 characters. Backfill of the 714-file store (49,559 chunks, local mode): 135 s writing all 712 documents.
- **Resolution**: shipped as C2. Ingestion writes the identity; existing indexes need the backfill once and behave exactly as before until they get it. DEPLOYMENT.md says so. Focus stays legal-only.
- **Not verified**:
  - Real data rooms: several drafts or versions of one agreement (the version resolver already hides superseded ones), file names that do name the parties (deliberately not read), and agreements whose opening names no party.
  - PDF and DOCX openings, beyond fixtures; non-English contracts.
  - Questions that write names in lower case: they name nothing and keep the fused choice.
  - Multi-document and decomposed questions: LegalBench-RAG has none. A question naming two documents keeps the fused choice by construction.
  - The CPU host itself (MiniLM was ranked on the GPU) and the selection step's time on two ARM cores.
  - Why the 3 MAUD test questions switched away from the right merger agreement.
  - The rule and the extractor were refined on mini-dev's pools before any recall was measured; fresh-dev and the test half were not used for that.
- **Guarded by**:
  - `tests/test_document_identity.py`: titles; parties from clauses, covers, templates and descriptions; roles, blanks, addresses and capitalised sentences rejected; tokens and acronyms; the opening's order, PII skip and lifted headings; identity on every ingested chunk and never from the file name; the backfill equal to ingestion, idempotent, deal-scoped, removing stale fields, and its command line.
  - `tests/test_named_document_focus.py`: the party a question names; a title's short form deciding between two agreements with one counterparty; a title's short form that is also a legal form ("SPA", as for an S.p.A.) weighed through the capitals, where it once raised KeyError (found in integration review; no measured query reached it); topic words in a title, lower-case words and lower-case acronyms naming nothing; two documents named, no identity, one document, the switch; and, through the retrieval executor, focus searching the named document while keeping every candidate, with the fused choice kept otherwise.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/{document_identity,ingest_pipeline}.py, src/vector_db/{named_document,identity_backfill}.py, src/agents/retrieval_executor.py, .env.example, tests/test_{document_identity,named_document_focus}.py, README.md, DEPLOYMENT.md

## Decision 87: A MiniLM distilled from bge closes most of the CPU host's legal-retrieval gap at the same cost per pair, and is not adopted: it refuses one more golden answerable
- **Date**: Session 17
- **Context**: the CPU host reranks with `ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` at focus depth 10 (Decision 71). On LegalBench-RAG's fresh-dev documents, with all 714 files indexed, it puts 68.9% of the ground truth into the final context against bge-reranker-v2-m3@30's 77.6% on the GPU (−8.7, Decision 80). Larger or quantised small rerankers cost too much on two cores (Decisions 77, 81). Question: does a MiniLM-L6 with the same architecture (same tokenizer and 512 tokens, so the same cost per pair), fine-tuned to imitate bge (knowledge distillation) on in-domain question–passage pairs, close part of the gap without hurting anything else?
- **Method** (written into the scratch notes before any pool, score, training or evaluation):
  - *Training data*: LegalBench-RAG mini, all 776 queries. Its test half was used up by Decisions 67, 68 and 70, and its dev half tuned Decisions 67, 71, 77 and 85, so mini measures nothing here. The pools are what the real pipeline reranks, from the production candidate function on a store holding only the 72 mini files: the fused top 40 (production's 20 plus 20 hard negatives) and the leading document's 30 focus candidates, with passages formatted as production formats them. That gives 774 distinct queries and 37,903 pairs. The golden data room and questions, the answerability dev set, every fresh document and FinanceBench were never read.
  - *Targets*: bge's raw logits, computed as the GPU host computes them (fp16, 1,024 tokens, 12.4 ms a pair). On these pairs the original MiniLM's Spearman correlation with bge is 0.786.
  - *Recipes*: both start from the production MiniLM snapshot. R1 is MSE on logits. R2 is a listwise KL over each query's group plus 0.1 × MSE. Both use AdamW at 3e-5 for 4 epochs, with a checkpoint per epoch: 8 candidates.
  - *Selection*: fresh-dev (942 queries, 322 documents), production retrieval through the real node, final-context character recall at focus 10, then Decision 71's depth rule at 0, 10 and 30. The node's searches were recorded once from the real store and then replayed: fusion, focus, rerank, expansion and context assembly run unchanged, and the store's scrolls are answered by qdrant's own filter function over a copy of its payloads. The replay was accepted only after it reproduced the real-store runs exactly: MiniLM@0, @10 and @30 and one fine-tuned checkpoint at 10, on all 942 queries, with every metric, ranking, context and gate decision identical (3,768 of 3,768 runs). The real-store MiniLM runs were themselves identical to Decision 80's, query for query.
  - *Rule*, fixed before training:
    - (1) fresh-test: Decision 80's sealed 320 documents, opened once for one frozen candidate; its gain over MiniLM@10 needs a document-resampled interval above zero.
    - (2) golden set (CPU numerics): no more controls admitted, no more answerables refused, gated metrics within 2 points.
    - (3) FinanceBench dev: neither the evidence-page interval nor the evidence-token interval lies entirely below zero.
    - (4) answerability dev: no fewer unanswerables caught, no more false vetoes.
    - (5) CPU cost per pair within 5%.
- **Training and selection** (fresh-dev, final-context recall at focus 10; MiniLM 68.9):
  - R1, epochs 1–4: 73.1 / 72.9 / 72.3 / 72.7.
  - R2, epochs 1–4: 72.5 / 72.8 / 72.5 / 72.3.
  - More epochs fit the teacher more closely on the training pools (Spearman 0.957 → 0.975) and sharpen ranking (MRR@10 38.9 → 42.3 for R2) without adding final-context recall.
  - The rule picked **R1 after one epoch**.
  - Depth: 62.3 / 73.1 / **77.0** at 0 / 10 / 30, so the rule picked **30**. Like bge and Ettin, and unlike MiniLM, the distilled model ranks the extra in-document passages well.
  - Fresh-dev against MiniLM@10: **+8.2 [+5.7, +10.7]** (119 queries up, 29 down). Against bge@30: −0.5 [−2.4, +1.2].
  - The candidate, frozen with its weights' sha256 before fresh-test was opened, is R1 epoch 1 at depth 30.
- **Calibration**: `run_reranker_calibration.py` at depth 30 used 1,354 dev pairs; Spearman with bge is 0.879 (MiniLM 0.83). The model's raw scores already sit on bge's scale (p50 / p90 / p95: 0.0010 / 0.150 / 0.504 against bge's 0.0015 / 0.155 / 0.569), so the quantile map is close to the identity. Refusal ceiling: 0.015. On the dev set's heuristic gate, answerable questions go 21/5/1 and unanswerable ones 18/9/1, against MiniLM's 21/5/1 and 17/9/2.
- **Measured against the rule**:
  - (1) **fresh-test, opened once: passes.** 935 queries over 320 documents.
    - Final-context recall: MiniLM@10 63.0 [59.0, 66.9]; distilled@30 74.0 [70.5, 77.7]; bge@30 74.6 [71.0, 78.1].
    - Distilled against MiniLM@10: **+11.0 [+8.6, +13.7]** (140 queries up, 26 down). Against bge@30: −0.6 [−2.6, +1.4]. It closes 11.0 of the 11.6-point gap.
    - MRR@10: 20.1 → 37.0 (bge 43.5).
    - By sub-corpus, against MiniLM@10: MAUD +17.1 (bge +13.0), CUAD +11.0 (bge +14.3), ContractNLI +0.9 (bge −5.3).
  - (2) **golden set: fails.** Metrics stay within tolerance: production fact coverage @5 / @10 / @context 91.9→91.3 / 96.2→96.2 / 98.1→98.1, recall@10 80.1→81.0, MRR 73.6→78.9, nDCG 72.0→75.6. Controls are unchanged (1/5/0). Answerables go 30/4/1 → **29/4/2**. The extra refusal is mh_04 ("implied EV/EBITDA multiple of the Vertex deal"). MiniLM's best calibrated score, 0.336, left the heuristic undecided, so it went to the LLM assessor. The distilled model scores it 0.377, which yields a heuristic verdict (quality 0.422) that the router's per-dimension thresholds send to the rewrite loop. bge on the GPU admits it at 0.88, so the student lands between its teacher and MiniLM. Two of three facts are in the context with either CPU model. With decomposition, answerables improve (32/3/0 → 33/2/0), but that is reported, not gated.
  - (3) **FinanceBench dev: passes**, with no gain. Evidence page in context 63.0 → 61.6 (−1.4 [−8.3, +5.9]); evidence tokens −1.1 [−5.1, +2.0]; document hit@1 −2.7 [−11.8, +7.8].
  - (4) **answerability dev: passes.** The veto catches 10/28 → 11/28 unanswerables, false vetoes stay 0/26 → 0/26, and AUC goes 0.801 → 0.826. Reported but not gated: the heuristic gate alone, which is what the CPU host runs, admits 18 unanswerable dev questions against MiniLM's 16.
  - (5) **CPU cost per pair: passes.** 84.0 against 84.4 ms a pair on two emulated E-cores (ratio 0.995). On fresh-dev at depth 30 a legal question reranks 33.8 pairs against 23.3 at MiniLM's depth 10: about +0.8 s per legal question on two cores, inside Decision 71's budget.
- **Template inflation, stated in advance**: fresh-test questions share LegalBench-RAG's templates with the training questions ("Consider the Non-Disclosure Agreement between X and Y; …", MAUD's "Information about …"), and the passage text carries the file name the question's parties appear in. The gain on fresh-test therefore overstates what the model buys on other question styles. The out-of-template evidence agrees: FinanceBench shows no gain, and on the golden set ranking improves (MRR +5.4) while the gate refuses one more answerable.
- **Resolution**: not adopted. Rule (2) fails, so MiniLM-L6 at focus 10 stays the CPU host's reranker and no `src/` change ships. The checkpoint (`D:/redline_data/distill8/ckpt/r1_e1`, outside the repository, never uploaded) and an unshipped loader (`D:/redline_data/distill8/unshipped_loader/`) are kept in case the owner revisits the trade-off. The loader matches a calibration profile by the weights' fingerprint, so a local directory or a later Hub id resolves to it. What blocks adoption is a gate question rather than a ranking one: mh_04's context holds the same two of three facts with either model, and it is refused because the distilled model scores it confidently enough for the heuristic to decide.
- **Also fixed** (harness): `eval/decisions/run_reranker_calibration.py` could not calibrate a reranker that has no profile yet. It loaded the target, which raised the `UncalibratedReranker` whose message tells the operator to run this harness; Decision 77 had worked around it with a shim. The target is now collected under an identity placeholder, asserted for its collection only, at the legal focus depth given by `--target-focus-k`. A profiled target keeps its profile, and the report records which profile the target was collected under.
- **Not verified**:
  - ARM. The CPU cost was measured on emulated x86 E-cores and the rankings on the GPU in fp32.
  - Live end-to-end answers with the distilled model.
  - Question styles beyond the three benchmarks.
  - Whether a gate change for confident-but-incomplete contexts (mh_04) would keep the golden refusals where they are.
  - Seeds and recipes beyond the eight pre-registered checkpoints. All eight sat within 0.9 point of one another on fresh-dev.
  - Licences: a published checkpoint needs CC BY 4.0 attribution of CUAD, MAUD and ContractNLI, its training text.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_calibration_new_target.py`.
- **Impact**: eval/decisions/run_reranker_calibration.py, tests/test_calibration_new_target.py, DECISIONS_LOG.md, README.md, DEPLOYMENT.md

## Decision 88: An optional step fails open to its documented default, a safety step fails closed, and the text parsers are fuzzed for the crashes benchmarks never reach
- **Date**: Session 18
- **Context**: round 8's integration review found a crash in the query path. `named_document.name_document` (Decision 86) raised `KeyError: 'spa'` for any question that wrote "SPA" while a retrieved document was titled "Share Purchase Agreement": "spa" is also a stopword (the legal form S.p.A.), so the acronym had no weight. `retrieval_executor_node` catches nothing, so one optional ranking heuristic would have failed the whole query, and LegalBench-RAG never phrases a question that way, so thousands of evaluation queries never reached the line (fixed in 692627e). The working assumption here: there are more like it.
- **Inventory** (every step of the two request paths; *required* = a failure fails the request, *safety* = must fail closed, *optional* = a quality heuristic with a documented default):

  | Path | Step | Class | On failure now |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | query | API: query guard (public callers) | optional (abuse filter) | Laya unavailable → allowed (unchanged, documented); other errors fail the request |
  | query | API: `include_pii` forced off for public callers | safety | no failure mode |
  | query | Query intelligence (LLM) | optional, already guarded | every model failed → defaults, trace `degraded` |
  | query | Retrieval config and clamping | required (an unreadable proposed value is dropped) | fails — a huge integer crashed it, fixed below |
  | query | Filters: `deal_id`, `is_current_version=1`, `contains_pii=0` (`hybrid_search._build_filter`, every lookup) | safety | raises → request fails (test added) |
  | query | Embedding, BM25, hybrid search, RRF, rerank | required | fails |
  | query | Filing-period preference (`prefer_named_period`) | optional | **guarded**: the lists as retrieved |
  | query | Table representation cap | optional | **guarded**: uncapped |
  | query | Document focus (leading document, in-document search) | optional | **guarded**: no focus candidates (as `document_focus_k=0`) |
  | query | Named document | optional | **guarded**: the fused choice |
  | query | Context expansion (parents, table siblings, section tables) | optional | **guarded**: the reranked chunks alone (siblings and section tables already failed open with a trace mark; a parent scroll failure failed the request) |
  | query | Financial verifier (figure registry) | optional | **guarded**: skipped, trace `reason: "failed"` |
  | query | Quality gate (heuristic; LLM assessor in the ambiguous band) | required (the LLM part falls back to the heuristic numbers, which route to a rewrite) | fails — a huge integer in the LLM's reply crashed its parser, fixed below |
  | query | Laya answerability veto | optional, already guarded | `LayaUnavailable` → no verdict, heuristic alone |
  | query | Query rewriter (LLM) | optional, already guarded | the current query is kept (its config clamp could still crash — fixed, below) |
  | query | Synthesis | required (model ladder; every model failed → `synthesis_unavailable`) | labelled apology |
  | query | Citation selection, numeric claims (`_select_cited_chunks`, `ground_answer_numbers`) | required (part of the answer) | fails — two crash bugs fixed, below |
  | query | Claim verification (numeric grounding, NLI, LLM judge) | safety: fails to a labelled `warning`, never to `passed` | unchanged |
  | query | Injection review at query time | safety | raises → request fails |
  | ingest | `sanitize_filename`, Office archive limits | required / safety | refuses (one invariant fixed, below) |
  | ingest | Classification (rules; Laya first when enabled) | required (`document_category` override available) | Laya → rules on `LayaUnavailable` (unchanged) |
  | ingest | Content sample for classification | optional, already guarded | "" |
  | ingest | Format processor, sections, structural and semantic chunking | required | `DocumentExtractionError` |
  | ingest | Filing metadata | optional | **guarded**: no `filing_*` fields |
  | ingest | Context headers (off by default) | optional | **guarded**: bare text |
  | ingest | PII detection (regex; Laya with `LAYA_PII=1`), parent PII propagation | safety | raises → upload fails (test added); **with `LAYA_PII=1` a Laya outage fell back to the regex — fixed, below** |
  | ingest | Risk signals (regex; Laya with `LAYA_RISK`) | optional (dashboard and trace; never a filter) | **guarded** per chunk: no regex signals |
  | ingest | Injection screen (labelling) | safety | raises → upload fails (test added) |
  | ingest | Document identity | optional | **guarded**: no identity fields |
  | ingest | Retired-version check on re-index | safety (current-version) | fails |
  | ingest | Embedding, BM25, upsert (rollback on a first ingest), stale-point delete | required | fails |
  | ingest | Version candidates (after the upsert) | optional | **guarded**: no proposal (the Qdrant read was already caught; the file-name comparison was not, and its failure turned an indexed upload into an error) |
  | tool | Identity backfill, per document | optional | **guarded**: the document is left as it is and counted `failed` — not given `{}`, which would delete fields it carries |

- **Policy** (`src/utils/fail_open.py`): an optional step runs through `fail_open(step, fn, ..., default=...)` (or `fail_open_async`). It catches `Exception` only — cancellation, `KeyboardInterrupt` and `SystemExit` pass through — logs one ERROR record with the step name and the traceback, counts it in `redline_optional_step_failures_total{step}` (`src/utils/metrics.py`), names it in the running graph node's trace (`perf.fallbacks`, only when one fell back), and returns the default, which is what the step does when switched off or when it finds nothing. No step whose default could widen what a caller sees is optional: every guarded default keeps the deal, version and PII filters of the request. `STRICT_OPTIONAL_STEPS=1` re-raises after logging and counting; `tests/conftest.py` sets it, so a guard cannot hide a bug from CI, and the fallbacks are tested with it off.
- **Property-based tests** (`tests/test_fuzz_properties.py`, Hypothesis 6.170.15, `hypothesis>=6.100,<7.0` in requirements-dev.txt; it changed no installed package). Text is built from the vocabulary the regexes key on (`tests/fixtures/fuzz.py`) mixed with what they did not expect: the four characters `re.IGNORECASE` reads as ASCII letters (ı, İ, ſ, K), other scripts' digits, odd whitespace, runs at the limits that matter (121, 201, 4,301 digits), stray brackets and tags, missing and empty fields. Invariants, not only "no crash": the named document is a retrieved one or none, and the result does not depend on payload order; identity never reads a PII chunk (inserting PII chunks anywhere changes nothing); title, party and file-name length caps hold; a period's year is 1900–2099 and its quarter 1–4; BM25 text is lower-case, made of its token grammar and idempotent; semantic chunks stay under the cap and lose no word; structural chunks and clause segments cover the text and keep their pages; an SSN is flagged and an "ignore all previous instructions" line labelled whatever surrounds them; screen and spans agree; figures are ordered, non-overlapping and well-typed; cited chunks are among the context; document text can neither close its `<document>` element nor forge a citation marker. Default profile: 50 examples, derandomized, no example database; `HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=fuzz` for a longer search.
- **Bugs found** (each pinned by its minimal input in `tests/test_fuzz_regressions.py` or `tests/test_fail_open.py`; every one reproduced on main ded2751):
  1. *A case-insensitive match used as a dict key* — one root cause, five sites. Python's `re.IGNORECASE` matches dotless ı (U+0131) and İ (U+0130) to "i", long ſ (U+017F) to "s" and the Kelvin sign (U+212A) to "k" (an exhaustive search of the code space finds no others); `str.lower()` turns none of them into ASCII, so the dict keyed by the pattern's own words raised `KeyError` on what the pattern had just accepted. Fixed by `src/utils/casefold.ascii_lower` at each lookup:
     - query path: "What was revenue in the f**ı**rst quarter of 2023?" failed the retrieval node (`filing_period._quarter`, `KeyError: 'fırst'`) — a dotless ı from a Turkish keyboard;
     - synthesis and verification: a context chunk "(in m**ı**ll**ı**ons)" — "IN MILLIONS" lower-cased under Turkish rules — failed `ground_answer_numbers`, i.e. the synthesizer node, after the synthesis call (`numeric_grounding.ContextIndex`, `KeyError: 'mıllıons'`);
     - ingest: a cover "For the fiscal year ended Augu**ſ**t 31, 2022" (`filing_metadata._date_from`, `KeyError: 'auguſt'`) and a 10-Q's "three and six month**ſ** ended" (`_quarter_of_10q`) failed the upload. The registry's own read of the same unit header now uses the same fold.
  2. *Pages that `int()` refuses*. Found by the `fuzz` profile: a chunk whose `page_number` is not a number made `_select_cited_chunks` raise after synthesis (the verifier's reading of the same markers, `claims.resolve_cited_chunks`, already let such a page leave the document match standing; now both do). And a runaway page number in a citation marker: "[a.pdf | p.999…]" with more than 4,300 digits made `int()` raise (Python 3.11+'s limit) in `_select_cited_chunks` — failing the request after synthesis — and in the verifier's citation resolution. `PAGE_IN_MARKER` now reads at most six digits; a longer number is no page.
  3. *Forged citation markers survived the prompt delimiting when nested*: one pass of the neutraliser rewrote the inner marker and so completed the outer — "[x [a|b] | y]" became "[x (a|b) | y]", which the citation parser reads (Decision 76's guarantee). Passes now repeat until none is left (16, then every bracket goes), with chat tokens rewritten first because "[INST]" → "(INST)" could complete a marker too.
  4. *A model's JSON integer of hundreds of digits*: JSON sets no length limit, `json.loads` returns an int, and `float()` raises `OverflowError` past ~10^308, which no handler expected — in the rewriter's config clamp (`clamp_retrieval_config`, which the retrieval executor also runs on read) and in the quality assessor's score parsing (`_unit_score`, behind a `from_llm` documented as never raising), each failing the request; the routing edge's score reader had the same gap. Such a value now reads as no value.
  5. *PII screening failed open under `LAYA_PII=1`*: a Laya outage at ingest kept the regex results, and Laya flags chunks the regex does not (P ≥ 0.25 alone), so such a chunk was indexed PII-free. The upload is now refused with `PIIScreeningUnavailable` (503) and nothing is indexed. The default (`LAYA_PII=0`, regex only) is unchanged; risk and category still fall back.
  6. *Broken invariants, no crash*: `sanitize_filename("a." + "b"*300)` returned a 301-character dotfile (the extension alone exceeded the budget; now the whole name is cut); a "THIS … AGREEMENT" title escaped the 120-character cap ("THIS " + "A"×200 + " AGREEMENT" gave 210 characters, written on every chunk); `flagged_locations` raised `TypeError` on a non-list `signals`.
  7. *Found, not fixed*: a text short enough to stay one semantic chunk has its `token_count` counted before stripping ("The \n"). Nothing reads the field, and recounting would move chunk boundaries at the cap.
- **Regex time budget** (every regex-heavy text function, 40 adversarial input families — runs of one character, whitespace, repeated keywords, clause and party fragments, markers — at the function's realistic maximum size; best of three; the time at half size shows growth; development laptop, single thread):
  - *Found quadratic, fixed*:
    - `name_tokens` on a long run of letters or digits (a hash, OCR without spaces): the compound pattern was retried from every position of the run. 87 ms at 4,000 characters, ×3.8–4.0 per doubling; through `name_document` over a legal question's 80 retrieved chunks, 1.8 s at 2,000-character chunks (~7 s extrapolated at 4,000). Now tried only where a run starts — the same matches, shown equal to the old pattern's by a property test: 0.6 ms and 48 ms.
    - `split_claims` on an answer repeating an abbreviation ("Atlas Inc. Atlas Inc. …", a degenerate model output): a regex rescanned the sentence that grew with each merge. 199 ms at 12,000 characters, ×3.85 per doubling, ~1.4 s extrapolated for an 8,192-token answer (it runs three times per request). Now a scan from the end with the same result (property-tested): 18 ms at 32,000.
  - *Linear and under budget* (worst family, at the size given): `extract_document_identity` 103 ms (4,000-character opening; round 8 measured ≤ 121 ms), `parse_text_sections` 126 ms (a 300,000-character .txt), `NumericalRegistry.from_chunks` 42 ms (10 × 4,000), `segment_document` 13 ms (300,000), `extract_numbers` 12 ms (12,000), risk signals 8 ms, `extract_filing_metadata` 4 ms (12,000-character cover), injection screen and spans 3 ms (12,000), PII 3 ms, `ContextIndex` 6 ms (10 × 4,000), `escape_document_text` 1.3 ms (12,000, after its fix above), `normalize_for_bm25` 1 ms, `question_fiscal_period` 0.2 ms (2,000-character query), version relation and classifier < 0.2 ms.
  - `tests/test_regex_budget.py` holds every function under 2 s on the worst families, and the two found cases at sizes where quadratic time would take minutes (a 200,000-character run, a 64,000-character answer), so a slower runner cannot flake them and a regression cannot pass them.
- **Startup check** (`collection_manager.identity_index_status`, beside `sparse_index_status`): reads one page of points (ids and identity fields, no text), takes up to 20 documents that carry neither `doc_title` nor `doc_parties`, retrieves their first 16 chunks by the point ids ingestion gives them (no filter on an unindexed field), and runs the extractor on them in a thread: a document it finds an identity for is one the backfill would update. A spreadsheet or minutes with no title or parties are therefore not reported. Run as a background task at startup with a 30 s ceiling; any error is logged and recorded as "not read". When a sampled document would gain an identity it logs the count with `python -m src.vector_db.identity_backfill` and reports the `identity_backfill_needed` degraded mode (`/health`, `/ready` → `document_identity`).
- **Gates** (each in its own gated job; "main" is ded2751):
  - Offline suite: 1,863 → 2,015 passed, 7 deselected (34.97 s); the new test files add about 6 s (the property tests 4.8 s). Each commit of the plan is green on its own, built on main's export: commit 1 alone 1,971 passed, with commit 2 2,007. Ruff clean on all three. `-m models`: 7 passed.
  - Golden set (`--baseline eval/baseline.json`): **PASS**, and identical to main's run of ded2751 question by question — every metric, ranking and gate decision, latency aside: production fact coverage@10 94.3 / @context 97.1, recall@10 78.7, MRR 77.2, nDCG 73.8; gate answerables 33 admitted / 1 to the LLM / 1 refused, controls refused 4 of 6 with Laya. p50 / p95 152 / 189 ms with the GPU idle.
  - Answerability dev set: identical to main's, run in the same job, question by question — 25 of 27 answerables admitted, 2 to the LLM, 0 vetoed; 12 of 28 unanswerables refused with Laya against 2 by the heuristic alone; AUC 0.801.
  - Real documents: 50 fresh-dev LegalBench-RAG questions (a seeded sample: 38 CUAD, 8 MAUD, 4 ContractNLI; dev only, Decision 80) through the real `retrieval_executor_node`, production legal config and bge-reranker-v2-m3 on the GPU, on resolve8's store of all 714 files with identity backfilled; and 20 FinanceBench dev-company questions on filings5's store, where the filing-period preference and the representation cap act. Main's export and the branch, in separate jobs: **70 of 70 identical** — ranking, reranker scores to six decimals, expanded context and gate decision — and the branch's failure counter stayed empty: no guard fired. (The runs were not timed against each other.)
  - Startup check on the real stores: on fresh6's copy of the 714 files, written before Decision 86, it sampled 181 documents, all without identity, checked 20 and found all 20 would gain one (4.8 s, local mode, after the store was loaded); on resolve8's backfilled copy it sampled the same 181, found one without identity — whose opening names nothing — and reported nothing (0.06 s).
  - With Decision 89 merged (round 9's integration): `document_identity.py`'s hunks from both merged without conflict, and the `fuzz` profile ran over both rounds' parsers together, 37 of 37 properties holding (190 s). Offline suite 2,041 passed; golden set and verification gate identical to main.
- **Not verified**:
  - Real faults. Each optional step was made to raise in tests; no Qdrant outage during a focus search or an expansion was drilled, and nothing ran against a Qdrant server.
  - The startup check on a server in strict mode (it uses no filter on an unindexed field, by design) and its cost there; it ran in memory and on two local-mode stores.
  - The case-folding characters on another Python: CI's suite passed on 3.11 (the PR branch), but the four characters come from an exhaustive search with 3.12's `re`, and another version's Unicode tables could add one.
  - The default Hypothesis profile is derandomized: CI explores the same 50 examples a property on every run. The `fuzz` profile (2,000 randomized examples a property) ran twice: the first run found the synthesizer's page crash; after the fix all 37 properties held (198 s). Longer searches were not run.
  - `LAYA_PII=1` failing closed was tested with a stubbed outage, not a real failure to load.
  - The format processors' own parsing (PDF, DOCX, PPTX, XLSX) beyond the existing fixtures; the deal and ingest routes.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_fail_open.py` (the helper in both modes; each optional step's default with the switch off and its exception with it on; nothing changes when nothing fails; safety steps fail whatever the switch; the backfill keeps a document it cannot read), `tests/test_fuzz_properties.py`, `tests/test_fuzz_regressions.py`, `tests/test_regex_budget.py`, `tests/test_identity_startup_check.py` (an index that predates the fields, a document without identity by nature, sampling, an empty index, the degraded mode, `/ready`, a failing and a slow store).
- **Impact**: src/utils/{fail_open,casefold,metrics,runtime_status,numerical_registry}.py, src/agents/{retrieval_executor,financial_verifier,retrieval_strategy,answer_synthesizer}.py, src/llm/schemas.py, src/workflow/{orchestrator,conditional_edges}.py, src/data_processing/{ingest_pipeline,errors,filing_metadata,document_identity,injection_screen}.py, src/vector_db/{filing_period,identity_backfill,collection_manager}.py, src/verification/{numeric_grounding,claims,prompt_safety}.py, api/main.py, tests/conftest.py, pytest.ini, requirements-dev.txt, README.md, DEPLOYMENT.md, tests/{test_fail_open,test_fuzz_properties,test_fuzz_regressions,test_regex_budget,test_identity_startup_check}.py, tests/fixtures/fuzz.py

## Decision 89: Contracts read from their PDFs — a box around the text is not a table, and identity reads PDF text
- **Date**: Session 18
- **Context**: every retrieval measurement so far ingested LegalBench-RAG's plain-text files, and Decision 86's document identity — the title and parties named-document focus matches — was checked on .txt only ("PDF and DOCX openings" were listed as not verified). A data room holds PDFs. CUAD v1 (The Atticus Project, CC BY 4.0; Zenodo record 4595826) ships each contract as a PDF and as the text file that LegalBench-RAG's CUAD corpus is, so the same contracts can be ingested both ways and compared.
- **Method** (written into the scratch notes before any PDF was parsed):
  - Coverage: LegalBench-RAG's `corpus/cuad` files mapped to CUAD's PDFs by file name and verified by content. The download (md5 checked against the record) was read only through `zipfile`, in isolated Python; nothing from it is committed.
  - Fidelity and identity: the production path (`extract_and_chunk`, Laya off, category auto-classified) on each PDF and on its .txt. Texts compared after `eval/span_alignment.py`'s normalisation (whitespace, no-break space, curly quotes; nothing else folded) by a word-anchored character alignment. Identity compared exactly and functionally — named_document's `identity_terms` and `identity_acronyms`, the words a question is matched on. Fixes tuned on the 232 dev contracts only (mini-dev and fresh-dev); a fix could change a .txt identity on a dev document of any sub-corpus only as a clear correction. The other 230 contracts (fresh-test 216, mini-test 14) were read once, after tuning froze — documents only, no query labels.
  - Retrieval, dev only: copies of the 714-file store in which the 232 dev contracts' .txt is replaced by their PDF through `index_document`. All 748 mini-dev and fresh-dev CUAD questions ran through the real retrieval node with named-document focus on, under bge-reranker-v2-m3 at focus 30 and MiniLM-L6 at focus 10 (ranked on the GPU, as before), against the unchanged .txt store. Scoring is LegalBench-RAG's in .txt coordinates. Each PDF chunk is placed on the .txt as the harness places a .txt chunk, after a one-to-one Unicode compatibility fold on both sides, so the ground truth is unchanged and .txt characters no PDF chunk holds count as misses. Paired; intervals resample documents. The rule: a PDF arm "loses" if a paired interval lies below zero or a point change is −1.0 or worse.
  - One candidate, G1, had its adoption rule written before any of its results. Against the fixed PDF path on pooled dev: bge@30 final-context recall's paired interval above zero, MiniLM@10's point change ≥ 0, and bge MRR@10 losing at most 1 point.
- **Coverage**: 462 of 462 map — 461 by exact name, 1 by name prefix (the PDF's name carries a second title, "…_Development Agreement_Option Agreement"). All 462 LegalBench-RAG texts are byte-identical to CUAD's own text release. Every PDF parses and has a text layer; 43 of their 8,877 pages (in 22 contracts) have none. No OCR was needed or attempted. CUAD has 48 more contracts outside LegalBench-RAG; they were not used.
- **What CUAD's text files are**: they were made from these PDFs and normalised. No-break spaces became spaces (the PDFs hold 583,879 of them in 98 of the 232 dev contracts) and curly quotes became straight. U+037E GREEK QUESTION MARK became ";": in 210 of the 232 PDFs it is the semicolon, 7,518 of the 8,517 in their raw text. En dashes became hyphens. Each page's EDGAR footer ("Source: <filer>, <form>, <date>") and page number stayed inline. So the .txt is not the PDF's text but a cleaner rendering of it, with page furniture left in; the PDF path drops that furniture as running footers.
- **Finding 1 — a box around the text was read as a table.** Exhibits printed from HTML, and files with page or paragraph borders, rule a box around their text. pdfplumber reports the box as a one-column table whose cells hold whole paragraphs, line breaks collapsed. The processor removed every row inside it from the page text and, in legal mode, appended the "table" after the clauses. 72 of the 232 dev contracts had table sections. In 35, more than half the indexed text was such cells (27 above 90%). Of 1,029 verbatim table sections, 795 were one-column grids with ≥ 90% of their characters in cells of more than 15 words; real tables (fees, price lists, signature blocks) have several columns and short cells. As shipped, those contracts had no clause boundaries and no parent chunks, and their text ran out of reading order. In some, the cell text no longer matched the contract where an answer sat: for 8 dev questions less than half the ground truth could be placed in the as-shipped PDF index. The fix recovers 3 of them and most of a fourth. The other 4, in 2 contracts, hold the answer verbatim in a PDF chunk that the harness's piecewise alignment fails to place on the .txt in every PDF arm. That is a limit of the scoring, not lost text. In one, the chunk starts with a stray page number, so it cannot be matched whole, and its first piece lands in the table of contents, which repeats every section title. There was also no title line or party clause for identity: 14 dev questions' contracts had no identity at all, and others got a title such as "15. Waiver".
- **Finding 2 — identity on PDF text.** As shipped, functional agreement with the .txt identity was 105 of 232 dev contracts, and the questions named the wrong document 12 times where the .txt index named it 0 times. Causes, by inspection of the dev disagreements:
  - the boxes above;
  - no-break spaces between words (the party patterns separate words with `[ \t]`, so such names were not read);
  - a line wrapped after the article ("…, a / Delaware limited liability company" escaped the description lookbehind and became the party "Delaware, limited");
  - long titles centred over two or three lines (the title read was the bare "AGREEMENT");
  - the clause segmenter's own numbers ("PREAMBLE", "1(a)") put back above the title as if printed headings;
  - a table-of-contents line taken as the title ("Section 2.10 No Duplication of Terms 16");
  - U+037E, which the clause-end pattern does not read as ";". Search is unaffected: BM25 and all three models' tokenisers normalise it.
  Many remaining disagreements favour the PDF. In 42 contracts the PDF has a title and the .txt none, because CUAD's text file glued the title line into the next paragraph. The .txt's parties also include "Recitals", "Source" (the EDGAR footer) or a postcode where the PDF's do not.
- **Finding 3 — clause-sized chunks.** In legal mode each numbered or lettered clause is its own section and, with its own clause_id, is never merged with a neighbour. The 232 dev contracts give 22,369 child chunks from their PDFs against 8,419 from their text files (8,114 under 200 characters against 952; median about 360 characters against 2,200). Every retrieval setting was tuned on the larger chunks. Thirty focus candidates of one clause each cover a fraction of a contract, and a question that opens with the agreement's parties ("Consider the Promotion Agreement between Depomed, Inc. and King Pharmaceuticals, Inc.; …") pulls up the preamble clause, which holds every party name, ahead of the clause that answers it. Ranking pays for this; the final context, built from ~2,048-token parents, mostly does not (below).
- **Fixes** (general; none reads a file name or a CUAD-specific string):
  - A — `PDFProcessor._is_text_frame`: a ruled grid with at most one non-empty column is a box around text, not a table, and stays in the text path (dropped in `_extract_tables` beside the label-less strips of Decision 68).
  - B — identity's whitespace class includes U+00A0, U+2000–U+200A, U+202F, U+205F and U+3000.
  - C — the description lookbehind also holds when a lower-case article ends the line; `_ORG` uses the same lookbehinds.
  - D — `extract_title`: a title line that is only a kind of document ("AGREEMENT") or opens lower-case ("to the Global Maintenance Agreement") takes up to two title-like lines above it; a version stamp, an exhibit number or a sentence above is not joined.
  - E — `document_opening` does not put back a section heading equal to the chunk's clause_id.
  - F — `extract_title`: "Section / Article / Clause / Paragraph <number> …" is a section reference, not a title.
  - H — `PDFProcessor._page_rows` NFC-normalises each line. This is canonical equivalence only (U+037E becomes ";"); no-break spaces, quotes and dashes stay as written.
- **Measured, dev — text and identity** (232 contracts; main → shipped):
  - Text recovered in order: 95.2% → 98.1% (median 98.9 → 99.6; worst 1.0% → 77.0%; contracts below 95%: 31 → 22). Noise: 4.1% → 1.0%. Order-independent share of the .txt that some chunk holds: 96.4% → 97.9%, against 99.5% for the .txt path's own chunks.
  - What remains is mostly page furniture the .txt keeps (EDGAR footers 0.6% of its characters, repeated stamps such as a court docket line 0.3%), real tables indexed as generated representations, and reading order: the processor sorts rows visually, where CUAD's text file follows the PDF's content stream.
  - Contracts with more than half their text as table cells: 35 → 0.
  - Identity, functional agreement: 105 → 130 (A alone 110; A–C 127; A–F 129; with H 130). The PDF has a title in 229 contracts against 188 for the .txt, and parties in 227 against 227. Dev questions whose capitalised words hit their own contract's identity: 731 → 745 of 748, the .txt's 745.
- **Measured once — the 230 other contracts** (documents only, after tuning froze): fresh-test (216): text recovered 95.5% → 98.3%, noise 4.1% → 0.9%, contracts mostly in table cells 33 → 0, identity agreement 99 → 128 (PDF title 196 → 211 against the .txt's 185; parties 198 → 215 against 214). Mini-test (14): agreement 9 → 11. The dev gains carry over.
- **Measured, dev — retrieval** (748 questions, 232 contracts; final-context character recall; paired changes, documents resampled):

  | Arm | bge@30 | change vs .txt | MiniLM@10 | change vs .txt |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | .txt (baseline) | 87.6 | | 78.7 | |
  | PDF, as shipped | 85.3 | −2.4 [−5.0, +0.2] (56 up, 101 down) | 79.5 | +0.8 [−2.3, +3.8] |
  | PDF, fixes A–F | 87.3 | −0.3 [−2.9, +2.1] (63 up, 84 down) | 81.1 | +2.4 [−0.7, +5.5] |
  | PDF, fixes + G1 | 88.2 | +0.5 [−2.3, +3.2] | 81.3 | +2.5 [−0.8, +5.6] |

  - As shipped, PDF ingestion loses by the rule (point change −2.4). The fixes recover +2.1 [+1.0, +3.3] with bge (32 questions up, 7 down) and +1.7 [+0.5, +3.0] with MiniLM, which brings the PDF path level with the text files on the final context.
  - On the 687 questions whose ground truth every arm's chunks hold entirely — where neither the .txt's order nor its furniture can cost a PDF arm in scoring — the fixed path is +0.7 [−1.7, +3.1] (bge) and +3.9 [+0.9, +7.1] (MiniLM) against the .txt.
  - Ranking still pays for clause-sized chunks. Fixed PDF against .txt: bge recall@10 69.4 against 79.2 (−9.7 [−12.9, −6.3]), doc_hit@1 −1.6 [−3.6, +0.1]; MiniLM MRR@10 17.7 against 24.1 (−6.4 [−8.7, −4.0]). bge MRR@10 is level (46.5 against 46.0).
  - Focus (bge run), .txt / as shipped / fixed: the focused document was the right one for 732 / 702 / 722 questions; a wrong document was named 0 / 12 / 0 times; the asked-about contract had no identity for 3 / 14 / 0 questions. The fixed path names the right document more often than the .txt path (646 against 636).
  - G1, packing a legal PDF's clauses in order into sections of up to the semantic chunker's 512-token target, split only at clause boundaries, against fixes A–F: final-context recall +0.8 [−1.3, +2.8] (bge) and +0.2 [−2.0, +2.3] (MiniLM). It closes the ranking gap: bge recall@10 +10.2 [+7.4, +13.1], doc_hit@1 +1.7 [+0.5, +3.2], MiniLM MRR@10 +6.7 [+4.7, +8.7], recall@10 +19.0. Its rule's first condition fails (the interval includes zero), so **G1 is not adopted**. Its longer chunks are placed by the scoring alignment a little less reliably, so it is slightly under-credited, but on the questions whose truth every arm holds entirely the result is the same (+0.8 [−1.4, +2.8]). It is recorded as the next step for anything that reads ranked chunks rather than the expanded context; adopting it would also retire the clause-per-chunk design that `tests/test_ingestion_robustness.py::TestLegalPdf` encodes.
- **.txt path**: on all 712 distinct LegalBench-RAG documents, main's extractor and the shipped one were run on the same .txt payloads. Six identities change, two of them functionally, and each change is a correction. Four strings lose a no-break or narrow no-break space, one of them also the junk party fragment "GAS, CORPORATION". Two titles that were section or table-of-contents lines go: one becomes the agreement's title ("ARTICLE VII GUARANTEE" → "STRATEGIC ALLIANCE AGREEMENT"), and one, "ARTICLE V ADDITIONAL AGREEMENTS 56", is dropped. The dev-tuning rule saw three of these changes (the other three are in held-out documents, read after tuning froze).
- **Gates**:
  - Golden set `--baseline`: PASS, identical to main. 9,407 result fields were compared with the shipped Decision 86 run and none differ (latency aside); production fact coverage@10 is 94.3, @context 97.1, recall@10 78.7, MRR 77.2, nDCG 73.8, and no focus document changes. The golden data room is .txt, and none of its 9 documents' identity changes.
  - Answerability dev set: identical, question by question, to the shipped Decision 86 run (58 of 58).
  - Offline suite: 1,856 → 1,872 passed. Ruff clean.
  - At integration, with Decision 88 merged: offline suite 2,041 passed, ruff clean; golden set and answerability dev set identical to main.
- **Not verified**:
  - Scanned PDFs (no OCR); DOCX contracts; PDFs not printed from EDGAR HTML, such as word-processor exports or signed scans with a text layer.
  - A data room of PDFs only. Here each PDF-ingested contract competed with 482 .txt-ingested documents, whose larger chunks the reranker may favour.
  - The retrieval runs used fixes A–F, built before H. H changes no ranking input, since every tokeniser normalises U+037E; its identity effect was measured separately (4 dev contracts change, 3 functionally; agreement 129 → 130; own-question coverage unchanged).
  - Side-by-side text: row merging, built for tables, interleaves two text columns (one fresh-test contract recovers 57% in order, 93% in any order). A box ruled into two or more non-empty columns is still read as a table.
  - FinanceBench's test companies (their questions were not run). And the tables the borderless detector rebuilds from a box's rows are not always whole: of the three statements checked, Adobe FY2022's cash flows are headed "Column 2, Column 3, Column 4", their years left in the body text above, the liabilities half of Verizon FY2022's balance sheet is split from the assets and headed the same way, and Adobe's net income and per-share lines stay in the body text below its income statement.
  - The CPU host itself (MiniLM was ranked on the GPU) and questions that do not name their agreement.
- **Recommendation, not a decision**: no parser swap. PyMuPDF's text was faithful — no ligatures, little hyphenation, a text layer on every page but 43 — and the defects were in Redline's own table and identity handling. Clause packing (G1) is the measured next step if ranked chunks matter; it needs the clause-per-chunk tests revisited.
- **Integration review**: fix E reads the chunk's `clause_id`, which `identity_backfill` did not fetch. Re-run on a legal PDF, as DEPLOYMENT advises after re-ingesting, the backfill would have put the clause number back above the title and overwritten what ingestion wrote: "PREAMBLE MASTER SERVICES AGREEMENT" for a two-line "MASTER SERVICES AGREEMENT". The fields `document_opening` reads are now one constant, `OPENING_FIELDS`, which the backfill fetches, as does Decision 88's startup check, merged in the same round. One test holds the backfill to ingestion's identity on such a document, another the constant to what `document_opening` reads, a third the startup check's reads to the constant. No measured number changes: every PDF store here was built by ingestion, and the .txt path has no clause ids.
- **Measured at integration — FinanceBench filings.** The rule, written before any result: the frame rule applies to filings unchanged unless a paired interval (filings resampled) for the evidence page or the evidence tokens in the final context lies entirely below zero (Decision 87's rule 3); otherwise it would apply in legal mode only.
  - Reach: across all 84 filings main keeps 4,520 ruled tables, and the rule leaves 870 of them, in 52 filings, to the text path — not the 26 of 560 an 8-filing sample of 10-Ks suggested. Eleven sit on dev evidence pages. Many are statements whose columns pdfplumber collapsed into one: main indexed Adobe FY2022's income and cash-flow statements and Verizon FY2022's balance sheet as one-column grids of 29, 40 and 50 rows, verbatim.
  - In the text path those rows reach the borderless-table detector, which rebuilds the columns: the same three statements become tables of 26×4, 36×4, and 19×3 plus 23×3, with the rows it cannot place kept as body text. On the 44 dev filings, tables indexed go from 3,912 to 3,482 and verbatim table chunks from 2,080 to 1,500, while the markdown, narrative and row-by-row representations each gain about 160 (markdown 2,352 → 2,513).
  - Retrieval: all 84 filings indexed once with main's code and once with the integrated code, and the 73 dev questions (Decision 68's company split) asked of each with the same retrieval code, production ablation. Paired, filings resampled: evidence page in the final context 63.0 → 65.8 (+2.7 [−2.5, +8.5]; 3 questions up, 1 down); evidence tokens in the final context 67.0 → 68.4 (+1.4 [−0.2, +3.5]; 7 up, 7 down); page hit@10 50.7 → 53.4; page hit@1 23.3 on both; MRR@10 +1.0 [−2.9, +5.3]. **The rule passes: the frame rule applies to filings unchanged.** Test-company questions were not read.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_identity_on_pdf_text.py` (no-break spaces; a wrapped article; "EXHIBIT A" above a name; titles over two and three lines; a lower-case continuation; version stamps and sentences not joined; section references; clause numbers not put back); `tests/test_pdf_contract_text.py` (a generated framed contract that pdfplumber reports as a table and the processor reads as clauses with parents and an identity; frame and non-frame grids; canonical characters normalised, compatibility characters kept).
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/{pdf_processor,document_identity}.py, src/vector_db/{identity_backfill,collection_manager}.py, tests/test_{identity_on_pdf_text,pdf_contract_text,identity_startup_check}.py, README.md, DEPLOYMENT.md

## Decision 90: The GPU checker second-guesses figure-only confirmations, reading table rows with their headers
- **Date**: Session 19
- **Context**: on GPU, every wrong benchmark claim that still passes is settled by the figure checks alone. The document's figure is correctly located, but the party, period or direction around it is wrong, so FactCG never reads it (Decision 63). Decision 64 let a model veto such figure-only confirmations. It was adopted on CPU (`-xsmall`, cue claims only) and rejected on GPU: FactCG's veto caught 4–5 of the 11 held-out false passes but sent 8.9pp / 3.0pp of correct claims to the judge, past the pre-registered bounds. The correct claims it vetoed were mostly table rows read without their column headers. Prepending the header line cut its dev cost from −4 to −1 correct claims, but only in a dev look after the test run: a lead that needed data no round had seen.
- **What the veto read**: the sentence-sized windows of `nli.split_premises`.
  - A fixed-width row more than two lines below its header was scored without it.
  - The converter's markdown grid and row-by-row representation have no sentence end, so each was one window holding the whole table.
  - The narrative representation is cut at its semicolons, which glues a row's second column to the next row's label.
- **Change**: numeric grounding already knew, for every figure, the row it sits in and the column-header line it read the period from. It now records both: `ContextFigure.row_span` / `header_line`, carried as `NumericCheck.evidence_row` / `evidence_header` / `evidence_cell` (internal, never serialised). `src/verification/table_context.py` builds the row as a reader sees it:
  - the chunk's title (a heading, not a table row or a sentence);
  - the nearest unit line above the row;
  - the header grounding used (from its first period on, when borrowed from another table of the file);
  - the whole line(s) of the row.

  Only table rows get it: a line in a chunk ingestion indexed as a table, or a line set in columns. A "Label: value" bullet in prose keeps the sentence windows that name its party, and a figure the claim's own arithmetic confirms adds no window. This is `FigureVeto(evidence="headed")`; the CPU veto keeps `"figure"`. Grounding's decisions are unchanged: on all 2,189 answers of both sets (2,175 figures), every `NumericCheck.to_dict()` is identical to main's (beae00c).
- **Method**, written into the scratch notes before any candidate was scored. Amendments A1–A8 came before any model scored anything, from reading the windows the builder produced.
  1. *Choose on dev*. Candidates: evidence {figure, headed} × scope {all figure-confirmed claims, cue claims only} × line (midpoints between consecutive dev scores), on the benchmark's dev half. Rule (Decision 64's rule B): fewest wrong claims passed, with at most 2 of 109 fewer correct claims passed locally and no more false alarms. Ties go to more correct claims passed, then cue before all, then figure before headed, then the higher line.
  2. *Confirm on new data*: FinanceBench's dev companies (Decision 68's split), from the int9 store as main's ingestion wrote it. For every converter table on a dev evidence page, up to 16 seeded (row, year) cells give:
     - supported claims: the figure as printed with its line item and period ("In fiscal 2017, goodwill was $5,821,561."; "Adobe reported goodwill of $5,821,561 in fiscal 2017."); the true direction against the prior year; the side of an "income (loss)" pair the printed sign selects;
     - wrong claims by construction: another year's figure, the next row's figure, the direction or the sign flipped, another dev company named.

     Each claim's context is every chunk the index holds for its page, shuffled per claim. The data is CC BY-NC, so it is generated under `D:/redline_data/veto10/`; the generator is `eval/figure_veto_set.py`.
  3. *Adopt on GPU* only if, with the dev-chosen line unchanged: (a) wrong claims passed fall, with a paired 95% interval (filings resampled) entirely below zero — tightened from Decision 64's point estimate; (b) false alarms rise by at most 2pp; (c) correct claims passed locally fall by at most 2pp. The old test half is reported once, as a repeat look.
- **The new set**: 85 converter tables touch the 84 dev evidence pages.
  - 66 carry generic "Column N" headers (1,088 eligible rows) and yield no claims.
  - 17 named tables in 14 filings give 1,647 claims: 694 supported (plain 271, company 271, direction 139, sign 13) and 953 wrong (company 271, neighbour 271, period 259, direction 139, sign 13). None was dropped as unfound.
  - A hand check of 60 seeded claims agreed with all 60 labels.
- **Dev pick** (`python -m eval.run_figure_veto_eval select`, dev half only): headed evidence, all figure-confirmed claims, line **0.03831**.
  - Wrong claims passed 7 → 6 of 167, correct claims passed locally 73 → 73 of 109, false alarms 3. A full replay reproduces the fast count.
  - The plain windows reach the same 6 only at the cost of one correct claim (line 0.0400, Decision 64's rule B pick reproduced), and neither form reaches 5 within the bound.
  - The one dev catch is a party swap in a board-deck bullet. The next dev score above the line is a correct claim 0.0025 higher.
- **Confirmed on FinanceBench** (`confirm`, dev-chosen configuration unchanged; production is FactCG without a veto). **The rule passes on all three counts.** This is the first form; the integration review below changed what ships.
  - Wrong claims passed: 408 → 322 of 953 (42.8% → 33.8%, **−9.0pp [−15.1, −4.4]**) — rule (a).
  - False alarms: 0 → 0 — rule (b).
  - Correct claims passed locally: 614 → 609 of 694 (**−0.7pp [−1.8, 0.0]**) — rule (c).
  - Judge load +5.5pp [+2.7, +9.2]. A full replay at the line reproduces every outcome.
  - What it catches: 82 of the 243 passing claims that name another company, 2 of 17 passing period swaps and 2 of 125 flipped directions. What it costs: 4 correct company claims and 1 plain one.
  - It works because the title line usually names the filer ("ADOBE SYSTEMS INCORPORATED", "SQUARE, INC."). The plain windows of converter tables are whole tables, on which FactCG supports almost anything: at the same line they would have caught none of the 408 (secondary, reported for comparison).
- **The line is a sharp choice, stated plainly**. Looked at after the rule had decided and not used by it: at 0.05 the same set would lose 24 correct claims (3.5pp) for 113 catches, and at 0.03, 0 for 49.
- **Benchmark test half, a repeat look** (read by Decisions 63–64; not part of the rule):
  - Wrong claims passed: 11 → 6 of 165 (−3.0pp [−5.6, −0.7]).
  - Correct claims passed locally: 65 → 60 of 101 (**−5.0pp [−9.3, −1.1]**). This is past the 2pp bound, had this been the deciding set.
  - False alarms 3 → 3; judge load 35.3% → 39.1%. The plain windows at the same line: 11 → 7 wrong, −3.0pp correct.
  - Three of the five correct claims lost combine two rows of one table ("Total assets were $560.4 million and total liabilities were $288.0 million"; EBIT plus D&A giving EBITDA; depreciation and amortization). The headed form gives each figure its own row, so no window holds the whole claim; a plain window of adjacent lines sometimes did.
  - The FinanceBench set holds only single-figure claims, so it could not see this cost.
- **Integration review: the shipped form reads a claim's rows together.**
  - *Why.* The review would not ship a veto that loses 5 of 101 correct claims of the kind real answers write. That half is contaminated, but what it shows is a gap in what the confirmation set covered, not noise.
  - *Change.* `FigureVeto.rows`:
    - `"joined"`: a claim whose looked-up figures sit in more than one table row gets one window, `table_context.joined_window`. Per chunk, in the order its figures first reach it, the window holds the chunk's title, unit line and header line(s), then the claim's rows in document order. Every line appears once, with at most four rows. It replaces the per-row windows; prose figures keep their sentence windows.
    - `"each"`: the per-row windows above.
    - `"single"`: no veto for such a claim.

    A claim within one row reads byte-identical windows in all three. Re-scored with the earlier scores as a cache, the single-figure set needed 0 new pairs, and every claim's veto scores are equal.
  - *Method*, written into the scratch notes before any new scoring:
    1. Re-run the dev pick with `joined` in place of per-row `headed`, otherwise unchanged.
    2. New data: claims joining two rows of the same 17 tables for one year, built the same way. Supported claims state both rows' figures as printed (plain, or naming the filer). Wrong claims replace one of the two figures with its other-year or neighbouring-row figure, or name another dev company. That gives 1,012 claims: 408 supported, 604 wrong; 204 row pairs from 14 filings. A hand check agreed with 40 of 40 labels.
    3. Adopt `joined` only if, on the two-row claims, correct claims passed locally fall by at most 2pp, false alarms rise by at most 2pp and wrong claims passed do not rise (point estimates; intervals resample filings), **and** Decision 90's rule still holds on the single-figure set. If the line moved, that is a second look at that set.
    4. Otherwise ship the per-row veto for one-row claims only (`"single"`).
  - *Dev pick*: `joined`, all figure-confirmed claims, line **0.03643**.
    - Dev wrong claims passed 7 → 6; correct claims passed locally 73 → 73; full replay matches.
    - The line moved from 0.03831: a correct dev claim joining two rows now scores just above the one wrong dev catch.
    - Decision 90's candidate set, re-run on the same scores, reproduces its own pick.
  - *Two-row claims*, against production FactCG — **the rule passes**:
    - wrong claims passed: 126 → 96 of 604 (**−5.0pp [−8.7, −2.2]**);
    - correct claims passed locally: 193 → 192 of 408 (**−0.25pp [−0.74, 0.0]**);
    - false alarms 0 → 0; judge load +3.1pp.
    - Read one row at a time (Decision 90's first form), the same claims would have gone 193 → 22. That is the coverage gap, measured on new data.
  - *Single-figure claims, at the moved line* (a second look at that set) — **Decision 90's rule passes again**:
    - wrong claims passed: 408 → 326 of 953 (−8.6pp [−14.7, −4.0]);
    - correct claims passed locally: 614 → 611 of 694 (−0.4pp [−0.9, 0.0]);
    - false alarms 0 → 0.
  - **Shipped:** `FigureVeto(line=0.03643, evidence="headed", cues_only=False, rows="joined")`. Full replays reproduce every outcome on both sets.
  - *Repeat look, benchmark test half*, for what ships (descriptive):
    - Wrong claims passed: 11 → 6 of 165 (−3.0pp [−5.6, −0.7]).
    - Correct claims passed locally: 65 → 63 of 101 (−2.0pp [−5.1, 0.0]). False alarms 3 → 3; judge load 35.3% → 38.0%.
    - Recovered: the two-row claims "total assets … and total liabilities …" (support 0.031 → 0.618) and "depreciation … and amortization …" (0.032 → 0.467). The parachute total now passes because the line moved.
    - Still lost: "EBIT plus $24.8 million of D&A gives EBITDA" (0.027 joined) and a prose claim no form passes.
- **Cost on GPU** (FactCG fp16):
  - Per figure-confirmed claim scored alone:
    - single-figure claims: unchanged, 1.06 pairs and 39 ms mean / 27 ms p50 / 50 ms p95;
    - two-row claims: 1.21 joined pairs against 2.07 per-row, 71 ms mean / 31 ms p50 (per-row 70 / 30);
    - benchmark: 26 ms p50 / 48 ms p95 joined.
  - Per answer, on 60 answers of nine single-figure table claims each, A/B interleaved with the real model: 2.1 → 10.6 pairs, median 10 → 47 ms (+36 ms paired). Joining changes no pair of these answers.

  Production scores the pairs in the answer's single batched call, with the same 40-claim budget.
- **Gates** (with the shipped `joined` veto):
  - CI's verification gate (`--no-nli --baseline`) is identical to its baseline.
  - The FactCG run of the benchmark, against main's FactCG run:

    | | dev | test |
    |---|---|---|
    | wrong claims passed | 6/167 (main 7) | 6/165 (main 11) |
    | correct claims passed locally | 73/109 (main 73) | 63/101 (main 65) |
    | false alarms | 3 (main 3) | 3 (main 3) |
    | judge load | 33.3% (main 33.0%) | 38.0% (main 35.3%) |
  - Offline suite 2,078 passed, 1 xfailed (the pinned defect); `-m models` 7 passed; ruff clean.
  - At integration, with Decisions 91 and 92 merged: offline suite 2,194 passed, 1 xfailed; `-m models` 7 passed; the `fuzz` profile 47 of 47; CI's verification gate identical to its baseline; the FactCG run identical to this branch's (wrong claims passed 6/167 and 6/165, false alarms 3/109 and 3/101).
  - The `fuzz` profile, run at integration, found a figure read across a line break ("452,800,000" with the next line's "K") ending past the row recorded for it. The row now runs to the end of the last line its figures reach. No context the veto was measured on holds such a figure (34 FinanceBench page contexts, 542 benchmark contexts), so no measured window changes; the case is pinned as an example of the property.
  - Two branches of this round read a FactCG run against `eval/verification_baseline.json` as main failing its gate. That file is recorded without a model (`"nli": "off"`), and CI runs the gate with `--no-nli`. The eval now refuses to compare a run with a baseline recorded with another checker (exit 2) and says which mode to run.
- **Failure class**: building a headed row is part of claim verification, a safety step (Decision 88). It raises, and the validator keeps the answer as a warning, never passed.
- **Found on the way, not changed**. Grounding's decisions were frozen this round, and the PDF processor is another territory.
  - *Grounding confirms 29 of the set's wrong period and neighbour claims*, i.e. shows a wrong figure as verified with no model involved. Three causes, by inspection of all 29:
    - *Several rows read as one sentence (14).* A row-by-row label that keeps the PDF's dot leaders ("Short-term investments ......: 2015=3,111,524 2014=2,622,091") is longer than the 60 characters `_LABEL_COLON_RE` allows, so the whole table is one prose paragraph. A neighbour's figure then sits in the same "sentence" as the claim's row label.
    - *A row-by-row line read without a unique column header gives every figure its last column's year (8).* In "Operating income: 2019=7,207 2018=7,820", `figure_periods` breaks the tie between the period before a figure ("2019=") and the one after it (" 2018") in favour of the one after. So "Operating income was $7,207 in fiscal 2018" comes back bound — a wrong figure confirmed — and the correct 2019 claim is a conflict. The markdown sibling's header fixes it when it is the file's only header of that width. Pinned as a strict xfail in `tests/test_figure_veto.py`.
    - *A single-figure row whose period nothing states (5)* binds to any year a claim names (the "undated" rule), for example when a grid's header reads "For the Years Ended December 31, 2018", which `_column_header` does not read as periods.
    - The other 2 are bound with a header and were not traced further.
  - *The converter's unit note cannot be read by grounding when the currency is unknown.* "Values are as stated in the source, in millions." matches none of `SCALE_HINT_RE`'s forms, so a correct "$1,488.4 million" fails against those tables. Of the 696 row-by-row chunks of the dev filings whose table states a scale, 631 carry a unit note grounding cannot read. The set's claims therefore print figures the way the table does, without a scale word.
  - *Generic headers*: of the 66 tables, 15 could take the header of a named table of the same width above them; the rest sit under a period row the borderless detector did not reach. Seen causes: a balance sheet's liabilities half split from its assets, and "$" cells breaking the run between header and figures ("Net income  $  5,070 $  6,046").
- **Not verified**:
  - Answers as a model writes them; these claims are single sentences built from statement tables (one or two rows each).
  - Claims joining more than four rows (the window holds the first four), or rows of different tables of one chunk, beyond the unit tests.
  - Statements whose titles do not name the company. Most catches rest on the title line.
  - Contracts and other prose-heavy documents, where the headed rows do not apply.
  - The CPU host: `-xsmall` was not measured with headed rows, and its veto is unchanged.
  - A held-out FinanceBench split: the set uses the dev companies only, and the test companies' questions and labels were not read.
- **Impact**: src/verification/{numeric_grounding,table_context,claim_checker}.py, eval/{figure_veto_set,run_figure_veto_eval}.py, tests/{test_figure_veto,test_figure_veto_eval,test_fuzz_properties}.py, README.md

## Decision 91: Read a question's names without capital letters, by how the retrieved text writes each word — rejected by its first rule, adopted on integration review against production's own standard, on questions built from held-out documents
- **Date**: Session 19
- **Context**: Decision 86 focuses a legal question on the agreement it names, by the words the question writes with a capital, matched against the title and parties ingestion reads from each agreement. LegalBench-RAG writes every question formally ("Consider the Acquisition Agreement between Parent X and Target Y; ..."); a deal team types "does the nda with northwind have a non-solicit?" or "change of control in the atlas spa", or pastes a name in capitals. By the code, a lower-case question names nothing, so focus keeps the fused leader, and an ALL CAPS question has every word read as a name and every short word as a title's short form.
- **Method** (the rule was written into the scratch notes before any rewritten question was generated):
  - *Data*, dev only: mini-dev and fresh-dev (Decisions 67 and 80), 1,362 questions about 359 documents, all 714 files indexed. The store is Decision 86's, with identities backfilled by main's extractor: 6 documents change (Decision 89's corrections), and no dev question's focus choice does.
  - *Question forms* (`eval/question_forms.py`, seeded by the question id, so a question's form depends on nothing else):
    - O, the original;
    - L, lower case;
    - U, ALL CAPS;
    - C, short: legal suffixes dropped, one party named without "between" ("the Hydron Technologies Sponsorship Agreement", "the NDA with IGC"), "Non-Disclosure Agreement" written "NDA";
    - CL, short in lower case.
  - *Runs*: the real `_candidates_for_query` recorded each question's first-search pool once per form. Matchers were applied to the recorded pools offline, with main's matcher frozen as the baseline, C0. The real retrieval node then ran with the focus document forced wherever a matcher's document differed from C0's: bge-reranker-v2-m3 at focus 30 and MiniLM-L6 at focus 10, ranked on the GPU as in Decisions 80, 85 and 86. Elsewhere a matcher's result is C0's.
  - *Measurement speed-ups*: three changes to local mode served the measurement only. Filter masks were computed with numpy and checked against local mode's own; sparse products went through a CSR matrix; the scroll order was cached. Together they reproduced local mode exactly: 40 of 40 searches, and 24 of 24 node runs, which also equal round 8's stored runs. A hybrid search took 0.26 s instead of 1.94 s.
  - *Candidates* were designed on mini-dev's pools only (focus choices, no recall) and frozen before fresh-dev's pools were read.
    - K1: a question whose capitals mark no name — lower case, a capital only where a sentence starts, or ALL CAPS — is *caseless*. Its words count when no retrieved text writes them in lower case: "state", "general" and "document" are ordinary words there, "northwind" and "bankshares" are not. A word counts as a title's short form when the retrieved text also writes it in capitals ("NDA"). Any other question is read as before.
    - K2: K1, and a mixed-case question's lower-case words counted the same way.
    - K3: K1, and a run of two or more question words that is also a run of a retrieved party's name counts whatever the text does with the words ("community bankers trust", "product development experts": names made only of ordinary words, which K1 lost on mini-dev).
  - *Rule*, pooled dev; every condition must hold:
    - on the four rewritten forms, right-document focus does not fall, and it rises on L;
    - bge@30 final-context recall's paired interval (documents resampled) is not entirely below zero on any rewritten form, and its point change on L is at least +1.0;
    - on the original questions, the focus choice is unchanged for at least 99%, and recall's interval is not entirely below zero;
    - **wrong documents named do not rise on any form**;
    - MiniLM@10's recall interval is not entirely below zero on any form;
    - the golden and answerability gates are identical to main.
- **Measured — naming** (pooled dev, 1,362 questions; named right / wrong; and the focus document right, where the fused leader is the right one for O 1,148, L 1,146, U 1,137, C 908, CL 909):

  | Form | C0 | K1 | K3 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | O | 1,050 / 4; focus 1,291 | = C0 | = C0 |
  | L | 0 / 0; focus 1,146 | 1,021 / 4; 1,279 | 1,072 / **2**; 1,293 |
  | U | 1,067 / **11**; 1,278 | 1,024 / 3; 1,266 | 1,073 / 1; 1,278 |
  | C | 958 / 20; 1,176 | = C0 | = C0 |
  | CL | 0 / 0; 909 | 842 / 6; 1,131 | 942 / **5**; 1,176 |

  - K2 equals K1 on the caseless forms. It names 6 more agreements on O (MAUD) and 8 more on C, none wrong.
  - PrivacyQA, whose policies carry no party identity, is named 0 times by every candidate on every form. C0 names 2 wrong PrivacyQA documents on U.
- **Measured — recall** (paired against C0 on the same form, documents resampled; bge@30 final-context character recall, then MiniLM@10):

  | Form | K1 | K3 | T: O's naming carried over | ORACLE |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | L | +3.35 [+2.11, +4.78] (51 up, 0 down); +2.39 | **+3.63 [+2.36, +5.05]** (56 up, 0 down); +2.69 [+1.57, +3.86] | +3.41 [+2.20, +4.73] | +5.68 |
  | U | −0.38 [−0.73, −0.11] (0 up, 7 down); −0.15 | +0.04 [−0.24, +0.42] (2 up, 2 down); +0.07 | 0.00 | +2.10 |
  | C | 0 | 0 | +2.34 [+1.41, +3.42] | +5.23 |
  | CL | +5.18 [+3.56, +7.12] (76 up, 0 down); +4.44 | **+6.37 [+4.62, +8.43]** (95 up, 0 down); +5.52 [+4.03, +7.23] | +9.12 | +12.27 |

  - Typed in lower case, a question loses the naming it had when capitalised. That costs 3.4 points of final-context recall (T), and K3 recovers all of it: +3.63, with doc_hit@1 +1.03 [+0.50, +1.64]. By half: mini-dev +1.67 [+0.23, +3.66], fresh-dev +4.50 [+2.85, +6.36].
  - Levels: C0 was run on every lower-case question.
    - Lower-casing the 1,362 questions moves bge@30 final-context recall from 81.1 [77.5, 84.1] to 78.0 [74.3, 81.4], a paired change of −3.0 [−4.7, −1.3].
    - MiniLM@10 moves from 72.6 to 69.4 (−3.2 [−4.6, −1.9]).
    - bge's doc_hit@1 rises from 89.8 to 92.5.
    - The naming accounts for the whole loss: 78.0 + 3.4 (T) is level with 81.1, so the cased embedder and reranker lose nothing measurable on lower-case questions.
  - On the original questions every candidate is identical to C0 (1,362 of 1,362 focus choices; K2 differs only in naming a document that was already the fused one).
- **Result under the rule written first: not adopted.**
  - K3 meets every condition but one. It names a wrong document for 2 lower-case and 5 short lower-case questions, where C0 names none.
  - K1 also lowers ALL CAPS recall: its interval lies below zero, and right focus falls 1,278 → 1,266.
  - `src/vector_db/named_document.py` was left unchanged at this point.
  - The rule compared each candidate with C0 on the same form, and on a caseless form C0 names nothing. Against the capitalised originals the comparison runs the other way: K3 names a wrong document 2 times on L against C0's 4 on O, 5 times on CL against 20 on C, and once on U. Its 7 wrong namings cost no question any recall with either reranker. The one MiniLM question that lost recall on L had its right agreement named. That comparison was not the rule, and is recorded, not acted on.
- **Where the wrong namings come from** (fresh-dev; read after the counts had fixed the verdict). In every one, the asked-about agreement's identity cannot match the name the question uses:
  - no identity at all: Southern Star Energy's affiliate agreement;
  - no parties: the Burger King franchise agreement, filed by International Fast Food;
