  - parties that miss the name: General Finance and "UR Merger Sub" for United Rentals; NFLA and Food For Athletes for Gridiron BioNutrients;
  - not retrieved: Nuance and Microsoft.
  
  What remains is a distinctive word of another agreement's party: "southern" for Southern Racing Promotions, "king" for King Pharmaceuticals, "rentals" for Herc Rentals, "microsoft" for an Inktomi agreement, "gridiron bionutrients" for Gridiron's supply agreement. In the capitalised question, the generic capitalised words (the title's) also matched other documents and left the choice ambiguous. The case-evidence rule drops them, and the one remaining match decides. So the failure is identity coverage that a case-free reading exposes. No wrong naming came from an ordinary word of the question read as a name; each matched a word of the name the question used ("king" of Burger King) to the same word in another company's name.
- **ALL CAPS**: C0 reads every word of an ALL CAPS question as a name. It names a wrong document 11 times on U (7 ContractNLI, 2 CUAD, 2 PrivacyQA); K3 names one, with the same right-focus count (1,278) and recall change +0.04 [−0.24, +0.42]. The rule's fallback, "a correction to the ALL CAPS behaviour only if it passes on its own", cannot be met by a change that leaves lower-case questions alone, since the L conditions apply. So it is recorded here as a measured weakness for a later decision with its own rule.
- **Integration review** (Session 19; the rule and the question set were written into the scratch notes before anything new was built or scored):
  - *The reference was wrong.* On a caseless form C0 names nothing, so "wrong namings do not rise on any form" let a caseless matcher pass only if it was perfect. The reference for a question's naming is production's standard on the same question written with capitals: C0 on O for L, C0 on C for CL, and C0 on U for U.
  - *New questions, built by construction* from fresh-test's documents. No fresh-test query was loaded or read.
    - Names come from the file names, independent of the identity extractor: a CUAD file's CamelCase company field split into words ("SouthernStarEnergyInc" → "Southern Star Energy, Inc.") with its title field, and a MAUD file's "<Target>_<Acquirer>".
    - Templates follow LegalBench-RAG's prefix style: "Consider Rae Systems, Inc.'s Co-Branding Agreement; ...", 'Consider the Merger Agreement between "Viasat, Inc." and "RigNet, Inc."; ...'.
    - Each document gets two generic questions from fixed lists. 60 more questions name no document.
    - The set has 232 named questions, from 77 CUAD and 39 MAUD documents, plus the 60 no-name questions, each in all five forms. The rule left out 166 documents whose file names give no word boundaries.
  - *Rule*, all four conditions required:
    - K3's wrong namings are at most C0's on the cased form of the same questions (L against O, CL against C, U against U), with no tolerance;
    - K3 is identical to C0 on every O and C question;
    - K3 names nothing on any no-name question in a caseless form;
    - the dev results above still stand.
    
    A fallback restricted to ALL CAPS questions was fixed in advance, for use only if K3 failed.
  - *Measured*, naming only, from the real first-search pools (named right / wrong / none):

    | Form | C0 | K3 |
    |---|---|---|
    | O | 172 / 4 / 56 | = C0 |
    | L | 0 / 0 / 232 | 169 / 1 / 62 |
    | U | 175 / 7 / 50 | 170 / 2 / 60 |
    | C | 159 / 6 / 67 | = C0 |
    | CL | 0 / 0 / 232 | 151 / 5 / 76 |

    - On the no-name questions, K3 names nothing on L, U or CL. C0 names one on O and on C (the same question, "Information about the Closing Condition ...", which matches "information" in an identity) and two on U.
  - *Verdict: K3 passes*: 1 ≤ 4, 5 ≤ 6 and 2 ≤ 7; identical to C0 on O and C; no naming on any no-name question.
    - The margin on CL is one naming.
    - Its wrong namings are the kind seen on dev: one word shared with another company's name ("hf enterprises", "midwest", "american securities").
    - Three of them are "investment group" questions, which C0 also gets wrong on C.
- **Resolution**: K3 is in `src/vector_db/named_document.py` (`caseless`, `written_case`, `party_runs`). A question that capitalises its names is read exactly as before.
  - Replayed on every recorded pool, the src code equals the evaluated K3 on all 6,810 dev (question, form) pairs and all 1,460 new ones: document, reason, matched words and scores.
  - The measurement ships too. `eval/question_forms.py` with `python -m eval.run_realdocs_eval legalbench --question-form lower|upper|short|short-lower` runs the production node on the rewritten questions. Ids and ground truth are unchanged, so `--compare` against an original-form run pairs them. The generator reproduces all 6,810 question texts measured here.
  - At integration, with Decisions 90 and 92 merged: the golden set is identical to main's (production: fact_cov@5 93.3, @10 94.3, @context 97.1; recall@5 72.4, @10 78.7; MRR@10 77.2; nDCG@10 73.8), the answerability dev set identical question by question (58 of 58), and the offline suite 2,194 passed.
- **Not verified**:
  - Questions that write some names with capitals and others without ("Does the NDA with northwind ..."): K2's case. They are read as capitalised, so "northwind" does not count. No form here writes them.
  - Real deal-team questions, rather than rewrites of the benchmark's. The new set's names come from file names, and two thirds of CUAD's file names had no word boundaries.
  - Recall on the new set. Only naming was measured there; recall was measured on dev.
  - The CPU host itself; MiniLM was ranked on the GPU.
- **Next**:
  - The wrong namings point at identity coverage — agreements with no identity, or parties that miss the name in use — more than at the reading of case. On the store measured here, 9 of 712 documents have no identity and 26 have no parties.
  - One guard to try is to never switch a caseless question away from a fused leader that has no party identity of its own. Another is to search both documents in that case (Decision 86's C3), so that a wrong name costs one focus search rather than the right agreement.
  - Either needs a rule fixed in advance and data no round has used. fresh-test's documents have now been used for naming.
- **Guarded by**:
  - `tests/test_named_document_case.py`:
    - which questions are caseless;
    - how retrieved text writes its words: URLs, e-mail addresses and dotted abbreviations skipped, compounds read as pieces and joined, accents folded;
    - party runs: in order, within one name, never from a title;
    - lower case and ALL CAPS naming the party, and naming alike;
    - ordinary words naming nothing, while the whole name still names;
    - a name made of ordinary words naming by its run;
    - short forms counting only when the text writes them in capitals ("app" is no short form);
    - questions that name nothing;
    - capitalised questions read as before.
  - `tests/test_named_document_focus.py`: the lower-case-word test restated for both kinds of question.
  - `tests/test_fuzz_properties.py`:
    - a question in one case is caseless;
    - a caseless question names alike in lower case and in capitals;
    - written-case invariants;
    - party-run invariants;
    - question forms are deterministic.
  - `tests/test_question_forms.py`.
- **Impact**: src/vector_db/named_document.py, eval/question_forms.py (new), eval/run_realdocs_eval.py (`--question-form`), tests/test_named_document_case.py (new), tests/test_named_document_focus.py, tests/test_question_forms.py (new), tests/test_fuzz_properties.py, README.md, DECISIONS_LOG.md

## Decision 92: A deal-level figure tie-out, where two figures share a period only when they cover the same time
- **Date**: Session 19
- **Context**: tying out figures — the revenue, EBITDA, debt or headcount a CIM, a QoE report or a set of statements states, checked against every other document that states it — is a standard step of diligence, and the roadmap's "deal-level figure tie-out report". The cross-source registry (`src/utils/numerical_registry.py`) already compared figures, but only inside one question's retrieved context, for the financial verifier. Run over whole filings it misfired. On FinanceBench dev filings (Decision 68's company split; the store `D:/redline_data/int9/fb_int9` read through its payloads only): American Water Works' three consecutive 10-Ks gave 125 comparisons and 11 "inconsistent"; JPMorgan's 10-K and three 10-Qs 189 and 181; Best Buy's three 10-Ks and a 10-Q 147 and 118.
- **Why** (read on the filings):
  - a line of prose naming a year was taken for a period header: "As of February 16, 2022, one state, New Jersey, continues …" headed a balance sheet as 2022, twice; "In November 2020, Cal Am refiled …" headed a rate-case table as 2020;
  - a 10-Q's "Three Months Ended | Six Months Ended" over "July 29, 2023 | July 30, 2022 | …" was read as fiscal 2023 and 2022, so quarters, half-years and the 10-K's years were one period, and so were a 10-Q's balance-sheet dates; the converter's own `period` for "Six Months Ended" (no year) was "SIXMONTHSENDED", shared by every such column of every document;
  - ingestion writes `scale_factor` 1 when a table states no unit ("the table gives no currency or scale"), and the registry took it for a stated unit: Best Buy's "Total rent expense 790" (millions, unstated) against "$790" million was a 100% conflict;
  - maturity and lease schedules ("2024  2025  2026") are restated by every filing as of its own date, and a performance graph restarts at 100 in a different year in every filing;
  - an unstated "0" agreed with anything up to half a billion: its rounding slack was rescaled with it while zero stayed zero;
  - a footnote marker in a pipe row's label ("Other (2) | 180 | 83") was read as its first value, shifting the rest by a column.
- **Resolution — the registry** (`numerical_registry.py`; the verifier reads periods the same way):
  - *A header must read like one*: at most eight words outside parentheses (a unit caption in them is part of a header), no sentence break, no "vs" or "compared". A line of prose that names a year resets the header instead of becoming one.
  - *Spans and dates* (`_header_keys`, `column_period`): the span heading a column — on the header line or the two header lines above it, dealt out in equal blocks when two spans head the columns — and its end date key the column: "3M ending 2023-07-29", "6M …", "9M …", "YTD …"; a year (twelve months, 52/53 weeks, "Year Ended") is "FY2023", or "FY ending 2023-01" when it ends in January–March (named for either year, Decision 70's problem). A date with no span is a balance date, "2023-07-29" (31 December in an annual document is that fiscal year). A bare year in a 10-Q, 8-K or earnings release is not a fiscal year (no key). "2024E" is "FY2024E"; LTM/TTM, budgets and forecasts get no key. Three or more different dates of one year across a header's balance-date columns are events — acquisitions — not periods.
  - *Converted tables*: a structured cell's period is read from its column header the same way; the converter's own `period` counts only as its verdict that a column names none. A "$" printed in its own cell gives its column to the amount after it ("| $ | 1,263 |" under "2021 | Column 3"). A cell is scale-known only when the table stated a unit.
  - *Not compared*: periods after the filing's own (its `filing_period_end`, else its latest fiscal year), rows that start at exactly 100 with three or more values (a rebased index, a common-size 100%), a whitespace row with a nil before its first value, and a row whose label names another period than its column.
  - *Rows*: a pipe row's label is its first cell, and a "%" in a cell of its own makes the value before it a percentage. Footnote markers ("(a)", "(ii)", "(1)", "†") are dropped from labels, so one line item keeps one key across filings.
  - *Zero*: a zero is zero in any unit; it is no longer rescaled with its slack.
- **Resolution — the deal-level report**:
  - `NumericalRegistry.from_documents` reads a data room document by document in `chunk_index` order: a table the chunker cut in two keeps its header (same `table_id`), and a table section with no header of its own reads the document's only header when the document prints exactly one, more than once, and is not a public filing (the sample financials' "KEY FINANCIAL METRICS" rows sit under the statements' "FY2023  FY2022").
  - `NumericalRegistry.tie_out` lists every (label, period) that two or more documents state, one value per document: conflicts first, largest gap first, then agreements. Stricter than the verifier's check: a percentage, a multiple and an amount under one label are three figures; labels that name nothing alone ("Total", "Other, net", "Average", "Basic", "Balance at December 31") are counted, not compared; a document that states one label and period twice with different values (a segment table and the statement) has no single figure and is counted, not compared.
  - `src/reports/tie_out.py` and `GET /deals/{deal_id}/tie-out`: the deal is read as a question reads it (`hybrid_search._build_filter`: deal, current version, no PII), minus the narrative and computed-summary representations (row-by-row and markdown carry every row), payload only. A facet lists documents with their chunk counts; documents are taken in name order while they fit the chunk cap (20,000, `TIE_OUT_MAX_CHUNKS`) and read whole or not at all, a page's worth of documents a request (2,048 chunks), until 60% of the time budget (20 s, `TIE_OUT_TIME_BUDGET_S`) is spent; the registry is built in a worker thread that stops at the budget's end — or, when the read ran over (a page is read whole once started), once the remaining 40% has passed since it ended, so the budget is exceeded by at most one page's read and nothing read is thrown away. When both bounds cut, the reason given is the budget. (A first version scrolled the deal in one pass; points come back in id order, not by document, so a cut left every document half read, and on a four-company store it returned nothing.) `truncated`, `truncation_reason` and `documents_not_read` say what was left out. Response bounds: 200 conflicts, 200 agreements, 10 documents a figure, labels 120 characters, values 40, names 200, sections 120. Builds run one at a time (30 s wait, then 503 with Retry-After) and are cached for five minutes under the deal's readable-chunk count, so an upload, a supersession or a purge is never answered from an older report; `purge_deal` drops the deal's cached report. Same `require_readable_deal` and `read_rate_limit` as the other deal reads.
  - Decision 88's classes: the store read and the build are the route's required steps — a failure is a 503 with a request id, never an empty report, since "no conflicts" from a report that read nothing would be false. The report is optional for a page load: both consoles render the rest of the deal view and say the report could not be loaded.
  - Consoles: a Tie-out tab in the web data room panel and a "Tie out the figures" link on the landing page, fetched when opened; a Tie-out tab in Streamlit beside Risk Signals and Versions, fetched once per deal every two minutes. Labels, figures, names and sections are document text: rendered as text (React; Streamlit tables), clipped again on arrival, escaped wherever Streamlit renders markdown.
- **Measured — the sample data room** (`data/sample_deal`, 9 documents, 111 child chunks; every finding read in both documents): 10 conflicts, each a difference the documents really print, and 12 agreements, all correct. Conflicts: between the financial statements and the QoE report, EBITDA FY2022 ($74.0 against "Reported EBITDA" $74.2), EBITDA margin FY2022 (19.1% / 19.2%), Adjusted EBITDA FY2023 and FY2022 ($97.3 / $99.0, $74.0 / $73.8) and its FY2023 margin (21.5% / 21.9%); between the balance sheet and the QoE's working capital, accounts payable ($28.4 / $21.8, $24.6 / $19.4) and accrued liabilities ($34.7 / $34.2, $29.8 / $29.7); between the balance sheet and the credit agreement summary, cash at 31 December 2023 ($89.4 / $70.8). Agreements: net income, income tax, interest expense and restructuring charges (both years), EBITDA and its margin FY2023, the adjusted margin FY2022, net debt FY2023 (financials / credit agreement). Missed, by label: total debt ("Total Debt (Current + Non-Current)" $110.0 / "Total Debt" $91.4), receivables ("Accounts Receivable, net" / "Accounts Receivable"), deferred revenue ("(current)"), prepaid expenses ("and Other"); and figures stated only in prose (the revolver's $110M drawn in Note 7). The headerless "KEY FINANCIAL METRICS" table is read only through the document's one header; the verifier, reading chunks alone, still does not read it.
- **Measured — FinanceBench** (dev companies only, 44 filings of 16 companies, 25,611 child chunks; the payloads read from the store's sqlite, read-only, into `D:/redline_data/tieout10/` — CC BY-NC, nothing in the repository; test companies never loaded). The precision rule was written before any report was read: a conflict is correct when the two filings really print different values for one line item and period, read on the PDF pages; an agreement when both values are that line item and period.
  - *Rounds*: v1 read seven companies' conflicts (AWK, Adobe, General Mills, Nike, Verizon, Best Buy, CVS): 49, of which 37 real and 12 false (maturity schedules ×8, a rebased index ×2, an unstated unit, the label "Average"). v2 fixed those and was frozen; on the seven its 37 conflicts are all real. The other nine dev companies' conflicts were read only then, as a held-out check: PepsiCo gave 11, **none real** — its acquisitions table ("Acquisition date | March 23, 2020 | April 24, 2020 | June 1, 2020") was stitched to the charges table on the next page, whose columns are fiscal years, so both 10-Ks keyed the charges by acquisition date, one year apart (by year they agree). v2's clean held-out precision is therefore 0 of 11, and 37 of 48 overall. v3 adds the event-date rule (three or more dates of one year in a header are not periods) and, from the sample room, the parenthetical and named-period rules; on FinanceBench it removes exactly those 11 conflicts and 26 of PepsiCo's agreements (the same table's correctly keyed rows) and adds nothing.
  - *Final (v3), in-sample*: 37 conflicts (AWK 9, Adobe 10, General Mills 14, Nike 2, Verizon 1, Best Buy 1) and 797 agreements. **37 of 37 conflicts are real**: General Mills' 2020 10-K "made certain changes in the classification of products and updated fiscal 2019 and fiscal 2018 net sales" (12) and its 2022 10-K reorganised its operating units (2); Adobe's 2016 10-K moved building improvements "previously misclassified as leasehold improvements" (2015: 247,535 → 98,315), its 2017 10-K split accrued media costs out of sales and marketing allowances (60,825 → 55,681 + 5,144), restated total assets for 2013–2016 and other assets by the same 9,868, the notes' carrying value and G&A; American Water Works' 2022 10-K folded the market-based businesses into "Other" (O&M 2020: 25 → 364 = 389 − 25), redefined working capital ((49) → (55)) and restated its rate-case tables; Nike's 2021 10-K recast the 2019 rate reconciliation; Verizon split out derivative collateral (2,287 → 2,266 + 21); Best Buy split out supply-chain capex (371 → 347). **40 of 40 agreements** drawn with `random.Random(92)` are the same line item and period in each filing (one by luck: Nike's "Federal" is the deferred-tax row in both because the current row's values were glued into its label).
  - *Coverage*: of 49,063 figures read, 38,128 (78%) have no period, 6,374 line-item periods are stated by one filing only, 459 sit under generic labels and 131 are stated twice by one filing. PDF statements reach the index as "Column 2" grids or lose the span above their years, and the earnings releases' "Three/Twelve Months Ended" headers are gone, so Ulta's release and 10-K tie on nothing (main's registry made 28 comparisons there, 9 "inconsistent": quarters against years).
- **Measured — the verifier (a correction, not a change of rule)**: the registry's entries changed, its comparison did not. Over each dev company's filings as one context, main's `find_inconsistencies` flagged 530 line-item periods, the branch 202 (the verifier still compares a document's own repeated labels and generic ones; the report does not). On realistic contexts — the 73 FinanceBench dev questions of int9's production run, every chunk on their top-10 pages (2,557 chunks; the run recorded pages, not chunk ids) — main handed the synthesizer 14 inconsistencies in 3 questions, the branch none: 14 removed, 0 added, all 14 false (Best Buy 10-Q balance dates read as fiscal years ×10; JPMorgan 10-Q year rows under year-less "THREEMONTHSENDEDJUNE30," keys ×4). The verification benchmark does not read the registry: on this branch and on main's tree (`git archive` of beae00c) its 542 claims get the same outcome and the same checks, claim by claim, in two runs. (The branch first read a FactCG run against `eval/verification_baseline.json` as main failing its baseline. That file is recorded without a model, and CI's `--no-nli` gate passes on both; see Decision 90's integration note.)
- **Measured — time and memory** (`perf_build.py`, `perf_tie_out.py` in the scratch notes; one gated job at a time):
  - *The comparison* (the registry and the report, on payloads in memory, three runs): the sample room 0.02–0.03 s; one company's three 10-Ks (1,817 chunks, 2.6 MB of text) 0.53–0.59 s, Python heap peak 1.1 MB; four companies' 14 filings (6,121 chunks) 1.7–1.8 s, 5.5 MB; all 44 dev filings (23,507 chunks, 26.6 MB) 6.2–6.6 s, 18.1 MB.
  - *The route's whole path* (`build_tie_out` on local-mode copies of the store — facet, scroll, build): the sample room 0.27–0.28 s, a 12.7 KB response; American Water Works' store (1,903 points) 8.5–8.7 s, whole, 94 KB; the four-company store (6,717 points) at the default bounds 18.2–18.3 s, cut by the budget after 8 of 14 filings (3,544 chunks), 153 KB, and with room to finish (300 s) 36.5 s, whole (6,121 chunks), 196 KB; the dev store (25,611 points) at the default bounds 14.3 s, cut by the budget after 5 of 44 filings (1,878 chunks), and at the default cap with room to finish (400 s) 138.7 s, the 35 filings that fit 20,000 chunks (19,825) read whole and the 9 after them named (`chunk_cap`), 251 KB. (These stores hold every company under one deal id, so their conflict counts mix companies and measure nothing.) A local-mode store filters every point it holds in Python on every request and spends 4–7 ms a chunk read, so there the default budget reads 1,900–3,500 chunks (five to eight of these filings) and names the rest; its memory is the store's (a working set of 1.8–2.1 GB for the 25,611-point copy), the report's own heap peak 4–43 MB.
- **Integration review** (round 10):
  - The report reads a deal through `hybrid_search.deal_read_filter`, a public name for what a question may read (current versions, no PII-flagged passage), instead of importing the private `_build_filter`.
  - The document facet lists at most 5,000 documents (`MAX_DEAL_DOCUMENTS`). A deal with more is reported as cut short (`chunk_cap`) instead of read in part as if whole.
  - Recording a document (`deals.register_document`) drops the deal's cached report, so an upload, or a new version exactly as long as the old, is not answered from an older report in that process. Across processes the cache key (the deal's readable chunk count) still covers every change but that one, for at most the cache's five minutes.
  - The synthesizer's flags on the sample data room are unchanged: over all 111 chunks, main's registry and this one flag the same 5 inconsistencies, and no pair of documents loses one.
  - CI found what the local suite could not. The Tie-out tab is the first Streamlit view to draw a table, which loads pyarrow; a fresh install took pyarrow 26, which refuses to import beside numpy 1.x (pinned). `requirements.txt` now pins `pyarrow<25`, the build the suite passes with. The Streamlit image installs Streamlit alone and is unaffected.
  - At integration, with Decisions 90 and 91 merged: offline suite 2,194 passed, 1 xfailed; the `fuzz` profile 47 of 47; web `tsc` clean, ESLint 0 errors, vitest 119 of 119; golden set and answerability dev set identical to main.
- **Not verified**: a Qdrant server's read time (no server could be run here; the default cap and budget assume one); precision on filings the rules were not developed on after the last rule — v3's 37 of 37 conflicts and 40 of 40 sampled agreements are in-sample (95% Wilson lower bounds 90.6% and 91.2%), the only held-out read (v2) was 0 of 11, and the test companies stay unread by rule; what the verifier's 14 fewer false inconsistencies do to answers (no hosted LLM); the two consoles in a browser (vitest, the production build and Streamlit's AppTest only); several API workers (the cache and the one-build lock are per process).
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_tie_out_registry.py` (agreement, conflict, scale and printed rounding, single-source, kinds, generic labels, a document stating a label twice, prose that names a year, quarter and half-year columns, a 10-Q's bare years, balance dates, 31 December in an annual document, estimates, January year-ends, event dates, spans that cannot be dealt out, a footnote in the label cell, a "%" cell, a "$" cell, "Column N" headers, a caption in parentheses, a label naming another period, document-order headers, a stop between documents); `tests/test_tie_out_report.py` (what a question may read and nothing else — superseded, PII, another deal and the narrative representation all present in the store; payload only; the chunk cap reads whole documents and names the rest; the time budget, the documents read before it ran out kept whole, a read that ran past the budget still compared, and the budget named when it cut a read past the cap too; documents read a page's worth a request; a failing store raises; response bounds and display labels; the cache answers an unchanged deal and rebuilds after an upload; a held build slot; the route under public, private, sandbox and admin callers, the shared read limit, a malformed id, a 503 that leaks nothing, a busy slot's Retry-After, purge forgetting the cache); `tests/test_console_tie_out.py` (Streamlit: conflicts first with document and place, a partial report named, a report that does not say it is whole, document text never markup, the app's deal view with the API down); `web/tests/tieOut.test.tsx` (normalisation bounds and never upgrading a figure or a partial report; conflicts first with document and page or section; agreements on request; markup rendered as text; the partial notice; the empty and failed states and retry; fetched only when the tab opens); `tests/test_fuzz_properties.py` (`test_period_keys`: every key is one of the documented forms and a 10-Q's reading only withdraws fiscal years; `test_tie_out`: conflicts first, one value per document, at least two documents, all of the figure's period).
- **Impact**: src/utils/numerical_registry.py, src/reports/{__init__,tie_out}.py, src/vector_db/hybrid_search.py, requirements.txt, api/routes/deals.py, api/models/response_models.py, app/components/tie_out.py, app/streamlit_app.py, web/{lib/types.ts,lib/normalize.ts,lib/api.ts,components/TieOutPanel.tsx,components/DataRoomPanel.tsx,app/page.tsx,tests/tieOut.test.tsx,tests/page.test.tsx}, tests/{test_tie_out_registry,test_tie_out_report,test_console_tie_out,test_fuzz_properties}.py, README.md

## Decision 93: Statements rebuilt from PDF rows keep their period headers, titles and pages; the tie-out tells a balance from a flow and one subheading's line from another's
- **Date**: Session 20
- **Context**: in a filing, a statement whose columns pdfplumber collapses goes to the text path (Decision 89), where the borderless detector rebuilds its columns and names them from the period row above the figures — or "Column 2", "Column 3" when none is within reach. Round 10 measured what that costs: 66 of the 85 converter tables on FinanceBench's dev evidence pages were headed "Column N", so the figure-veto set could build no claim from them (Decision 90); 78% of the figures the deal tie-out read from the dev filings had no period (Decision 92); and an earnings release's tables lost the "13 Weeks Ended" / "52 Weeks Ended" lines that set its statements apart. Decision 90 named two causes: a "$" printed in its own cell breaks a value row ("Net income $ 5,070 $ 6,046"), and a run with no period row in reach should take the one above it, or the one of the statement it continues.
- **Two rounds**. The first change (headers only) was not adopted as measured: its tie-out rule failed on two false conflicts, and retrieval passed its rule but leaned below zero. The revision added titles, per-page parts, label-column spans and four registry rules, under rules written before anything new was scored, on the same dev questions and filings — a second look, chosen knowing which questions had lost their page and why.
- **Method** (written into the scratch notes before any scoring; dev only unless stated):
  - *Development data*: the 44 dev filings of Decision 68's company split, only. Each filing's page rows, ruled tables and running-line cleanup were dumped once, and the layout pass replayed over them in seconds. Main's processor replayed that way reproduces main's ingestion exactly: 3,482 tables (int9's count), and on the evidence pages Decision 90's 85 converter tables, 66 "Column N", 19 named.
  - *M1, parsing*: converter tables with generic headers before and after; and a seeded hand check of rebuilt headers — is the period a column is given the one printed over it on the page? Rule: zero wrong; a wrong one means the mechanism is fixed or removed and a fresh sample drawn. The revision (M1′) adds captions: zero captions naming the wrong statement, 40 tables a sample.
  - *M2, retrieval*: all 84 filings ingested with the branch's code; main's store (int9, whose result with main's code is reproduced exactly) against the branch's, on the 73 dev questions, production ablation, paired, filings resampled. Round 1's rule (Decision 87's rule 3): neither the evidence page nor the evidence tokens in the final context may have its interval entirely below zero. The revision (M2′) adds: nor page_hit@10, MRR@10 or page_hit@context.
  - *M3, tie-out*: the share of figures with a period on the dev payloads; every conflict the branch reports that main does not (or a random.Random(93) sample of 40) read on the PDF pages, Decision 92's rule (a conflict is correct when the two filings really print different values for one line item and period), and a sample of 40 new agreements. Rule: no false conflict among those read. The revision (M3′) compares main's payloads under main's registry with the new store under the new registry; a branch and a main conflict are the same when they name the same documents with values that agree, whatever their period key or label; every main conflict that disappears must be read and be a duplicate or a false one.
  - *M4, contracts*: Decision 89's 232 dev CUAD PDFs through ingestion with main's processor and the branch's: every section identical (a hash per contract), or the change is gated to filings.
  - *M5*: golden set and answerability dev set identical to main's runs (both .txt), the no-model verification gate passing; in the revision also the sample data room's inconsistency flags and tie-out, main's registry against the new one. *M6* (round 1 only): the test companies' filings, one generic-header count after the freeze, no question read.
- **What lost the header** (main's processor, the 1,247 per-page "Column N" detections on the dev filings, by what lies between the table and the nearest period row above it):
  - a "$" row — a lone sign, or the next column's sign glued to an amount ("5,070 $") — 288 (29 of the 43 on evidence pages); often with a label wrapped past 80 characters as well;
  - no period row on the page above: 950, of which 337 printed their dates in a form the period test did not read ("December 28, 2019", "3/25/2023", two dates on one line) and 87 their years elsewhere;
  - on the evidence pages the other 23 of the 66 were ruled grids: EDGAR rules a statement's figures and prints its years above the rules, so pdfplumber's first row is a section label ("ASSETS") with empty cells beside it.
- **Changes, `src/data_processing/pdf_processor.py`** (layout mode only; legal mode is unchanged):
  1. *Words*: lines are read from PyMuPDF's "rawdict" — the same text and boxes as "dict" on every page of four filings (649 pages), 2.6 ms a page against 1.7 — so a short line keeps its words' edges and two glued cells can be cut apart.
  2. *Value rows*: a currency sign printed apart from its amount is joined to it ("$ 5,070", "$ —"), and a cell that is not one value is read word by word ("5,070 $", "1,603,451  1,499,218").
  3. *Labels*: a one-line label is part of the statement when it ends left of the figures, whatever its length; a label whose figures sit a line lower is one row with them; a row the columns cannot hold (a footnote marker read as a figure) stays in the table as text when the next row continues under the same columns.
  4. *Headers*: dates are period cells, and a line holding several periods is cut at its words. Up to three lines over the years are read: a span over every column, one piece per column ("December 2," / "January 28,"), or one per block of columns ("Three months ended June 30, | Six months ended June 30,"; "Pension | Retiree Medical"); one line set in the label column is passed over and kept as caption text. A single line that names no period is read only when it is short and spans every column, and no piece may be a sentence. A unit line between the years and the figures is the table's caption.
  5. *A repeated name is no header*: when two columns would get the same name, a span line was not read; the period row is not used and the columns stay unnamed.
  6. *Inherited headers*: a run with no period row in reach takes the nearest one above it on the page, reached over nothing but rows of figures in as many columns and lines that start left of them; at the top of a page, the previous page's statement when it ran to the foot of its page and its columns sit where the run's do (and no ruled table comes between).
  7. *Ruled grids that name no column*: a pdfplumber table whose header row is empty beside its first cell is left to the text path when, on every page it covers, at least half its rows fall in borderless tables under a period header.
  8. *Titles* (revision): the lines printed right above a table's header — up to four, each a single cell of at most twelve words, none a sentence or ending in "." or ":", none set beside the table (the other column of the page), past the header lines a ruled grid prints over its rules — are its caption, ahead of the unit line: every chunk of the table names its statement ("ADOBE SYSTEMS INCORPORATED CONSOLIDATED STATEMENTS OF CASH FLOWS (In thousands)"). A run whose header was not read takes no title (the lines over its first figures are its own section labels); a grid stitched over pages keeps main's caption; the lines over one ruled grid are no longer the next one's caption.
  9. *Each page's part is its own table* (revision). Main joined a borderless table to the last one of the previous page whenever their headers matched, whatever lay between, and cited every row at the first page: Best Buy's 2017 statements of earnings, comprehensive income and cash flows were one table "on page 56". Now each part is cited at its own page; a part that prints the statement's header again and no title of its own takes the statement's title. (MultiPageTableStitcher's ruled grids are stitched as before.)
  10. *A span in the label column* (revision): a period row whose label cell prints the columns' span or day ("Fiscal Years Ended | January 28, 2017 | …", "Year Ended December 31, | 2021 | …", "At December 31, | 2021 | …") names every column with it; a unit note in the cell stays there.
- **Changes, `src/utils/numerical_registry.py`** (revision; public API unchanged — `NumericalEntry` gains a defaulted `basis`, and `column_basis` is new):
  - *A balance is not a flow*: a figure read under a balance date ("December 31, 2020", "At May 31, 2023") is never compared with one read under a year's or a quarter's span, though a 31 December balance in an annual document and the year's flow are both keyed "FY2020"; a figure whose header says neither is compared with both, as before, unless both kinds meet in one figure, where it is left out.
  - *A subheading's line*: a line item a table prints more than once, under different subheadings, is read as "Subheading: line item"; when its subheading is not within reach it is not read.
  - *One table, one reading*: where a table's structured rows carry its periods, its markdown and narrative forms for those rows are not read again; rows under "Column N" are still read from their text, where a ruled grid's years printed above its rules survive as its caption line.
  - *Numeric dates*: "12 Weeks Ended 3/25/2023" is "3M ending 2023-03-25".
- **Changes, `src/reports/tie_out.py`** (revision): the route asks the store for `filing_period_end` and `filing_fiscal_years`, which the registry reads to drop schedules after a filing's period (Decision 92) but never received there, and for `table_row_range`.
- **Hand checks (M1, M1′)**:
  - *Sample 1* (random.Random(93); 60 tables generic on main and named on the branch, 20 renamed, plus the one inherited table on an evidence page): 79 right, **2 wrong** — General Mills' quarterly table named "2020", "2020 (2)" (a label-column line stopped the stacking of "First Quarter / Fiscal Year"), and Ulta's earnings release naming its 13-week and 52-week columns both "2023". One mechanism: a period row read without its span lines. **Rule failed**; fixed by changes 4 and 5 (no ingestion had been scored).
  - *Sample 2* (random.Random(94), drawn after the fix): **80 of 80 right**, 1 not applicable (two ruled tables shared a first label). A later text-only fix to change 4 changed no period in any table (2,226 tables unchanged, 12 lost or gained a caption prefix).
  - *Sample 95* (revision, 40 tables of the final code: 20 whose caption gained title text, 20 named new or renamed against main): **failed** on captions, no period wrong. A ruled table in the right column of a JPMorgan 10-Q page took a line of the left column's paragraph ("allowance for credit losses on impaired AFS securities as of") for its title — a row of two columns, one a sentence ending "2021 and 2020.", had been passed over as header lines because each piece named a month — and three runs whose header was not read took their own section labels ("Loans / Nonaccrual loans") for a title. Fixed (change 8 as stated), the store being ingested stopped and discarded.
  - *Sample 96* (drawn after the fix): **40 of 40 right** — every column's period is the one printed over it and every caption names the table's statement or note, or only its unit (95% Wilson lower bound 91.2%).
- **Measured — round 1** (headers only; changes 1–7):
  - *M1*: converter tables headed "Column N" 1,623 → 1,430 and named ones 418 → 864 over all dev pages; on the evidence pages 66 of 85 → 11 of 59.
  - *M2*: evidence page in the final context 65.8 → 60.3, −5.5 [−14.1, +1.5]; evidence tokens 68.4 → 67.7, −0.7 [−4.8, +2.7]; page_hit@10 −6.8 [−16.4, +1.5]; MRR@10 −4.0 [−8.9, −0.1]. The rule passed, every page measure leaned below zero. Of the 8 questions whose evidence page left the top 10, 5 were statements whose title, header and first figures main kept together in a text chunk (the "$" rows broke main's table there); the branch put those rows in a table whose chunks named no statement. 32 of the 84 dev evidence pages lost more than 30 text-chunk tokens.
  - *M3*: figures with a period 22.3% → 36.4%; conflicts 37 → 66. Of 29 new figures in conflict, 27 were differences the filings really print and **2 false**: American Water's "Accrued taxes" FY2020, a balance of 50 against the cash-flow change of 3 (a 31 December balance is keyed as the fiscal year), and Verizon's swaptions row against its interest-rate-swaps row (the instrument is a subheading); 4 more counted Best Buy twice (row-by-row text and markdown keyed one figure two ways). **Rule failed**; not adopted.
  - *M4* identical (232 of 232); *M5* identical; *M6* (once, after the freeze, no question read): "Column N" on the test companies' filings 77.2% → 62.6% of tables (1,150 of 1,489 → 998 of 1,593), named 339 → 595.
- **Measured — revision** (changes 1–10 and the registry rules; code frozen before the store was built; all 84 filings re-ingested into a new store):
  - *M1′, parsing*: replaying the 44 dev filings, converter tables 2,041 → 2,600 (parts no longer joined), "Column N" 1,623 → 1,619, named 418 → 981. Counted by rows, which joining does not change, rows under "Column N" fall from 15,991 to 15,447 and rows under named headers rise from 3,138 to 10,958. On the dev evidence pages, 66 of 85 tables are generic → 11 of 60, and named tables rise from 19 to 49. The new store's payloads agree: 2,600 tables, 1,613 generic and 987 named; 49 and 11 on the evidence pages. Tables spanning pages fall from 367 to 185: the 172 borderless joins are gone, and the 184 grids MultiPageTableStitcher stitches remain (with one of main's text frames). Hand check: sample 96 is right on 40 of 40 (above).
  - *M2′, retrieval*: on the same 73 dev questions, paired, filings resampled:

    | Metric | Main | Revision | Change [95% interval] | Up / down |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | page_hit@1 | 23.3 | 26.0 | +2.7 [−3.7, +9.6] | |
    | page_hit@10 | 53.4 | 60.3 | +6.8 [−1.4, +15.9] | 7 / 2 |
    | MRR@10 | 32.1 | 35.3 | +3.2 [−2.0, +8.7] | 15 / 10 |
    | page_hit@context | 65.8 | 65.8 | 0.0 [−4.0, +4.1] | 1 / 1 |
    | evidence tokens in the final context | 68.4 | 73.0 | +4.6 [+0.5, +9.0] | 28 / 13 |
    | doc_hit@1 | 90.4 | 87.7 | | |

    - Both rules pass. The evidence-token interval lies above zero.
    - None of round 1's eight lost questions is below main now.
    - The evidence page entered the top 10 for American Water 2022, Best Buy 2019, General Mills 2020 and 2022, Kraft Heinz 2019 and PepsiCo 2022 (two questions). It left the top 10 for AMD 2015 and General Mills 2019.
    - On the evidence pages, pages whose table chunks name their statement rise from 5 to 42 of the 54 that have table chunks.
    - The three round-1 losses not explained by titles were PepsiCo 2021 (its cash-flow table had been joined over pp. 63–64 under a new title), American Water 2021 and Ulta's release. In each, the join did not hide the evidence page: the harness counts every page of a chunk's range. The other two were outranked.
    - One question in main's store, Verizon 2022 p. 77, reaches its page only through a joined chunk's later page; the revision has none.
  - *M3′, tie-out*: main's payloads under main's registry are compared with the new store under the new registry.
    - Figures with a period rise from 22.3% to 37.9% (10,935 of 49,063 → 24,618 of 64,881). Conflicts rise from 37 to 63 and agreements from 797 to 2,549.
    - **23 new conflicts, all read, all real.** 20 are round 1's real ones. Best Buy's five cash-flow restatements now appear once each, keyed "FY ending 2017-01" at their own pages, 58 and 55.
    - Three had not been read before. General Mills' "Net sales: North America Retail" and "Operating profit: North America Retail" for FY2020 differ because of the 2022 10-K's segment reorganisation; the subheading tells the two apart. Block's "Customers payable" at 31 December 2016 is 388,058 in the 2016 10-K's balance-sheet data and 431,632 in the 2020 10-K's.
    - Round 1's two false conflicts and four duplicates are gone. **No main conflict disappears.**
    - A random.Random(93) sample of 40 new agreements is 40 correct.
    - Cost: 42 of main's 797 agreements are not matched one to one. Ten sit inside a figure over more documents. Others are read under one label where main read two ("Revenue $" and "Revenue ....." are one "Revenue" over three 10-Ks). Some are no longer compared because a filing now states the label twice with different values: Verizon's "Depreciation and amortization expense" appears in both its statement and its special-items table.
  - *M4*: all 232 of Decision 89's dev CUAD PDFs are identical (a hash per contract), with no error and 99 → 99 tables.
  - *M5*:
    - The golden set passes `--baseline`, and its result is main's apart from the command line and timings.
    - The answerability dev set is identical on 58 of 58 questions, field by field.
    - The `--no-nli` verification gate exits 0.
    - On the sample data room (111 chunks), the synthesizer's inconsistency flags (5 → 5), conflicts (10 → 10) and agreements (12 → 12) are identical.
  - *Gates*: the offline suite gives 2,267 passed, 7 deselected and 1 xfailed; ruff is clean. The fuzz profile passes 55 of 55, after it found a NaN spread in a conflict over a 2,000-digit "figure". Main's registry gives the same NaN; the fix is that a non-finite value is not registered.
  - *Cost*: rawdict adds 0.9 ms a page. The 84 filings took six ingestion batches of 451–684 s.
- **Resolution**: proposed for adoption. Every rule of the revision passes: M1′ (sample 96), M2′, M3′, M4 and M5. Round 1's measurements and its failed hand check stay recorded above. This was a second look at the same dev questions, and the fixes were chosen knowing which questions had lost their page and why. M2′'s gains are therefore not evidence for held-out filings. Main's store gave its own figures for those questions with main's code, and the revision was scored against them once.
- **At integration** (round 11, third part; on main with Decisions 94 and 95 merged): a read-only reviewer, given the diff and none of the conclusions, reported six findings; each was checked in the code, and four were real. They are fixed (c80bc5f), each with a test that fails without its fix: a ruled grid that opens its page took the previous page's last line as its title (the page-break guard compared each line with the next, never the last with the grid; on the 44 dev filings' rows 2 of 4,036 captions change, both wrong before — a sentence from the previous page of JPMorgan's 2021 Q1 10-Q and a mangled "Company's" in Foot Locker's 8-K — and both are now empty); "December 31, 2023  2022" gave only the first column a balance basis, and a text table continued in the next chunk lost its columns' basis; a row of nils was read as a subheading; and reading a title is now optional (Decision 88), falling back to the caption without it. On the dev payloads the tie-out's 63 conflicts and 2,549 agreements are the same set (49 fewer figures without a period), and the sample data room is identical. Not fixed: a short centred title right over the columns could prefix the column names (none of the 12,444 converter column names on the dev filings shows it), and a taller line sorted after a shorter one can end a title early (a missed title, never a wrong one). The fuzz profile also drew repeated lines that the title property placed with `list.index`; the property now checks a subsequence (919fa02). Decision 94 on this store (claims rebuilt by `eval/figure_veto_set.py` from its tables with the merged code; thresholds written before scoring; not paired with Decision 94's sets, whose tables differ): wrong claims confirmed by grounding alone 1.0% (Decision 94's sets: 0.6%), passed by the production verifier 0.9% (0.5%), correct claims failed 0.1% (0.1%), wrong company, direction and sign claims passed 4.0% (4.0%) — no threshold crossed. Reading the 23 wrong-sign claims that passed (0 of 52 on Decision 94's sets) found a Decision 94 defect, fixed with it (see Decision 94). Gates on main with this decision and that fix: offline suite 2,413 passed; `-m models` 7; the `fuzz` profile 63 of 63 (two draws); ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run is identical to main's record for record; the golden set (production: fact_cov@5 93.3, @10 94.3, @context 97.1; recall@5 72.4, @10 78.7; MRR@10 77.2; nDCG@10 73.8) and the answerability dev set (58 of 58) are identical to main's; the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors) and vitest (132 of 132) clean.
- **Not verified**:
  - Numeric grounding and the GPU figure veto on the newly named and titled tables: their periods and titles now reach both, but neither was re-measured here (ground11's territory this round); nor were answers.
  - Ruled grids MultiPageTableStitcher stitches over pages (184 on the dev filings) are still cited at their first page and captioned with their unit only: the stitcher keeps no row count per part (a request for its owner).
  - Headers that print a change column beside the periods ("2022 vs. 2021", "% Change"), "Fiscal 2023" cells, text headers ("Operating Leases | Finance Leases") and segment headers inside ruled grids stay "Column N" — 11 of the 60 converter tables on the dev evidence pages.
  - A figure whose header names neither a balance nor a flow (a bare year) is still compared with both; a balance printed under a year's span (selected financial data's "Total assets" under "For the Years Ended") is now compared only with other span-headed figures.
  - Titles on pages laid out in two columns beside a borderless table, on non-EDGAR PDFs and on scanned PDFs; the CPU host.
  - The verifier's flags on FinanceBench question contexts under the new registry (the sample data room's are identical).
- **Guarded by**:
  - `tests/test_pdf_statement_headers.py` — currency cells, labels, headers (dates, spans, blocks, captions, a span in the label column, a label cell that adds nothing), inherited headers, continued statements (each page its own table, cited at its page; a part under its header again takes the statement's title; a new statement under the same years is neither joined nor retitled), titles (a statement's title is its caption; header lines passed over; a sentence, a footnote, a line beside the table or a sentence naming a month ends a title; a run whose header was not read takes none; lines from the previous page are not a title; a ruled grid on one page takes the title over its header, a stitched one does not), period cells, a generated cash-flow page through `extract_and_chunk`.
  - `tests/test_ingestion_robustness.py` — a two-page statement is a table per page, each under the statement's header and title, at its page.
  - `tests/test_tie_out_registry.py::TestIdentity` — a balance and a flow of one name are never one figure; a balance still meets a figure whose header does not say; a line item repeated under subheadings is read with them, and not at all out of reach of its subheading; a subheading carries to the next chunk of its table; a table is read once; a grid whose rows name no period is still read from its text; column periods and bases, numeric dates included.
  - `tests/test_pdf_contract_text.py` — its PyMuPDF stand-in answers "rawdict".
  - `tests/test_fuzz_properties.py::TestStatementRows` — borderless tables, period cells, words, title lines, label-column spans, column basis, numeric dates, and a tie-out that never puts a balance and a flow in one figure nor reads a subheading as a line item.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/pdf_processor.py, src/utils/numerical_registry.py, src/reports/tie_out.py, tests/test_pdf_statement_headers.py (new), tests/test_pdf_contract_text.py, tests/test_ingestion_robustness.py, tests/test_tie_out_registry.py, tests/test_fuzz_properties.py, README.md

## Decision 94: Numeric grounding reads a converted table as a table, and checks what a figure's row says of the direction, sign and company a claim gives it — adopted on its revision's rule (A4), after the first pass's rule failed its CI-gate clause
- **Date**: Session 20
- **Context**: Decision 90 found that numeric grounding alone — before any model looks — confirmed 29 of its FinanceBench set's wrong period and neighbour claims: a wrong figure shown as verified. It also found the table converter's unit note unread when the currency is unknown ("Values are as stated in the source, in millions."): 631 of the 696 row-by-row chunks of the dev filings whose table states a scale carry it, so a correct "$1,488.4 million" is "not found" against them. Grounding's decisions were frozen that round; this is the change. It was handed back once: the first pass's rule failed its CI-gate clause, and reading rows right exposed claims with the right figure and the wrong company or direction. The revision (A4) is below.
- **Why** (the 29, read one by one on Decision 90's set — development data, not a confirmation):
  - *14: a table read as one paragraph.* Adobe's balance sheet keeps the PDF's dot leaders in its row labels ("Short-term investments ........: 2015=3,111,524 2014=2,622,091"), longer than the 60 characters a "Label:" may have. With too few "listed" lines the chunk was prose, cut into "sentences" at the leaders, each holding one row's figures and the next row's label.
  - *7: the last column's year.* In "Research and development: 2015=947 2014=1,072 2013=1,201" each figure sat as close to the period after it as to the one before its "=", and the tie went to the period after.
  - *5: undated figures.* Grids headed "| For the Years Ended December 31, 2018 | … |" or "| At December 31, 2018 | … |", which the header reader did not read; their rows mix "6,149" (separated) and "181" (bare), so they were not "like figures" a header could date, and the "undated" rule bound them to any year a claim named. One was a footnote marker ("Notional value settled (2): 2021=— 2020=1,600") read as a figure.
  - *3 others*: an alias read from a sign parenthetical ("Net (income) loss attributable to noncontrolling interests" named by "net income (loss)"), and two "Other assets" claims bound to "Other intangible assets" (two of three label words) while the "Other assets" row tied on shared words.
- **Change, first pass** (`src/verification/numeric_grounding.py`; `table_context.py` for the unit line):
  - *A period labels the figure after its "=" or ":"* ("2019=7,207", "FY2022: 387.1", "Fiscal Years 2014=$ 4,147,065", "2016=(1,570)"): that figure's period; never the period of the figure before it; never the text's "only" period for a figure ahead of it. A period naming an empty cell ("2021=—", "2021: —;") is a label too. A row that labels its own cells takes no column header: one of the same width would also date a footnote marker or a value the PDF left in the row label. The strict xfail of `tests/test_figure_veto.py` passes.
  - *Rows are rows*: a dot leader (four dots or more, spaced or not) is a column gap; the converter's row-by-row line ("Label: column=value …") is labelled whatever its label's length; and a paragraph holding one of the converter's rows (row-by-row, or narrative "Label: 2019: value; …") is read line by line whatever its other lines — a short table's title, unit caption and unit note outnumbered its rows, and read as prose one "sentence" spanned rows (F2b, added as a labelled second look, below).
  - *Grid headers read cell by cell*: a pipe header whose every column cell names exactly one year, distinct from the others', with nothing else that is a figure but a month's day, a footnote marker or "52 weeks", and no span shorter than a year ("Three months ended June 30, 2019"), is a column header. A pipe row under a pipe header with as many cells takes each figure's period from its cell, so an empty "—" cell or a footnote in the label cell no longer shifts the columns; a figure in the label cell, or sharing its cell, takes none. A bare "181" beside a separated "2,522", or an amount printed with "$" on its first figure only, is a row of like figures a gap header can date.
  - *An undated figure confirms no year*: a tied figure whose period nothing states comes back "unbound" ("the documents do not say which period this figure is for, so not that it is 2022"), for the judge, instead of "bound" to whatever year the claim names (A4 below gives it its chunk's one year). A claim that names no year is unaffected. The test that pinned the old behaviour ("alone, the undated table is all there is to go on") now expects "unbound": the claim it let through was wrong — $452.8 million is FY2023's revenue, not FY2022's.
  - *The converter's unit note is a scale* ("Values are as stated in the source, in [USD ]thousands|millions|billions", with or without "(rows a–b of n)" and "; per-share rows are not in millions"): a unit header for ContextIndex, and the unit line of the figure veto's headed window. Per-share and percentage rows stay unscaled, as under any unit header. `SCALE_HINT_RE`, which the financial verifier's registry shares, is unchanged: the registry reads a converted table's scale from its payload.
  - *Which row a claim names*: a sign parenthetical ("(income)", "(loss)", "(benefit from)") is no alias; at equal shared words a table row the claim names whole outranks one it names in part, a label's parenthetical aside ("other assets" names "Other assets", not "Other intangible assets"; "total debt" names "Total Debt (Current + Non-Current)" whole).
- **Method, first pass** (scratch notes written before any scoring; amendments recorded before the set they concern was scored):
  1. *New data by construction* (`python -m eval.figure_veto_set build --kind grounding`): the converter tables of the 44 dev filings (Decision 68's company split; main's ingestion, the int9 store's payloads) that touch none of Decision 90's 84 evidence pages, so none of the tables its sets were built from. Of 2,041 tables: 85 touch those pages, 1,551 have "Column N" columns, 65 lack two full fiscal-year columns (the converter's own `registry_period`: no quarter, budget or "52 weeks"), 3 have no eligible cell; 337 tables of 28 filings (261 pages) remain. Four seeded (row, year) cells a table. Supported: the figure as printed ("In fiscal 2019, net sales was $32,136."), and in a currency row of a table the converter states a scale for, the same with that scale word ("$32,136 million"). Wrong: another year's figure of the row, the neighbouring row's figure in the same column (each in both forms), the figure with another scale word, a per-share row's figure in the table's scale. Context: every chunk the index holds for the table's first page, shuffled per claim. No claim is dropped for what grounding does with it. 7,507 claims: 2,263 supported (as printed 1,337, scaled 926), 5,244 wrong (period 1,260 + 892 scaled, neighbour 1,256 + 897 scaled, scale 926, unit 13). A seeded hand check of 48 (six a kind) agreed with all 48 labels; the 13 "unit" claims are mostly share counts whose label says "per share", stated as dollars — wrong in their unit, though not the error the kind was built for.
  2. *Metrics*: per claim, numeric grounding alone — confirmed (every figure confirmed, as `claim_checker._FigureView` reads it), conflict, unbound or not found — with main's code (`git archive` of de5c316) and the branch's, on the same claims and contexts (`eval/run_grounding_eval.py`). Secondary: the production verifier (FactCG fp16 with Decision 90's veto, no judge). Intervals: 10,000 paired resamples of filings.
  3. *Adoption rule*, fixed before any scoring: (a) wrong claims confirmed by grounding alone fall, the 95% interval of the change entirely below zero; (b) supported claims confirmed fall by at most 1pp, on all supported claims and on those stated as printed; (c) CI's no-model gate does not regress — `run_verification_eval --no-nli --baseline eval/verification_baseline.json` exits 0, and neither its false-pass rate nor any random-figure rate rises; (d) the FactCG benchmark run against main's: wrong claims passed not higher on either split, false alarms within 2pp; (e) the production verifier passes no more wrong claims on the new set.
  4. *Development*: Decision 90's set (descriptive: it chose and confirmed Decision 90 and confirms nothing here), unit tests and the benchmark's dev half. Amendment A1 wrote the change down before anything was scored; A2, after the dev runs and before the new set was scored, added that a row labelling its cells takes no header, that a narrative empty cell is a label, and that the "named whole" rule ignores a parenthetical. A3 (F2b), after the frozen run, was a labelled second look with its own rule, passed on every clause.
- **The first pass, measured** (main → frozen change → with F2b): wrong claims confirmed by grounding alone 304 → 114 → 30 of 5,244 (5.8% → 0.6%); supported confirmed 1,575 → 2,226 of 2,263 (as printed 1,200 → 1,317 of 1,337); through the production verifier wrong claims passed 285 → 108 → 24, correct ones 1,556 → 2,208, false alarms ("not found") 501 → 3, judge load 43.6% → 58.0%. Benchmark: FactCG against main's run, wrong passed 6/167 and 6/165 (main 6 and 6), false alarms 3/109 and 3/101 (3 and 3). **CI's gate exited 1**: the test half's supported pass rate fell 45.5% → 41.6%, past its 2pp tolerance — four correct claims of the test half (and one of the dev half) whose figure no text in their chunk dates (two on a QoE revenue-quality table, a debt table's "Net Debt" row, "Reported EBITDA" named only by "EBITDA", and "SaaS ARR growth of 27.9% YoY", whose prose label line the new label scope had undated). **The first rule's verdict: (a), (b), (d), (e) passed; (c) failed on "exits 0" — not adopted as written.** Decision 90's set, read again: grounding confirmed none of its 29 wrong period and neighbour claims, but now confirmed claims with the right figure and the wrong company, direction or sign (271, 139 and 13, from 243, 125 and 11), which main had left unconfirmed only by misreading the same rows; through FactCG's veto wrong claims passed rose 326 → 357 of 953.
- **Integration review of the first pass** (round 11): not adopted as handed back, for two reasons — the rule's clause (c) failed as written, and a pre-registered clause is not overridden; and the Decision 90 re-run showed a cost no rule covered (326 → 357). A revision, A4, was asked for: claims of the kinds grounding cannot see, built and hand-checked before anything was scored; generic rules for the gate; deterministic checks of direction and company; then one scoring pass of main, the first pass (A3) and A4 under a rule written first.
- **Change, revision A4** (`numeric_grounding.py`; each check can only withhold a confirmation):
  - *An undated figure takes its chunk's one year* (`_chunk_period`): a single figure whose own text states no period (not a row of like figures whose columns are unknown) takes the one year its chunk states; when the chunk states none, the one year its document's name states ("quality_of_earnings_report_fy2023.txt": the revenue-quality section of a QoE report names no year of its own); nothing when the text or name states several years, a span shorter than a year ("Q3 2023", "six months ended …", which the period reader takes for 2023) or a budget, forecast, projection, outlook or guidance. In `bind()` a figure dated this way ranks below a conflict and above nothing: a figure the text itself dates still wins. (`doc_title` was considered and rejected: the benchmark's QoE report's title came out as "Add: Restructuring Charges (Note 1) $4.5 $0.0".)
  - *A label inside parentheses labels inside them only*: in "SaaS ARR Growth: 27.9% YoY (SaaS ARR as of Dec 31, 2023: $210.5M)" the "2023:" labels the $210.5M, and the 27.9% before the parenthesis takes the line's only period again. A converter row's label is no parenthetical: "Revenue ……. $ 4,795,511: Fiscal Years 2014=$ …" still leaves its first value undated.
  - *Direction*: a claim that a figure is the level something rose or fell to ("rose to $6,149", "increased 17.0% from $387.1 million in FY2022 to $452.8 million") is checked against its row: a candidate the claim names, for the claim's year, whose row holds the prior year's figure of the same kind, both printed positive (a shrinking loss "falls" whichever way its sign is read). A contradiction is a conflict, "the documents give 15,740.4 for 2018 and 16,865.2 for 2019: a rise". Only "to"-levels: "up to $5M" is no direction, and a change ("grew by $65.7M") keeps Decision 73's direction veto.
  - *Sign*: a row whose label pairs two sides — "Net income (loss)", "Other income (expense), net", "Increase (decrease) in …", "(Increase) decrease in …", ten pairs — is read for the side its printed sign selects ("(2,131.0)", "$ (596)", "-5.0" are negative). A claim naming one side only ("net earnings was $2,131.0" against a printed "(2,131.0)") on the other side is a conflict; a written minus names the other side ("net earnings was -$2,131.0" agrees). A claim naming both sides is not read.
  - *Company*: a claim whose figures alone confirm it is withheld — its figures left unbound, for the veto and the judge — when the chunks holding them belong to a filing whose registrant ingestion recorded (`filing_company`, written for SEC filings and earnings releases), the claim names that registrant nowhere — all the distinctive words of its name, the name run together, its first word not followed by another name ("Adobe reported" for ADOBE SYSTEMS INCORPORATED, not "General Motors" for GENERAL MILLS, INC.), or the stem of the filing's file name ("COCACOLA_2017_10K.pdf") — and the claim names a name those chunks never hold. A name is a word with a capital ("PepsiCo", "AMD", "eBay", "Coca-Cola"), less periods, months, finance's abbreviations ("EBITDA", "YoY"), the parts of a filing a claim cites ("Note", "Statement") and any word with a digit ("FY2023", "10-K"); a sentence's first word counts only when its capitals go past its first letter, it is possessive, or it is followed by another capitalised word or a verb of reporting ("Block reported"): "Revenue rose to …" names nobody. A document with no recorded registrant (a CIM, a QoE report) is never withheld on this ground.
- **Method, revision A4** (notes written before each step):
  1. *New claims of the kinds grounding cannot see* (`python -m eval.figure_veto_set build --kind grounding-cds`), on the same 337 tables in Decision 90's construction: another FinanceBench dev company named for the filing's own; the opposite verb against the row's prior-year figure ("Net sales fell to $16,865.2 in fiscal 2019" where the year before was 15,740.4; both printed positive and different); the side of an "income (loss)" pair the printed sign does not select, at the printed magnitude. Each is paired with its correct counterpart. Company and direction claims sit on the first set's seeded cells; sign claims on every signed cell of a table, decided before anything was scored because the seeded cells held 11 pairs and the tables 52. 3,832 claims: company 1,337 pairs, direction 527, sign 52; 28 filings. A seeded hand check of 48 (eight a label and kind) agreed with 47; the 48th renders a "years" row as "$0.5" (a unit slip of the builder, which also touches one sampled wrong claim without making it right); two sampled "supported" claims state a sub-table's row as the company's ("General Mills reported total net sales of $2,043.9" — one segment's), Decision 90's convention.
  2. *Development*: Decision 90's set (development only), unit tests, the no-model gate on both halves, and the benchmark's dev half with FactCG. **The gate rules were chosen knowing which benchmark claims had moved and why** — dev f015.1; test f012.1, f016.1, f028.1, f028.3 and the wrong f058.4, read one by one in the first pass. With the parenthetical scope fixed (f016.1), the example rule (an undated figure takes its chunk's only stated period, none when it states none or several) brought back none of the other four correct claims (f015.1, f012.1, f028.1, f028.3) — the QoE revenue-quality chunk states no period, the EBITDA and Net Debt chunks state several — and brought back the wrong f058.4 (its covenant chunk states only 2023); the gate still exited 1 (test 42.6%). The document's name was added as the fallback for a chunk that states none, with the sub-annual and budget exclusions; f012.1 ("Reported EBITDA" named by "EBITDA") and f015.1 (a debt table stating two years) were left to the judge, since reaching them would mean "EBITDA" naming "Adjusted EBITDA" or a year chosen between two.
  3. *Rule, fixed before the scoring pass* (adopt only if all hold): (a), (b), (d), (e) as in the first pass; (c) the gate exits 0, with false passes and random-figure rates not up; (f) on the new company, direction and sign wrong claims the production verifier passes no more than main on each kind (point estimate), and over the three together the paired 95% upper bound of the change is at most +2pp; (g) on their correct counterparts plus the first set's supported claims, production supported passed falls by at most 1pp against A3 (point estimate). Judge load reported, not a criterion; Decision 90's set stays descriptive.
  4. *One scoring pass*: main (de5c316's tree), A3 and A4, the same harness on the same claims; intervals resample filings, 10,000 draws, seed 20261120. The first pass's intervals had been computed with Decision 90's seed 20261119, which `run_figure_veto_eval.paired_intervals` uses by default — not the 20261120 its notes fixed; counts and point estimates do not depend on it, and the harness now passes 20261120 explicitly. Main's first-set runs in this pass reproduce the first pass's claim for claim.
- **Measured, revision A4** (one pass; main → the first pass (A3) → A4):
  - *The new company, direction and sign claims, through the production verifier* (FactCG fp16 with Decision 90's veto, no judge): wrong claims passed 1,461 → 1,614 → **76** of 1,916 — another company 953 → 1,060 → 75 of 1,337, the opposite direction 458 → 508 → 1 of 527, the other side of a signed row 50 → 46 → 0 of 52; main → A4 **−72.3pp [−80.8, −62.2]** — rule (f). Correct counterparts passed 1,584 → 1,717 → 1,717 of 1,916. By grounding alone: wrong confirmed 1,707 → 1,871 → 85, correct 1,661 → 1,816 → 1,816. On data no round had seen, the first pass had made these claims pass more often (1,461 → 1,614): the cost Decision 90's set showed was real.
  - *The first set* (7,507 claims): A4 gives every claim A3's outcome, by grounding alone and through the production verifier. Wrong claims confirmed by grounding alone 304 → 30 of 5,244, **−5.2pp [−7.8, −3.2]** — rule (a); correct ones +28.8pp [+18.5, +37.6], as printed 1,200 → 1,317 of 1,337, **+8.8pp [+4.7, +14.1]** — rule (b); through the production verifier wrong claims passed 285 → 24, −5.0pp [−7.5, −3.0] — rule (e); correct ones 1,556 → 2,208; false alarms ("not found") 501 → 3.
  - *Correct claims of both sets through the production verifier, A3 → A4*: 3,925 → 3,925 of 4,179 — rule (g). The new checks took no correct claim's pass.
  - *The benchmark*: CI's gate **exits 0** — false passes 5/167 and 11/165 (the baseline's; the first pass had 10/165, and the difference is the covenant minimum stated as the actual ratio, "1.25x", f058.4, figure-confirmed again as on main, where FactCG's veto still sends it to the judge), false alarms 0 and 0, every random-figure rate identical, correct claims passed 47.7% and 44.6% (baseline 48.6% and 45.5%) — rule (c). Against main, two correct claims still go to the judge: f015.1, "Net Debt was $20.6 million at FY2023 year-end" (its chunk states two years), and f012.1, "EBITDA was $92.8 million in FY2023" ("Reported EBITDA" is not named by "EBITDA"; the other $92.8s are a headerless two-column row and a sentence in a chunk stating three years). FactCG against main's run: wrong claims passed 6/167 and 6/165 (main 6 and 6), false alarms 3/109 and 3/101 (3 and 3) — rule (d); correct claims passed 66.1% and 61.4% (main 67.0% and 62.4%).
  - *Judge load* (reported, not a criterion): on the new kinds 20.5% → 13.1% → 53.2% — A4 sends the judge 1,840 wrong claims and 199 correct ones, where main sent 455 and 332; on the first set 43.6% → 58.0% → 58.0%; on the benchmark with no model 64.9% and 65.0% → 65.2% and 65.4%, with FactCG 33.3% and 38.0% → 33.7% and 38.4%.
  - *Decision 90's set* (development and descriptive: the checks were written against its construction, so this is in-sample): through the production verifier wrong claims passed 326 → 357 → 21 of 953 (company 163 → 205 → 19, direction 124 → 139 → 0, sign 11 → 13 → 2, period and neighbour 28 → 0 → 0); correct ones 611 → 684 → 684 of 694.
  - *Reproducibility*: in this pass main's and A3's runs on the first set, by grounding alone and through the production verifier, give every one of the 7,507 claims the first pass's outcome.
- **The revision's verdict: every clause of A4's rule holds (a)–(g) — adopted.** `eval/verification_baseline.json` is re-recorded with `--update-baseline`, after the rule passed: correct claims passed on the dev half 48.6% → 47.7% and on the test half 45.5% → 44.6% (f015.1 and f012.1); false passes, false alarms and random-figure rates unchanged; CI's gate against it exits 0.
- **Found, and what did not work** (none of it entered the verdict):
  - *The first rule's failure*: its clause (c) was stricter than the brief's — the gate's exit also holds the supported pass rate within 2pp — and the first pass's undated rule cost exactly the claims that clause counts. Of the five benchmark claims it sent to the judge, the document's name brought back two (the QoE revenue-quality table, f028.1 and f028.3) and the parenthetical scope one (f016.1); the example rule alone (the chunk's only period) brought back none of the supported ones and one wrong claim (f058.4, figure-confirmed again).
  - *The company check needs a registrant*: of the 75 wrong company claims A4 still passes, 68 are on Coca-Cola's 2017 10-K, whose chunks carry no `filing_company` (ingestion's registrant read found none), so the check never fires there; 5 name the filer inside the row label ("net earnings attributable to General Mills", "total NIKE Brand"); 2 name it by accident — the first-word rule matches "general" in "selling, general, and administrative" for GENERAL MILLS, INC., because it reads the claim without case. Block's 2016 10-K records its old name, "SQUARE, INC"; the file name ("BLOCK_2016_10K.pdf") is what lets "Block reported" name it.
  - *One direction claim passes*: "Greater China rose to $288 in fiscal 2021" (321 the year before). Six correct "other comprehensive income" sign claims stay conflicts, as in A3: their tables print that label on several rows (shared labels, below).
  - *The builder's unit slip*: a "years" row rendered as currency ("expected life (in years) of $0.5") — the hand check's one disagreement.
  - *The first pass's intervals* were computed with Decision 90's seed (20261119), not the 20261120 its notes fixed (above); with 20261120, rule (a)'s interval is [−7.78, −3.22] against the [−7.88, −3.20] first reported.
  - *From the first pass, still true*: of the 30 wrong first-set claims grounding alone confirms, 11 differ from the row's own figure only in sign — construction slips (the set compared printed strings, "(11)" with "11"; grounding reads magnitudes); 7 name a label two rows print ("related to prior periods") or one word apart ("Increases …" for "Decreases …"); 7 are tiny or coarse figures ("$2", "-$0.1") that a footnote marker, a "par value $0.10" or a "$2.4 billion" matches within the claim's rounding; 3 sit in "Column N" grids that borrow a same-width year header from another table of the file; 2 are scale claims ("$7,555 billion") on a JPMorgan table, which main confirmed too. A converter line with generic columns ("Revenue: Column 2=452.8 Column 3=387.1") gives grounding the column numbers as figures; nothing measured here turns on it.
- **Cost**: grounding alone on the first set, one process each, 4.5 ms a claim (main), 5.3 (A3) and 5.5 (A4); the production verifier 40.2, 38.6 and 41.4 ms a claim there, and 43.7, 35.8 and 47.8 on the new kinds (one shared GPU host; another agent's gated job ran beside the pass for about three minutes).
- **Gates** (A4's code): offline suite 2,254 passed (the 7 `models` tests deselected; nothing xfailed), 49 s; the `fuzz` profile on the grounding properties 16 of 16; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0 against the recorded baseline and against the re-recorded one; the FactCG benchmark run as above. Commit unit 1 (grounding and its tests) alone on main's tree passes the offline suite (2,242); unit 2 (the builder and `run_grounding_eval`) alone on main's code passes ruff and its tests, and its builder writes byte-identical claims there. Thresholds and model paths are unchanged, so `-m models` was not run.
- **At integration** (round 11; on main with Decision 95 merged, Decision 93 still in revision): offline suite 2,318 passed, nothing xfailed; `-m models` 7 passed; the `fuzz` profile 55 of 55; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0 against the re-recorded baseline. The FactCG benchmark run against main's (de5c316), claim by claim: two outcomes move, f015.1 (dev) and f012.1 (test), correct claims that passed and now go to the judge, as measured above; wrong claims passed 6/167 and 6/165 and false alarms 3/109 and 3/101 are unchanged. The golden set is identical to main's (production: fact_cov@5 93.3, @10 94.3, @context 97.1; recall@5 72.4, @10 78.7; MRR@10 77.2; nDCG@10 73.8), the answerability dev set identical question by question (58 of 58), and the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors) and vitest (132 of 132) clean.
- **After integration** (round 11, third part): claims rebuilt from Decision 93's store, whose statements now name their columns, passed 23 of 378 wrong-sign claims (0 of 52 on this decision's set). 18 were real misses: `_sign_pair` read a row label as one left-to-right pattern, so in "Net actuarial (loss) income" it took "actuarial (loss)", found no pair and spent the parenthesis — General Mills prints its pairs negative side first after a word ("Net (loss) gain on mark-to-market valuation of commodity positions", "Other comprehensive (loss) income, net of tax"), as does American Water ("Net (decrease) increase in cash") and the common "Other (income) expense, net". The other 5 were the claim builder flipping an account name ("Other comprehensive income (loss)"), not a figure's sign. Fixed (c3e25cf): each parenthesised word is paired with the word right before or right after it. Claim by claim: this decision's set and its company, direction and sign set, grounding alone and the production verifier, 0 outcomes change (7,507 and 3,832 claims); on Decision 93's store, 0 of 19,821, and 18 of 10,220 — all wrong-sign claims, confirmed → conflict and pass → judge. Wrong-sign claims passed 23 → 5 of 378, correct ones unchanged (359 of 378); the FactCG benchmark run and CI's no-model gate are unchanged. Guarded by `tests/test_grounding_tables.py::TestSign` (a claim on "Net actuarial (loss) income" end to end, and the pair read from nine labels).
- **Failure class**: grounding is part of claim verification, a safety step (Decision 88): if it raises, the validator keeps the answer as a warning, never passed. The new checks add no step and can only withhold a confirmation; every new reader of claim text works with string operations and linear patterns over bounded windows (100 characters before a figure for a direction), and the pathological-line tests still pass.
- **Not verified**:
