  - The production verifier's judge on the 70 claims now sent to it (no hosted LLM): they are wrong claims, so the judge can only fail or pass them; it was not run.
  - Retrieval: no `name:` key was added, so the period preference is unchanged by construction; not re-run.
  - Filings with no name on either page (3M, Microsoft among the test companies): the company check still does not apply to them.
  - Item 3's classification rests on reading each value on its page; two small PepsiCo pension values ((69), 3) were matched to their columns in one filing each, not both.
  - *Outstanding (review)*: the first-word rule is partial. A claim that writes a line item in Title Case still names the filer by its first word — "PepsiCo reported Selling, General and Administrative expenses …" (GENERAL MILLS), "North American" or "Latin American net sales" (AMERICAN EXPRESS, AMERICAN WATER WORKS), "Best net sales" (BEST BUY), "First quarter" (FIRST SOLAR) — and the measured gain exists only because the claim builder writes labels in lower case. The direction the review suggests: count the first word only where the claim uses it as a name — not inside a phrase the cited chunk itself prints as a row label or heading, and not followed by a word the chunk prints with it — measured on claims written the way models write them.
  - *Outstanding (review, pre-existing)*: the file-name rule reads a stem of three letters or more anywhere in the run-together claim, so a filing uploaded as "report_2023.pdf" (stem "report") is "named" by every "X reported …" claim and the company check never fires for it; a generic or descriptive file name should not count as a name.
  - *Evaluation blind spot (review)*: every source file in Decision 94's claim sets is named after its company ("COCACOLA_2017_10K.pdf"), so the file-name rule rescues a correct claim whatever `filing_company` says. A misread registrant cannot show up there as a false alarm. With a generic file name ("annual_report.pdf") and a misread name such as the first reader's "Paul, State of Minnesota. 3M COMPANY", 57 of the 75 correct company claims on Coca-Cola's 2017 10-K went from confirmed to unbound in the review's probe. The fix below closes the misreads found; the sets cannot measure the residue.
  - *Not fixed (review)*: two registrants on one line before one label ("ACME CORP ACME FINANCE LLC (Registrant)") are read as one name; a combined filing whose parent's block has no label and whose subsidiary's has one gives the subsidiary's name.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_filing_signature.py` (the image cover named from the signature page, keys unchanged; the cover's name keeps precedence; the 10-Q, 8-K, "PepsiCo has", "on behalf of", dated and placed sentences, an upper-case label; no label, a far label, a label without the sentence, a date, a "By: /s/", a number or "THE COMPANY" between, the label before the name; two blocks naming two registrants, one printed two ways; a block read as a table; releases, contracts and S-1s untouched; a failing read keeps the cover's fields, and raises under `STRICT_OPTIONAL_STEPS=1`; linear on 300,000 characters; ingest writes it on every chunk; the company check withholds "PepsiCo reported …" over Coca-Cola's table only once the name is there), `tests/test_grounding_tables.py::TestCompany::test_a_first_word_names_the_filer_only_written_as_a_name`, `tests/test_fuzz_properties.py::TestFilingSignature` (the signature page fills only a missing name and changes no other field or key; a signature name is a labelled name). Without the change, 12 of these fail.
- **Review** (round 12, independent; it reproduced the headline numbers):
  - *Found, fixed — the signature reader could return a wrong name.* The sentence ended at its first period, so an abbreviation in its closing clause ("in the City of St. Paul, State of Minnesota.", "Washington, D.C.", "the U.S.") or a sentence with no full stop ("…authorized AMAZON.COM, INC. (Registrant)") gave "Paul, State of Minnesota. 3M COMPANY", "C. FANNIE MAE", "S. ACME CORP" or "COM, INC". What lay between was not checked for being a name alone, so a running header or prose came through: "Table of Contents ACME CORP", "SIGNATURES ACME CORP", "Index ACME CORP", "The following is the Company's report ACME". Fix: the sentence ends at "authorized." or after a clause opened by a comma; inside it, a period after a single letter or "St."/"Mt."/"Ft." is an abbreviation; and the clause cannot cross a parenthesis. Those cases now give the right name ("3M COMPANY", "EMERSON ELECTRIC CO") or nothing. A name alone also has no running header or heading (Table of Contents, Contents, Index, Signature(s), Page), no sentence break (a full stop after a word that is not an initial, a dotted abbreviation or Co./Inc./Corp./Ltd.), and every word written as a name is, but for connectives ("of", "and", "de") and a possessive "s". "J. C. PENNEY COMPANY, INC", "COCA-COLA FEMSA, S.A.B. DE C.V", "AT&T INC", "NESTLÉ S.A", "3M COMPANY", "AMAZON.COM, INC", "eBay Inc" and "McDonald's Corporation" are still read. Measured: the 84 filings again — only COCACOLA_2017_10K changes, to the same "THE COCA-COLA COMPANY"; read directly on all 84, the signature name gives the cover's own key 19 of 19 times, 64 give none; every wrong name the review listed is gone; the worst-case timings stay at 0.04–0.12 s on 2 MB.
  - *Found, fixed — one registrant named vouched for another's figures.* `_company_withheld` returned when the claim named any filer among the chunks its figures came from, so once Coca-Cola's chunks carry a name, "Coca-Cola reported net revenue of $63,525 [PepsiCo's] and goodwill of $9,401" was confirmed (withheld before item 1). Each registrant is now checked on its own — its name, its file names, its chunks' text. It can only withhold. Measured on both claim sets (copies, production verifier and grounding alone): 0 of 7,507 and 0 of 3,832 outcomes change — correct passed 2,208 and 1,717, nothing newly passes — so adopted by the rule the integrator set. The sets cannot show its benefit or its cost: every claim's context is one page of one filing. Its cost in production is a correct claim whose figure the binding finds in another registrant's chunk too: sent to the judge, not failed. As first written it also withheld two kinds of correct claims the follow-up review found (below): one whose second figure sits in a document with no recorded registrant, and one over a renamed registrant's two names.
  - *Found, documented, not changed*: the first-word rule's Title Case gap, the "report" file-stem gap and the evaluation's blind spot to misread names (Not verified, above).
  - *Docs corrected*: "6 of 1,916" counts wrong claims of all kinds (5 company, 1 direction); wrong company claims passed 75 → 5 of 1,337. The signature sentence is not universal: 6 of FinanceBench's 78 SEC forms word it otherwise (Corning ×3 "caused his report", Microsoft ×2 "undersigned;", Pfizer 2021).
  - *Tests*: `tests/test_filing_signature.py` gains the review's wrong names, the names that must still read and the mixed-registrant claim; `TestFilingSignature`'s second property now builds pages from a sentence, a clause, a stop, a header or field, a name and its label, and asserts the reading is the printed name or nothing, and the printed name on every clean page. On e183b3e's code 15 of them fail: the 11 wrong names, St. Paul and St. Louis, the mixed-registrant claim and the second property. Gates after the review: offline suite 2,460 passed, 7 deselected, with STRICT_OPTIONAL_STEPS=1; ruff clean; the `fuzz` profile on `TestFilingSignature` and `TestClaimReadings` 6 of 6; CI's no-model gate exits 0 with the baseline's numbers, no benchmark claim changed.
- **Follow-up review** (round 12, of the review's fixes; every case the first review reported was confirmed fixed and the counts right):
  - *Found, fixed — the per-registrant check withheld correct claims that b1159a0 and e183b3e confirmed.* "Disney reported revenues of $69,570 million and segment operating income of $14,868 million", the first figure from a 10-K table recorded as WALT DISNEY CO and the second from a release with no recorded registrant whose text says "Disney" or whose file is "DISNEY_Q4_release.pdf", was unbound; so was "Block reported …" over a SQUARE, INC filing and a BLOCK, INC one. Fix (`ContextIndex` now keeps each chunk's `filing_entity_keys`): filers whose chunks share an identity key are one registrant; a chunk with no recorded registrant counts with a registrant when it shares one of its keys, or, keyless, when its text or its file name holds a word of the registrant's name — its text when the text does, its file name when the file name does. So another company's unnamed filing never speaks for this one: an unnamed chunk of "COCACOLA_2017_10K.pdf" (with its own `sec:1-2217`, or with no keys at all) does not vouch for PepsiCo's $63,525. Measured on both claim sets (copies; grounding alone and the production verifier): 0 of 7,507 and 0 of 3,832 outcomes change against step 2 and so against e183b3e — correct passed 2,208 and 1,717, nothing newly passes — adopted. Remaining false alarms, all sent to the judge, none failed: two names of one registrant with no shared key (the review's renamed probe carries no keys; ingestion's payloads do — FinanceBench's two Block 10-Ks both record "SQUARE, INC" and `sec:1-37622`); a registrant named only by a later word of its name ("Disney" for WALT DISNEY CO with no other document, withheld in every version since Decision 94). Remaining hole: a keyless document with no recorded registrant (a CIM, a QoE report) that holds a word of the filer's name and the claimed company's name lets that company's claim through for the filer's figure — narrower than e183b3e, where any document's text or file name did.
  - *Found, fixed — Title Case headers and the block's own words passed as a name*: "PART IV 3M COMPANY", "Annual Report ACME CORP", "Exhibits and Financial Statement Schedules ACME CORP", "Chief Executive Officer ACME CORP", "The Board of Directors of ACME CORP", "On Behalf Of ACME CORP", "Signed for ACME CORP", "ACME CORP and the Undersigned Officers of the Company", "Smith et Wesson Holding". The words the review named (part I–X headers, annual report, exhibit(s), schedule(s), officer(s), director(s), behalf, signed, undersigned) are no part of a name alone, and "et" is no connective. The tighter option (a name must end in a legal form) was not taken: "THE COCA-COLA COMPANY", "3M COMPANY" and "J. C. PENNEY COMPANY, INC" would pass it, but so would the prose before them.
  - *Found, fixed — real lower-case names were rejected*: "Medtronic plc", "Johnson Controls International plc", "Amazon.com, Inc.", "Overstock.com, Inc.", "e.l.f. Beauty, Inc.", "Mt. Bachelor Inc." are read again (legal forms and "com" in lower case, a dotted lower-case acronym read apart, "Mt."/"Ft." as abbreviations). Still not read, by choice: "salesforce.com, inc." and "lululemon athletica inc." — a whole word in lower case other than a connective or a legal form is what prose looks like, so they give no name (fail-safe).
  - *Not changed*: two registrants joined by "and" before one label ("ACME CORP and ACME FINANCE LLC") are still read as one name.
  - *Measured*: the 84 filings once more — only COCACOLA_2017_10K changes, to the same name; read directly, the signature gives 20 names, all right (the cover's own key 19 times, and Coca-Cola 2017's). The second signature property draws its headers from the new kinds too ("PART IV", "Annual Report", officer titles, "On Behalf Of"); over every combination of its parts (2,880) it holds, and on 6dacda1's code 8 of the follow-up's cases fail it. On 6dacda1's code 20 of the new tests fail (10 names, 6 lower-case names, the two Disney cases, the renamed registrant, the property). Gates: offline suite 2,480 passed, 7 deselected, with STRICT_OPTIONAL_STEPS=1; ruff clean; the `fuzz` profile on `TestFilingSignature` and `TestClaimReadings` 6 of 6; CI's no-model gate exits 0 with the baseline's numbers, no benchmark claim changed.
- **At integration** (round 12; integrate12 at 9c8a345, main b1159a0 with Decisions 96, 97 and 98, the constraints lock, the GPU-state record, the token-window fix and the localhost-only base compose file): offline suite 2,704 passed and 3 xfailed (all strict: Decision 96's three pinned limits); `-m models` 7 passed; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set and the answerability dev set are identical, field for field with timings aside, to the first round-12 gate run (4059f38), which was identical to round 11's main; the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors; the one warning is pre-existing, in a file this round did not touch) and vitest (133 of 133) are clean. Merged with the follow-up review's fixes (a3ea08f, 261a519); the integrator re-ran the signature, grounding and fuzz test files on the branch (218 passed) before the merge.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/filing_metadata.py, src/verification/numeric_grounding.py, tests/{test_filing_signature,test_grounding_tables,test_fuzz_properties}.py, README.md

## Decision 99: A run refused only because the quality assessor reached no model is reported as an outage (outcome `synthesis_failed`), not as a refusal for want of documents
- **Date**: Session 22
- **Context**: Round 12's Docker check ran the production image with no LLM reachable. Asked "What is the interest rate margin on the term loan?" over a data room that answers it, the API returned "I was unable to find sufficient relevant information in the data room … the relevant documents haven't been uploaded yet" with `outcome: "refused"`, and both consoles showed INSUFFICIENT CONTEXT. The trace said why: all three quality assessments `degraded: true, error: "connection"`. On the free tier this is every question once the day's quota is spent: the user is told to upload documents that are already there.
- **Cause** (verified on the Docker run's trace and in `src/agents/quality_assessor.py`): in the ambiguous band (best chunk 0.05–0.30) the assessor needs an LLM's second opinion. Without one it degrades to heuristic numbers that the router never admits (the best chunk is under the 0.30 relevance floor), which is right: the gate fails closed. The rewrite loop then ends in `insufficient_context` whatever the documents hold, and that node's fixed text, and `run_outcome`, read every arrival there as "no usable context". `refused` is documented as exactly that (`web/lib/types.ts`: "no usable context; the pipeline declined"), so the outcome contradicted its own definition; `synthesis_failed` is documented as "no model returned a usable answer; nothing verified" (also `api/models/response_models.py`), which is what happened.
- **Change** (`src/workflow/orchestrator.py`): `assessor_unavailable_kind(state)` reads the run's last `quality_assessor` trace entry — the pass the router refused on — and returns its failure class when it is degraded. When it is, `insufficient_context_node` says the language model service did not return a usable response and that this is a service problem, not a finding about the question or the documents (no "best quality score": the heuristic number is not a finding either), and writes `reason: "assessor_unavailable"` and the failure class on its trace entry. `run_outcome` maps that entry to `synthesis_failed`; the stream's stage line says "Declined - the language models could not assess the retrieved passages". Both consoles' fallbacks for a response without `outcome` (`app/components/verification.py`, `web/lib/normalize.ts`) read the same entry, as they mirror `run_outcome`.
- **What did not change**: the routing edge (the retrieval harness calls it too) and the assessor, so every admit/refuse decision is the same; `is_refusal` stays true and confidence 0; no outcome value was added, so no client needs a change — both consoles already show their "the language models did not respond" panel for `synthesis_failed`. A genuine refusal is untouched byte for byte: a heuristic-confident refusal and a Laya veto never reach the LLM and write no `degraded`; an LLM pass that refuses after an earlier degraded one is the last pass, and refuses as before. `reason` and `error` were already on the public trace's allow-list (Decision 96); `error` is redacted for public callers as every trace error is (`scrub_public`); the stage detail gains no key.
- **Gates**: offline suite 2,727 passed and 3 xfailed (Decision 96's strict limits); `-m models` 7 passed; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS against eval/baseline.json) and the answerability dev set are identical field for field to round 12's final gate run on 9c8a345, apart from timings, the output path and GPU memory in use; the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors; the pre-existing warning) and vitest (134 of 134) are clean. The new tests (`tests/test_assessor_outage.py`, 23) fail on main except the six that pin the unchanged refusal; one drives the real assessor node on a dead ladder through the router into the terminal node, and the API tests cover the blocking and the stream route, degraded and not. An independent review found no defect.
- **Not changed, noted**: the Prometheus `QUERIES` counter labels by `is_refusal`, so this case — like a synthesis failure before it — still counts as `outcome="refusal"` there while the API body and the audit log say `synthesis_failed`. Relabelling it changes dashboard series, an operator's call. With no model reachable the rewrite loop still runs its two passes (the rewriter degrades to the same query); they cost about 0.4 s of retrieval and no model call that could succeed, and skipping them would change routing, so they stay.
- **Not verified**: a live quota exhaustion on the free tier (no hosted LLM is called in this work); the new text through the Docker image (the code path is the one the Docker run took, re-driven here with the same trace shape).
- **At integration** (round 13; integrate13 at 157a237, main e0d2a02 with Decisions 99 and 100): offline suite 2,735 passed and 3 xfailed (2,736 on the final tree, whose refs fix adds one test); `-m models` 7 passed, and 7 again with the Hub forced offline; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS) and the answerability dev set are identical, field for field with timings, the output path and GPU memory aside, to round 12's final gate run; the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors; the pre-existing warning) and vitest (134 of 134) are clean. Docker, both architectures (arm64 under QEMU emulation, so its timings say nothing about the ARM host): Dockerfile.hf builds; every installed package equals its pin (141 of 151 installed, 0 differ). The production image, under docker-compose.prod.yml's environment against throwaway Qdrant and Postgres with no LLM reachable, answered the round-12 question with the new text and `outcome: "synthesis_failed"`, the trace's insufficient_context entry carrying `reason: "assessor_unavailable"`, on amd64 and on arm64 — the case round 12's Docker check found, now closed in the image. /health, /ready, ingest, the document list, the 404 without the admin key and the graceful stop passed as in round 12.
- **Impact**: src/workflow/orchestrator.py, app/components/verification.py, web/lib/normalize.ts, tests/test_assessor_outage.py, tests/test_streamlit_truthfulness.py, web/tests/normalize.test.ts
## Decision 100: The default models load at pinned Hub commits, and the image bakes the same commits
- **Date**: Session 22
- **Context**: Audit finding M5: `Dockerfile.hf` fetched bge-m3, the MiniLM reranker and the xsmall NLI model at whatever their repositories' `main` was on the day of the build, and a machine with an empty cache fetched them the same way at runtime. Only Laya (`LAYA_REVISION`) and FactCG (`nli._CHECKERS`) were pinned. The relevance floors, the reranker calibration profiles (Decision 17 and its successors) and every benchmark were measured on particular commits, so a new upload could move them in a fresh build, unannounced.
- **Evidence gathered first**: every default model's development-cache `refs/main` predates the round-12 gates and benchmarks, so those are the commits they measured; and each equals upstream `main` today (Hub API, read-only): bge-m3 `5617a9f`, ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2 `233902d`, nli-deberta-v3-xsmall `a150876`, bge-reranker-v2-m3 `953dc6f`, nli-deberta-v3-small `fa28048`. MiniLM's repository was renamed upstream in August 2026 (`ms-marco-MiniLM-L6-v2`; the old id redirects) with a new commit; the cache holds both that commit and the July one, and every file but the README is byte-identical between them (sha256), so the calibration is not affected.
- **Change**: `src/utils/model_cache.py` gains `PINNED_REVISIONS` (the five defaults) and `pinned_revision(repo_id)` (None for an override or a local directory). The four load sites — the embedder and the reranker (`src/vector_db/reranker.py`), the NLI cross-encoder (`src/verification/nli.py`) and the token counter (`src/utils/token_counter.py`) — pass the pin both to the cache lookup and to the loader itself, so a model missing from the cache is fetched at its commit, not at `main`. `Dockerfile.hf` fetches each of its three at `EMBEDDING_REVISION`, `RERANKER_REVISION` and `NLI_REVISION` (and the bge-m3 tokenizer at its commit), defaults equal to the pins; `tests/test_model_pins.py` fails when the two differ.
- **What did not change**: which models load, and from where on this machine: the cache's `main` already was the pinned commit for each, so every load resolves to the same snapshot directory as before. An overridden model (`EMBEDDING_MODEL`, `RERANKER_MODEL`, `NLI_MODEL` set to another repository) is unpinned at runtime as before; at build time its revision arg must be set too, or the fetch fails (a commit of one repository is not one of another) — loudly, at build time.
- **Gates**: offline suite 2,712 passed and 3 xfailed; `-m models` 7 passed, and 7 passed again with HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 and TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1 (the real weights, loaded at their pins from the cache with the Hub unreachable); each of the five pins resolves to a cached snapshot offline; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS) and the answerability dev set are identical field for field to round 12's final gate run apart from timings, the output path and GPU memory in use. The load-site tests in tests/test_model_cache.py asserted an unpinned load (`@None`) and failed on this change until updated to the pins; tests/test_model_pins.py (8) fails on main.
- **Not changed, for the owner**: base images still float on tags (`python:3.11-slim-bookworm`, `python:3.11-slim`, `postgres:16-alpine`, `caddy:2-alpine`; Qdrant is pinned at `v1.18.0`). Pinning them by digest makes builds reproducible but stops security patches reaching a rebuild until someone bumps the digest — a patching policy, not a code fix. FastEmbed's `Qdrant/bm25` lives in FastEmbed's own cache, not the Hub cache, and is not pinned here.
- **Not verified**: the image build itself until the round-13 Docker check (recorded under Decision 99's and this one's integration note); an override of a model at build time; the arm64 build of the pinned layer (emulated only, as in round 12).
- **At integration** (round 13; integrate13 at 157a237, main e0d2a02 with Decisions 99 and 100): offline suite 2,735 passed and 3 xfailed (2,736 on the final tree, whose refs fix adds one test); `-m models` 7 passed, and 7 again with the Hub forced offline; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS) and the answerability dev set are identical, field for field with timings, the output path and GPU memory aside, to round 12's final gate run; the web console's `tsc`, ESLint (0 errors; the pre-existing warning) and vitest (134 of 134) are clean. Docker, both architectures (arm64 under QEMU emulation, so its timings say nothing about the ARM host): Dockerfile.hf builds; every installed package equals its pin (141 of 151 installed, 0 differ). The first Docker check found one defect, fixed before the merge (1cb97f7): a download at a commit writes no `refs/main`, so the smoke's own load of bge-m3 by id with no revision found nothing with the Hub offline (online it would have fetched upstream main); the app's loaders pass the pin and were unaffected — the API warmed its models from the baked cache in 15 s with no Hub request in its log. The build now writes each model's `refs/main` at its pin and fails if a pinned snapshot is missing. Re-built on both architectures, with the Hub offline: `refs/main` is at the pin for all three, each pinned snapshot is cached, and the app's embedder, reranker, NLI model and token counter load, as does a load by id with no revision. Found, not changed: bge-m3's pinned commit ships `pytorch_model.bin` only, and a load by id (the build step's) also fetches `model.safetensors` from the Hub's safetensors-conversion branch (`refs/pr/130`, commit 9a0624b, not pinned). The app loads the snapshot directory at the pin, which holds the `.bin`, so its weights are the pinned ones — as on the development machine, where the benchmarks ran — and the extra 2.27 GB is unused image weight, present since before this round (the round-12 image is the same size). Review notes: overriding a model to another pinned id at build time with a revision other than its pin bakes one commit while the app asks for the other (the first query then downloads); a cache holding a different commit of a pinned model now fetches the pin rather than using it.
- **Impact**: src/utils/model_cache.py, src/vector_db/reranker.py, src/verification/nli.py, src/utils/token_counter.py, Dockerfile.hf, tests/test_model_pins.py, tests/test_model_cache.py
## Decision 101: A first-time ingest is staged and committed, so an upload cut short leaves nothing readable
- **Date**: Session 22
- **Context**: Outstanding issue M3 since round 12: "a crash mid-ingest leaves a partial document searchable". `index_document` wrote a new document's child points with their `deal_id` in batches of 100, then its parents. Its rollback (`except Exception`: delete every point of the document) runs only when the code gets to run it; a killed process, an OOM kill or a container restart does not, and neither does a cancelled request (`asyncio.CancelledError` is a `BaseException`). The batches already written then stayed searchable, the document list showed the document, and no registry entry or audit line said anything had been uploaded.
- **Measured on main first** (in-memory Qdrant, batches of 2, `tests/test_staged_ingest.py` run against main's code): killed during the third child batch, 4 children readable; cancelled at the same point, 4; killed during the parent upsert, 13. Of the first 15 tests written, 11 fail on main, each on that kind of leftover or on the recovery that follows it; of the 4 that pass, 3 check what must not change (a completed ingest, a re-index) and the fourth, uploading the file again after a crash mid-upsert, already completed the document on main.
- **Change** (`src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py`):
  - A first-time ingest (`existing.count == 0`) writes every point without `deal_id`, carrying `staged_deal_id` (`constants.STAGED_DEAL_FIELD`) instead. Every reader filters on `deal_id` (search, parent and sibling expansion, the tie-out, the checklist, the document list and counts, version candidates, supersession), so staged points are read by nothing.
  - Once every child and parent is written, `_commit_staged` sets `deal_id` with one `set_payload` per collection naming exactly the written ids — parents first, so a child is never readable without its parent section. The server applies a request whole (and replays it from its write-ahead log), so a client that dies there leaves each collection committed or still staged. It is not isolated from readers: a search running while the server applies it can see part of the children for that moment.
  - After the commit, optionally (Decision 88, `staging_cleanup`), the marker is removed from the committed points (so a committed point stores exactly what a direct write stores) and points an earlier, cut-short upload of the same document staged under other ids (a different chunking) are deleted.
  - A failed upsert or commit rolls back the committed and the staged points of the document.
  - A re-index (`existing.count > 0`) is unchanged: it writes in place with `deal_id`, so the serving copy is never hidden (audit I9).
  - Uploads of the same bytes are serialised per `(deal_id, doc_id)` by an in-process lock (`_document_lock`): staged side by side, the later upload's upserts would hide the earlier one's committed points again, and the earlier one's cleanup would strip the later one's marker (found by the independent review). One at a time, the later upload finds the document and re-indexes in place. The API runs a single worker; a separate process (a CLI loader) is not covered, and for that case the cleanup only strips the marker from points that carry `deal_id`.
- **Recovery and retention**: uploading the same file again commits it whole; purging the deal deletes staged points too (`_delete_deal_vectors`, a second delete per collection); the sandbox sweeper facets `staged_deal_id` in both collections, so a sandbox holding only staged points is still found and purged (new keyword index on both collections, created at startup by `setup_collections`); `reingest_report` lists staged documents apart ("staged, never committed"), not as documents of a deal; the identity backfill skips points with no `deal_id`.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_staged_ingest.py` (committed points equal a direct write; a re-index never stages; killed and cancelled mid-upsert; between the two commits; the same file again; leftovers of another chunking deleted, or left staged when the delete fails; a failed commit rolls back; purge; the sweeper; the report; concurrent identical uploads — fails with the lock disabled; the cleanup guard; the backfill), `tests/test_api_security.py` (the purge issues the staged deletes). The staged tests run with unit-weight sparse vectors (the `default_bm25_is_a_stub` fixture).
- **Gates**: offline suite 2,755 passed and 3 xfailed (2,736 on main plus this decision's 19 tests); `-m models` 7 passed, and 7 again with the Hub forced offline; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72; ruff clean; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS against eval/baseline.json) and the answerability dev set are identical, field for field with timings, the output path and GPU memory aside, to round 13's final gate run (0 differences in each). On a Qdrant **server** (v1.18.0, as compose; the tests' `rd.new_client` pointed at it, the expected store kept in memory): `tests/test_staged_ingest.py` and `tests/test_ingest_stamp.py`, 28 of 28. The first server run failed 4 comparisons: the new file had imported the unit-weight BM25 stub fixture without applying it, and real BM25 weights stored as float32 on the server do not equal the in-memory store's Python floats. The fixture is now applied as in test_ingest_stamp.py; a replay of one failed case with the real encoder on the server, compared field by field with a clean ingest, found ids, payloads, dense vectors and sparse indices identical and the 13 children's sparse values equal at float32. Docker, both architectures (arm64 under QEMU emulation, so its timings say nothing about the ARM host): Dockerfile.hf builds; every installed package equals its pin (141 of 151 installed, 0 differ); the model and PDF smoke passes, every model at its pin with the Hub offline; the API under docker-compose.prod.yml's environment, against throwaway Qdrant and Postgres, answers /health and /ready, ingests the sample credit agreement (13 children, 5 parents) and lists it; on the server afterwards no point lacks `deal_id`, none carries the staging marker, and the `staged_deal_id` keyword index exists on both collections; the query returns Decision 99's outcome; the 404 without the admin key and the graceful stop pass; the owner's volumes are unchanged. Ingest took 5 s on amd64 (round 13: 4 s) and 1,694 s emulated on arm64 (round 13: 1,606 s) — emulation noise around embedding, not a measurement of the staging cost. An independent review found one defect (concurrent uploads of the same bytes, fixed above with the lock and the cleanup guard) and, on re-review, none.
- **Cost**: two extra payload writes per point of a first-time ingest (the commit and the marker removal), on top of the upsert; small beside embedding on the CPU host (Decision 71: ~1.6 s a chunk on the emulated host), not measured on the ARM host.
- **Not covered**: two processes uploading the same bytes at once (no cross-process lock); points from a cut-short upload stay until the deal is purged or the file is uploaded again (the report lists them; nothing deletes them on its own outside a sandbox).
- **Not verified**: a process actually killed during an ingest against a server (the tests raise a BaseException inside the client calls, which is what a killed process leaves on the server: whatever requests completed); the cost of the two extra payload writes on the ARM host; cross-process concurrent uploads of the same bytes.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, src/vector_db/constants.py, src/vector_db/collection_manager.py, src/vector_db/reingest_report.py, src/vector_db/identity_backfill.py, api/routes/deals.py, README.md, tests/test_staged_ingest.py, tests/test_api_security.py

## Decision 102: The document list keeps pre-restart documents, the read limiter keeps a minute of history, and a purge is not undone by an ingest in flight
- **Date**: Session 23
- **Context**: Three defects an independent review of the less-reviewed API code found and reproduced (round 15), none recorded as a trade-off:
  1. `_deal_documents` (the document list and the risk dashboard) returned the in-memory registry whenever it was non-empty and rebuilt from Qdrant only when it was empty. After a restart, the first upload to a deal made a one-record registry, and every document indexed before the restart disappeared from both views while retrieval still used them (repro: 3 rebuilt documents, one upload, 1 listed).
  2. `_SlidingWindowLimiter.hit` trimmed every bucket's history at 24 hours. The read bucket has no daily cap (120 a minute), so a client staying under the minute limit held ~7,200 timestamps an hour (~5.6 MB a day), and every request scanned them all on the event loop for the minute count (3.1 ms a request after an hour).
  3. A purge (tab-close beacon, admin DELETE, sandbox sweeper — all `purge_deal`) that ran while a file was being indexed deleted the points written so far and forgot the deal; the ingest then wrote the rest, committed (Decision 101), and registered the document: the purge was undone, and the upload stayed readable until a sweep, never for a permanent deal.
- **Change**:
  1. `_deal_documents` merges: every document the store holds, with this process's record in place of the rebuilt one wherever it has one (it carries the upload date and the extractor's full risk signals); a rebuilt document a registered upload supersedes is shown superseded, as the store says once `mark_superseded` stamped it. An unreadable store returns the registry, as before.
  2. The limiter keeps only the history its limits in force can count: a day with a daily cap, a minute without one, nothing when both are off. Every admit/deny decision and `Retry-After` is unchanged with fixed limits.
  3. `deals.watch_for_purge` gives each ingest a ticket for the time it runs; `purge_deal` marks the open tickets of its deal before its first await, and a ticket opened while a purge of its deal runs starts marked. The route sets the ticket's `doc_id` (the id `index_document` derives, hashed before indexing) and checks the ticket immediately before `register_document`, with no await between. A marked ingest discards its upload: it waits for the deal's purges to finish, then `ingest_pipeline.delete_document` deletes the document's committed and staged points in both collections under the per-document lock — unless the same bytes were uploaded to the deal since the purge: a registered upload keeps the points (they share their ids); one still in flight is waited for, and the decision is taken again when it ends, so one that fails or is cancelled without registering does not leave them behind. The caller gets 409 ("a delete of this deal was requested ... the upload was discarded"), the audit log `document_discarded` (with `points_deleted`); a delete that fails is a 500 with the request id, metric `error`, audit `document_discard_failed`.
- **Found in review and fixed before the merge** (two further independent reviews and the automated security review): the first discard deleted a valid later upload of the same bytes (same `doc_id`, re-indexed in place) — reproduced with the real pipeline; a failed delete gave a bare 500 with no audit record; an ingest opened after the purge marked tickets was not marked; then the discard skipped its delete as soon as it saw such an upload in flight, so one that then failed on the re-index-in-place path (which deletes nothing on failure, audit I9) left the purged deal's points readable with no record — reproduced. Each fix has a test that fails without it (checked by mutation: purge marking off, the re-upload guard off, the purge wait off, and the previous commit's route).
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_document_registry_restart.py` (6; 3 fail on the old code), `tests/test_limiter_memory.py` (4; 2 fail on the old code), `tests/test_purge_during_ingest.py` (15: tickets, the route's 409 and audit, a failed delete, a purge running when an ingest starts, a failed purge, the same file uploaded again — registered, in flight, in flight then failing — the wait for a running purge, the ticket's `doc_id` against `extract_and_chunk`'s, `delete_document` on committed and staged points of a real in-memory Qdrant).
- **Gates**: offline suite 2,780 passed and 3 xfailed (2,755 on main plus 25 new: 6, 4 and 15); ruff clean; the `fuzz` profile 72 of 72. `-m models` 7 passed, and 7 with the Hub forced offline; CI's verification gate exits 0; the FactCG benchmark run, the golden set (PASS) and the answerability dev set have 0 differences from round 14's runs (`compare_runs.py`, timings and paths aside) — run on the tree with Decision 103 and this decision's first commit; the later commits change only the ingest and purge paths, which those runs do not exercise, and the full suite was re-run on the final tree. In the image (amd64, below): one upload indexed 13 chunks and was registered (200).
- **Cost**: the document list and the risk dashboard now scroll the deal's chunk payloads on every call, as they already did for every deal not uploaded to since the last restart (the common case); a discard can wait as long as a concurrent re-upload of the same file takes; one extra threaded SHA-256 of each (size-capped) upload.
- **Not covered**:
  - A discard whose own request is cancelled while it waits (in practice a server shutdown) and whose awaited re-upload then ends unregistered leaves the points: the document list now shows them, the sandbox sweep removes them, an admin DELETE again removes them.
  - When the purge itself fails before dropping the registry, a re-upload of an already registered file keeps its points (the deal and that document were never deleted), and the uploader is still told the upload was discarded; a first upload is deleted although the deal survives.
  - Runtime changes of the rate-limit variables: switching a daily cap on mid-day counts only the history kept since (env changes restart the process on the hosts in use).
  - Uploads into a sandbox purged but not expired are still accepted, as before (`require_sandbox_deal` checks expiry only).
- **Impact**: api/routes/deals.py, api/routes/ingest.py, api/security.py, src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, tests/test_document_registry_restart.py, tests/test_limiter_memory.py, tests/test_purge_during_ingest.py

## Decision 103: A model outage counts as synthesis_failed on the query metric, and the image no longer bakes unused bge-m3 weights
- **Date**: Session 23
- **Context**: Outstanding issues 8 and 9 (round 13). `redline_queries_total` labelled a completed run `refusal` whenever `is_refusal` was true, so a run whose model was unreachable — `synthesis_failed` in the API and the audit log (Decision 99) — looked on a dashboard like an unanswerable question: an outage read as a run of refusals. And the image carried 2.27 GB of bge-m3 weights the app never reads: the build step loaded the embedder by Hub id, and transformers, finding a `.bin`-only commit, fetched a safetensors conversion from the Hub's unpinned conversion branch (`refs/pr/130`, commit 9a0624b); the app loads the pinned snapshot directory, which holds the `.bin` (Decision 100). Both were held as optional owner decisions; the owner asked for the Docker disk space back and for the clearly needed improvements to be made.
- **Change**:
  - `api/routes/query.py::_metric_outcome`: a run whose outcome is `synthesis_failed` is counted with that label, blocking and stream alike; every other run keeps `answered` / `refusal`. No dashboard or alert in the repository reads the series.
  - `Dockerfile.hf`: the embedder is fetched with `snapshot_download` at its pinned commit (other runtimes' copies of the weights — `onnx/`, another 2.27 GB — and images ignored), then loaded from that directory with the Hub offline, as the app loads it; the build fails if that load fails. The reranker and the NLI model ship safetensors at their pins and are loaded as before. `tests/test_model_pins.py` now also fails if the embedder is loaded by id again.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_platform_lifecycle.py::test_failed_synthesis_is_not_verified_and_is_reported_as_such` (the metric, both transports; fails on the old code), `tests/test_model_pins.py` (fails on the old Dockerfile).
- **Gates**: as Decision 102, plus the image (linux/amd64, built from a clean worktree at the final commit, Docker 29.1.3): the build's offline load of the embedder passes; the HF cache holds one bge-m3 snapshot, the pin, and no safetensors file under bge-m3; its `pytorch_model.bin` has the SHA-256 of the development machine's (b5e0ce34…), and the app's embedder, loaded offline from the snapshot, gives the development machine's CPU embedding of a fixed sentence (cosine 1.0000001, largest difference 6.7e-8); a load by id with no revision still works offline (refs/main). The API under the production environment against throwaway stores: the query with no LLM reachable answers `synthesis_failed` and `/metrics` shows `redline_queries_total{outcome="synthesis_failed",transport="blocking"} 1`; no request to the Hub in its log; the owner's volumes and containers unchanged. Image size as Docker reports it: 3,158,560,916 bytes (round 14: 4,512,815,497). **Not verified**: the linux/arm64 build — the round's Docker run was stopped under memory pressure during it and not repeated; the change is a Python call in a RUN step, the same on both architectures, and round 14 built the rest of the file for arm64.
- **Impact**: api/routes/query.py, src/utils/metrics.py, Dockerfile.hf, tests/test_platform_lifecycle.py, tests/test_model_pins.py

## Decision 104: The pinned dependencies are audited against published advisories, the reachable ones are fixed, and the Streamlit image runs
- **Date**: Session 24
- **Context**:
  - Round 12 pinned every package (`constraints.txt`) so that builds are reproducible; nothing looked at the advisories published against the pins since. pip-audit (OSV) on 2026-11-22: about 135 distinct advisories in 13 pinned packages, every one installed in the API image — Pillow 54, GitPython 33, aiohttp 20, filelock 9, transformers 6, anyio 3, urllib3 3, litellm 2, and one each for cryptography, h2, hpack, multidict and sentence-transformers. Dependabot alerts were disabled and CI ran no audit. torch, installed separately at `Dockerfile.hf`'s `TORCH_VERSION`, was outside the lock and so outside any audit (one advisory, found by the second review). npm: 6 moderate, all in the lint toolchain.
  - Reachability, checked rather than assumed: the app's code never imports Pillow, but openpyxl's full load (every workbook up to 25 MB) opens each embedded picture with `PIL.Image.open` to read its size — the one path by which an upload reached Pillow's parsers. The PDF, Word and PowerPoint readers decode no images.
  - Streamlit, GitPython and pyarrow were in the API image (one `requirements.txt`), which never imports them.
  - The Streamlit image was broken: `Dockerfile.streamlit` copied `app/` and `.streamlit/` only, and the console has imported `src.utils.admin_client` since 2026-09-24 (commit dcfd018). Rebuilt in the image's layout, `import app.streamlit_app` fails with `No module named 'src'`; Streamlit's own health check would still pass. The image also installed unpinned ranges.
  - The "Uptime check" workflow exits 0 when `BACKEND_URL` is unset, which it is: it showed green four times a day while checking nothing.
- **Change**:
  - **Excel**: `excel_processor._skip_embedded_images` drops a workbook's pictures the way openpyxl does when Pillow is absent (its own `PILImage` switch). Cell values, number formats, merged cells and charts are untouched: the extraction of a workbook with pictures and a chart is identical with and without the switch, and Pillow opens 2 pictures without it, 0 with it.
  - **Upgrades** (each release at least two weeks old; filelock 4.3.0 was one day old, so 4.2.4): aiohttp 3.14.4, anyio 4.14.2, urllib3 2.8.0, h2 4.4.1, hpack 4.2.0, multidict 6.9.1, cryptography 50.0.0, filelock 4.2.4, litellm 1.89.7 (the 1.89 line's security release; its diff on the SDK path is end-of-stream error handling and a sensitive-parameter list), Pillow 13.0.0 — which needs fastembed 0.9.1 (0.7 capped Pillow below 12, and no 12.x release fixes 37 of the 54) and Streamlit 1.67.0 (the first that allows Pillow 13; 1.62 dropped GitPython). GitPython, gitdb, smmap, blinker, cachetools and toml are no longer installed. fastembed 0.9.1's BM25 vectors are bit-identical to 0.7.4's on 12,000 stored chunks (6,000 LegalBench, 6,000 FinanceBench) and 68 queries, through the app's own encoding functions: existing indexes stay valid. Not taken: litellm 1.95+ (the remaining advisory is in its proxy server, which the app does not run, and a seven-release jump in the layer every LLM call goes through needs a live check against the provider); npm's fix (typescript-eslint 8.73 and minimatch 11.0.9 were published a week before: a dev-only lint-time ReDoS against a week-old toolchain that runs on the build machine — to be taken after 2026-11-30).
  - **Package split**: `requirements-ui.txt` (Streamlit, pyarrow, requests, python-dotenv) for the console; `requirements.txt` (the API image) no longer lists Streamlit or pyarrow; `requirements-dev.txt` includes both, so CI tests both. `Dockerfile.streamlit` installs `requirements-ui.txt` with `-c constraints.txt` as wheels, and copies the three `src` files the console imports.
  - **The audit**: `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml`, weekly and on every change to the pins, runs pip-audit (OSV) over `constraints.txt` plus torch at `TORCH_VERSION` and `scripts/check_vulnerabilities.py` over its report. An advisory fails it unless `security/accepted-vulnerabilities.toml` accepts it for that package version, with a reason and a review date at most 200 days out; an overdue review fails it; so does a pin missing from the report or skipped by it (an empty report no longer passes). npm: packages that ship fail at moderate, the toolchain at high; npm has no accepted list.
  - **Accepted** (9, review by 2027-02-28), none reachable as the app runs: transformers 4.57.6 ×6 (malicious `config.json`, `Trainer`, LightGlue, `load_custom_generate`, `save_pretrained`, X-CLIP conversion), sentence-transformers 2.7.0 ×1 (local `modules.json` code), torch 2.11.0 ×1 (`torch.jit.script` on a crafted function; the app scripts only transformers' DeBERTa-v2 helpers, at import), litellm 1.89.7 ×1 (proxy endpoint). The four pinned checkpoints were checked: no config sets `_attn_implementation*`, bge-m3's `modules.json` names only sentence-transformers' own modules, the cross-encoders and Laya ship no code. Their fixes need transformers 5 with sentence-transformers ≥ 5.6 (2.7 requires transformers < 5), or torch 2.13: each moves every embedding, rerank and NLI score the calibration rests on, and is a measured round of its own, not taken here.
  - **Workflows**: the uptime job is skipped (grey) without a URL. `.github/dependabot.example.yml` (npm and Actions, 14-day cooldown; pip left out because `constraints.txt` is generated from the measured environment, which Dependabot cannot do) is inert until the owner renames it.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_excel_untrusted_images.py` (2; both fail without the switch), `tests/test_streamlit_image.py` (6; 3 fail on the old Dockerfile, 1 without python-dotenv), `tests/test_check_vulnerabilities.py` (19), `tests/test_constraints.py`.
- **Gates**: final tree (4ea74fd): offline suite 2,852 passed and 3 expected failures; ruff clean; fuzz profile 72/72; the seven model tests pass, and again with the Hub offline; verification, FactCG, golden retrieval (PASS) and answerability outputs show 0 differences from round 15 field by field; `update_constraints.py --check` clean; the Dependency audit run locally as CI runs it: 146 pins audited, 9 advisories, all accepted. Images: linux/amd64, built from a clean worktree at e10be39 in a dedicated buildx builder removed afterwards (no global prune): the API image 3,086,896,671 bytes (round 15: 3,158,560,916), every installed package at its pin, Streamlit, pyarrow and GitPython absent; the models load offline at their pins, the embedding equals the development machine's (cosine 1.0000001), Pillow 13.0.0 and fastembed 0.9.1; the API against throwaway Qdrant and Postgres: ingest, a query with no LLM reachable `synthesis_failed` and `redline_queries_total{outcome="synthesis_failed"} 1`, the audit input built in the image (146 pins), no Hub request. The Streamlit image 191,800,647 bytes, at its pins: the console imports in the image and `/_stcore/health` answers. The owner's images, volumes and containers unchanged. linux/arm64 (QEMU emulation, at dea471b, run after the merge on the owner's word): the API image 3,037,137,177 bytes, every package at its pin, Streamlit, pyarrow and GitPython absent, the models load offline at their pins, the embedding equals the development machine's (cosine 1.0), Pillow 13.0.0 and fastembed 0.9.1; the Streamlit image 184,364,347 bytes, the console imports and `/_stcore/health` answers. The owner's images and volumes unchanged after both runs.
- **Not covered**: the transformers 5 / torch 2.13 upgrade (above); setuptools in the base image (2 build-time advisories, outside the lock: decision 11, base-image pinning); the npm toolchain advisories until 2026-11-30.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/excel_processor.py, requirements.txt, requirements-ui.txt, requirements-dev.txt, constraints.txt, Dockerfile.streamlit, scripts/check_vulnerabilities.py, security/accepted-vulnerabilities.toml, .github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml, .github/workflows/keepalive.yml, .github/dependabot.example.yml, tests/test_excel_untrusted_images.py, tests/test_streamlit_image.py, tests/test_check_vulnerabilities.py

## Decision 105: A backup and restore tool for the index and the audit log, and the re-ingestion runbook rehearsed on a Qdrant server
- **Date**: Session 24
- **Context**: Blocker H3 — no backups, no restore drill — and outstanding issue 5: the re-ingestion runbook (Decision 97) had run in local mode only; its snapshot, download and rollback commands, written from Qdrant's documentation, had never run against a server. The persistent state is the index (both collections) and the audit log; Postgres holds checkpoints (each run clears its own) and the day's quota counters, and uploaded files are deleted once indexed.
- **Change**: `scripts/qdrant_backup.py` — `backup` (one snapshot per collection, downloaded with its SHA-256 and deleted from the server, whose snapshot directory is not on a volume; exact counts and, with `--fingerprint`, a hash of every point, each taken before and after the snapshot: a collection that changed meanwhile fails the backup unless `--allow-changes`; the audit files; `manifest.json`), `verify` (every file against the manifest), `restore` (verifies first; plans the audit files before writing anything and never overwrites one — a live file holding the saved lines is kept, any other difference is written beside it as `*.restored`; replaces a collection that holds points only with `--replace-existing`; uploads with `priority=snapshot` and the checksum; checks counts and fingerprints; a failure after one collection was replaced says so). DEPLOYMENT.md: "Backups and restore", and the runbook's phase 0 and rollback now use the script. An independent review of the first version found twelve problems (among them a restore that could replace a live audit file with the backup's shorter copy, a half-replaced index on a failed upload with no word of it, and a backup whose counts were read after its snapshot); all were fixed before the rehearsal, each with a test. A second review found that each collection was checked only around its own snapshot (both are now counted before either snapshot and after both); that a restore which timed out or met a 5xx said nothing was replaced while Qdrant carries on a recovery it has accepted (it now says the state is unknown, and keeps "not replaced" for an upload refused up front); that a failure checking a collection after its upload did not name what was replaced; that `*.restored` audit copies were left out of later backups; and that a failed snapshot delete went unreported. Each fixed with a test.
- **Rehearsal** (Qdrant 1.18.0, the production pin, in throwaway containers; the tools run from the host against them; GPU embedding; `LAYA_ENABLED=0`; round 12's inputs: the old index of 28 documents, its original files, its manifest):
  - Old index loaded into server A: 7,761 + 1,556 points. Backup: 12 s (113 MB and 24 MB snapshots), stable, fingerprinted; verify: every file matches.
  - Runbook on A: P1 report, P2 dry run and re-ingest (19 of 19 planned, none failed, 6 min), P3 identity backfill, P4 report — every step exit 0.
  - Against a fresh ingest of the same 28 files by the same code on server B (364 s), round 12's pre-registered checks: point ids (C1), payloads field by field (C2), the report's invariants (C3/C4), identity and sparse encoding (C5), vectors (C6: worst cosine 0.9999999999999998, largest difference 0.0) and the dry-run's classification (C9) hold for all 28 documents; counts per deal equal (FinanceBench 7,439 + 1,318, LegalBench 961 + 88, sample deal 111 + 35). C8 (the 9 text documents untouched bitwise against old_master) fails for all 9 on a server for a reason of the rehearsal, not the procedure: old_master is a local-mode store holding the vectors as Python floats, and loading it into a server stores them as float32. C7 (tie-out equal to the reference) failed on FinanceBench — conflicts and agreements equal (58, 14), figures without a period 17,393 against 17,438 and single-source 2,515 against 2,473 — and was traced to the product, not the procedure: the runbook's tools write through the app's client, which uses gRPC whenever no API key is set, and over gRPC Qdrant does not keep a JSON object's key order, so every table row came back with its columns shuffled; the reference had been written by the rehearsal's own client over REST, which keeps it. Value by value and type by type the two indexes were otherwise identical. Fixed by Decision 108: re-ingested with the fix, the same index gives the reference's tie-out exactly (2,473, 17,438, 58, 14).
  - Rollback: a restore without `--replace-existing` refused ("holds 8511 points"); with it, 8 s, both collections back to the backup's counts and fingerprints — the pre-procedure index point for point; the audit file kept, not overwritten. A restore of the same backup into an empty server: likewise.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_qdrant_backup.py` (24, against an in-process stand-in for the snapshot API: checksums, the stable-backup rule across both collections, every audit case, refused, timed-out and half-done restores, refusal to replace).
- **Not covered**: the runbook's one-off-container invocations (the tools ran from the host against the containers); a two-core ARM host's embedding time; where backups are kept off the VM, encryption at rest, schedule and retention — the owner's decisions (H3), set out in DEPLOYMENT.md.
- **Impact**: scripts/qdrant_backup.py, tests/test_qdrant_backup.py, DEPLOYMENT.md

## Decision 106: Every local-model call carries one context window, and a prompt that does not fit is not sent — found by the first end-to-end run with a real LLM
- **Date**: Session 24
- **Context**: H1 said the current code had never run end to end with a live LLM. With hosted calls ruled out, the first run used the configured local rung (`ollama/qwen2.5:14b`, Ollama 0.34.4, RTX 5070 Ti 12 GB) behind the real API in the deployed CPU profile (bge-m3, MiniLM, the xsmall NLI and Laya on CPU, no Gemini key, embedded index), with the web console built against it and driven in a browser. Seven checkable questions on the sample deal: 4 of the 6 answerable ones ended `synthesis_failed`. Ollama's log showed why: `truncating input prompt limit=2050 prompt=4412 keep=4 new=2050`, 12 times (4 prompts × 3 attempts, 4,126–6,080 tokens). Only the JSON calls set `num_ctx` (8,192); every prose call ran at Ollama's default — 4,096 on a GPU under 24 GiB — and a longer prompt keeps its first 4 and last ~2,046 tokens: the synthesis instructions were gone, the replies came back uncited and were refused, and the model reloaded each time the window changed between calls (5 loads at each size). Measured on the same GPU (prompt of 5,532 tokens, marker instruction at its start): default — 2,050 read, instruction lost; 8,192 — all read, 100% on GPU, 41 tokens/s; 12,288 — 90%, 31 tokens/s; 16,384 — 83%, 24 tokens/s; a 9,079-token prompt at 8,192 lost its start (4,098 read) and at 16,384 did not. A large `num_predict` never truncates the prompt; only a prompt longer than the window does.
- **Change**: every local call (structured, prose, streamed prose) sends one `num_ctx` — `OLLAMA_NUM_CTX`, default 16,384 — so the model loads once, and asks Ollama to refuse what does not fit instead of cutting it: `truncate=false` (a prompt longer than the window is refused, counted with the model's own tokenizer) and `shift=false` (a reply that reaches the window ends with finish_reason `length` instead of discarding the context's start). Verified on Ollama 0.34.4: a 5,522-token prompt at 4,096 → HTTP 400 "request (5522 tokens) exceeds the available context size (4096 tokens)"; a 2,713-token prompt asked for 4,000 tokens stopped at 4,096 in all with `length` (with shift on it ran to 6,713 tokens, past its own instructions). LiteLLM (1.89.7) puts every provider option inside `options`, where Ollama ignores these two (measured: the prompt was truncated with both set there), so `_move_ollama_flags_to_top_level` moves them in LiteLLM's Ollama request builder; a test checks the request it builds, so an upgrade that changes the builder fails there. Ollama's refusal, which LiteLLM reports as a connection error, is classified `REQUEST_INVALID` and raised as `LocalContextExceeded`: not retried, and synthesis ends at once with the counts in its trace. The local structured-call deadline is 120 s (was 60): at 16,384 the model runs ~24 tokens/s, and the judge's 1,500-token budget alone takes ~62 s. Cloud calls are unchanged. `.env.example` listed `OLLAMA_URL`, which nothing reads (a run with it set to a closed port still reached the default address); LiteLLM reads `OLLAMA_API_BASE`, which the compose file already sets (Decision 8).
- **Found in review and changed before the merge**: the first version estimated the prompt with the embedder's tokenizer × 1.25 and refused it here. A second independent review showed that qwen2.5 splits every digit into its own token, so number-heavy tables undercounted (a 400-row table passed the estimate at ≥ 17,090 real tokens), and that the check left no room for the reply, which Ollama would then shift out of the window. Ollama's own refusal replaced the estimate.
- **Result** (same questions, data and model, before → after): consideration ($58.00 a share, ~$696M), FY2023 revenue ($452.8M vs $387.1M), the credit agreement's change of control (yes, §8.1(k)) and the DataFlow litigation (patent infringement, N.D. Cal., 2023-CV-04821) went from `synthesis_failed` to answered with the expected facts, cited, three with `validation_status` warning (the validator flagging qwen's filler sentences); adjusted EBITDA ($99.0M, 21.9%) stayed answered; the out-of-scope question stayed refused. Ollama: no truncation, one load at 16,384. In the browser the same consideration question was answered "Verified", 6 of 6 statements supported, in 52.9 s.
- **Also measured in the run**: streaming (first token 21.5 s, 397 token events); the anonymous sandbox (create, upload, ask, purge, then an empty document list); the document list, risk signals, tie-out and checklist (0.02–1.24 s); documents rebuilt from the store after each restart (Decision 102, live); an outage (`OLLAMA_API_BASE` on a closed port): `synthesis_failed`, the outage text in the API and the console ("a service problem, not a finding about your question"), and `redline_queries_total{outcome="synthesis_failed"}` (Decision 103, live), after ~40 s on Windows, where each refused local connection takes ~8 s.
- **Found, not changed here** (outstanding):
  - The top-ten-customer question (47.8% of FY2023 revenue, in the customer contracts schedule) was answered "the documents do not contain" under outcome `answered`: classified financial, retrieval searched only the financial documents, and the heuristic quality score (0.975 on figure-rich context) triggered no rewrite. A second question classified financial (the consideration, in the merger agreement) scored 0.01 and recovered through the rewrite. Changing category routing or the heuristic moves retrieval for every question and needs the benchmarks.
  - With `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""` on a CUDA build of torch on Windows, `torch.cuda.is_available()` is True with 0 devices: the embedder and rerankers still loaded on the GPU and Laya failed ("Invalid device id"). `-1` hides the GPU. Not the CPU-only ARM host's case.
  - The log line "All cloud quota exhausted, falling back to local model" also appears when no key is configured (its `keys_configured` says 0).
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_local_context_window.py` (11: one window and both flags on every local call, none on cloud calls, the flags at the top of the request LiteLLM builds, Ollama's refusal classified and not retried, synthesis ending at once with the reason); the live checks above.
- **Not covered**: the cloud path — no hosted call was made, by rule; the ARM host; the local rung on another GPU (`OLLAMA_NUM_CTX` lowers the window: 8,192 keeps qwen2.5:14b entirely on 12 GB). A synthesis context that fills its budget (64,000 characters, ~16.7k tokens) does not fit 16,384 and is refused with the count, rather than answered from fewer chunks: fitting the context to a local window is outstanding.
- **Impact**: src/llm/litellm_wrapper.py, src/llm/failures.py, config/litellm_config.yaml, .env.example, tests/test_local_context_window.py

## Decision 107: The owner's decision — the LLM provider stays on free tiers for now
- **Date**: Session 24
- **Context**: Decision 2 of the round-14 report (a paid tier under a data processing agreement, or a self-hosted model). The owner, 2026-11-22: "llm provider will be run on free tiers for now".
- **What it settles and what it leaves**: no paid provider or self-hosted model is set up. Blocker B1 stands as the round-12 audit put it — the free tier's terms say not to send it confidential data — so a deployment on it is for non-confidential material: the pilot rule. Capacity stays the free tier's (about 20 requests a day per model and key, walked as `src/llm/model_registry.py`'s ladders describe); its exhaustion and outages now read as such to users (Decisions 99, 103, 106). The local rung works again (Decision 106) where a GPU host runs Ollama; the ARM VM does not.
- **Proposed to the owner, not done**: a line on the upload panel and in the console saying uploaded text is sent to a third-party AI service on a free tier, so a pilot user does not upload what they must not; its wording is the owner's.
- **Impact**: none in code.

## Decision 108: A table row's cells are read in the table's order on a Qdrant server too
- **Date**: Session 24
- **Context**: Found by the re-ingestion rehearsal (Decision 105). A structured table row stores its cells as `values`, a JSON object keyed by column. The app's Qdrant client uses gRPC whenever no API key is set — the production compose file sets none — and over gRPC Qdrant does not keep a JSON object's key order (protobuf maps carry none): every FinanceBench chunk came back with its rows' columns shuffled ("Column 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 2, 11, 5, 10, 3"), while local-mode stores, where every benchmark runs, keep them. The figure registry behind the tie-out reads a figure's period from neighbouring cells and a "$" cell's header from the cell before it (`cells[index - 1]`): on the 28-document batch the tie-out counted 45 fewer figures without a period and 42 more single-source ones than the same documents in natural order, conflicts and agreements unchanged there. In real JPMorgan 10-K and 10-Q chunks, reversing a row's columns changes which figures are recorded. The batch's rows: 4,800 with placeholder names only ("Column N"), 1,301 with header names, 171 mixed.
- **Change**: `financial_table_converter.structured_rows` writes each row's order in `columns`, a list (a list keeps its order over REST and gRPC alike). `numerical_registry.row_cells` reads cells in that order; for rows written before, placeholder names sort by number — exactly their order — and any other names keep the stored order, the best there is without re-ingesting the document.
- **Verified**: on two Qdrant 1.18.0 servers, the batch's old index re-ingested by the fixed code through the app's gRPC client gives the fresh reference's FinanceBench tie-out exactly (2,473 single-source, 17,438 without a period, 58 conflicts, 14 agreements), against 2,515 and 17,393 before the fix; on the shuffled index written before it, the placeholder fallback alone already restores the figures without a period (17,438). Rows in natural order read as before (sorting them changes nothing), so the local-mode benchmarks do not move.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_table_column_order.py` (10, on one real JPMorgan 10-Q chunk: shuffled cells record other figures when read in stored order; with `columns`, or with placeholder names, they read as the natural row; the converter writes the order).
- **Not covered**: an index already written to a server keeps its header-named rows shuffled (about one row in four in this batch) until each document is re-ingested from its file; the re-ingestion report names such documents since Decision 111.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/financial_table_converter.py, src/utils/numerical_registry.py, tests/test_table_column_order.py, tests/fixtures/table_column_order_chunk.json

## Decision 109: Agent 1's guess at the document category is a hint, not a search filter
- **Date**: Session 25
- **Context**: In the first end-to-end run with a real LLM (Decision 106), "What share of FY2023 revenue comes from the top ten customers?" was answered "the provided documents do not contain specific information about the revenue contribution from the top ten customers": Agent 1 classified it `financial` and proposed `document_category: financial`, and retrieval applied that as a hard Qdrant `must` on the first pass, so the customer schedule that states 47.8% (categorised `legal`) was outside the search space. The context that remained scored 0.975 in the heuristic quality gate, no rewrite ran, and the claim checker's judge confirmed the absence statements against the passages it was given — the same passages the filter had chosen. The progressive relaxation (the filter dropped only after a rewrite) never triggered because a well-scoring wrong context is incomplete, not weak. No benchmark had measured this: every retrieval eval passes no category.
- **Measured**: the real retrieval node on the 35 answerable golden questions, the category set the way Agent 1 sets it, scored with the eval's own `score_run` (no LLM). No category: 97.1% of expected facts in the synthesis context, recall@10 0.787. The right category as a filter: 95.9%, recall@10 0.891 — a sharper ranking, but no context gained a fact (0 better, 1 worse). A wrong category as a filter: 21.0%, recall@10 0 — facts lost in 97 of 103 question/category pairs, and the heuristic gate admitted 21 of those 97, which is the confident "the documents do not contain it". Two candidate handlings of a wrong guess: dropping the filter, 97.7%; the filtered candidates plus the unfiltered search's, 98.1% — tied on context, at the cost of a second search and up to ten more cross-encoder pairs per pass.
- **Change**: `retrieval_executor` filters every pass on deal, version and PII only; the guessed category is recorded in the trace and the stream as `category_hint` (classified public: one of seven category names, already in Agent 1's public output) and never filters. The progressive relaxation and the per-sub-question stripping, which existed to undo the filter, are gone. The stream's query-intelligence stage reports `category_hint` instead of `filters`, which would now name a filter that is not applied. Production now retrieves the way every benchmark measured it.
- **Verified live** (local qwen2.5:14b through the real API, the same sample deal and questions as Decision 106): the concentration question answers "The top ten customers represent 47.8% of Aurora's FY2023 revenue", citing the customer schedule; the merger-consideration and credit change-of-control answers move from warning to passed (the first had needed a rewrite); revenue, adjusted EBITDA and litigation are unchanged and the MSCI question is still refused. One run per question on a local model: the answers' wording is not a measurement.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_agents.py::TestCategoryIsAHintNotAFilter` (no pass of any attempt, parent or sub-question, carries a category or any other proposed key; the hint is in the trace), the decomposition and batching tests rewritten to the same rule, `tests/test_public_trace.py` (the hint is shown, `filters` is not).
- **Not covered**: a "the documents do not contain X" answer is still labelled `answered` and its absence statements can be confirmed by the judge against the retrieved passages only — true of the passages, not necessarily of the data room. With the filter gone the known cause is removed; how such a decline should be presented (a new outcome, or the badge) is a product decision recorded in PROGRESS.md.
- **Impact**: src/agents/retrieval_executor.py, src/agents/query_intelligence.py (comment), src/workflow/orchestrator.py, api/security.py, tests/test_agents.py, tests/test_retrieval_batching.py, tests/test_public_trace.py

## Decision 110: Ingest parses PDFs in a child process — a large upload no longer stalls the questions asked meanwhile
- **Date**: Session 25
- **Context**: Blocker H2 asked whether a large ingest slows queries. Measured through the real API with the local model: four questions asked one after another while a 4.4 MB 10-K (AES 2022, FinanceBench, 1,354 chunks, 522 s) was ingested into a scratch deal. Idle, retrieval took 0.23–0.39 s and a question 45–60 s. During the ingest, the first question's retrieval took 33.3 s — 19 s of it embedding three short texts — and finished within a second of the ingest logging "PDF processing complete"; the later ones 1.4 s. Cause, reproduced outside the API: the PDF parse (pdfplumber's pure-Python layout analysis and the table stitcher) ran on `asyncio.to_thread` and held the GIL; the question's embedding and reranking run torch, which releases and re-takes the GIL around every operation, and each re-take waited behind the parse (the convoy effect). An event-loop heartbeat barely moved (median 13–28 ms late, 3–4.5 s stalled over a 43–47 s parse); the question's model work went from 0.10 s to 1.2, 32.2 and 9.6 s.
- **Change**: `src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py` runs `PDFProcessor.process` in a child process (spawn, a fresh child per document) and returns its sections and warnings; ingest's PDF branch calls it. Parses run one at a time behind a lock, so a child that dies or times out was parsing the caller's own file: that upload fails ("the parser stopped", or "took longer than N seconds" after `PDF_PARSE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, default 900 s, when the child is killed), no other upload fails with it, and nothing is retried. The processor's own errors cross unchanged (the client sees the same message). Nothing falls back to parsing in the API's process, where the same file would take the API down. Each child raises its own `oom_score_adj` to 1000 on Linux, so under the container's `mem_limit` the kernel kills the parser before the API that holds the models. Optional steps that fail in the child (`fail_open`) are reported back and counted in the API's metrics. At shutdown a parse still running is stopped rather than waited for past the grace period. Only the parse moves: classification, chunking, PII and risk scans need models the child would load again, and the parse was the stall.
- **Review** (independent, before merge): the first version had no timeout (a parser stuck in a loop would hold every later PDF upload behind it), retried once on a fresh child (with two uploads in flight, an innocent file could be failed by another's), lost the child's optional-step failure counts, and was not stopped at shutdown — all fixed as above, each with a test. Left as is: the rewriter and Agent 1 prompts still call the category a filter (their output is now a hint; changing the prompts changes the model's behaviour for no gain), and an error's `__cause__` arrives as the child's traceback text.
- **Verified**: the question's model work during the same parse, isolated: 0.09–0.13 s over 190 samples (idle 0.11 s), no event-loop stall; output digest equal to the in-process parse. Live, the same contention run: the question asked during the parse spent 0.56 s in retrieval (33.3 s before) and 47.7 s in all (85.6 s); the mean question during the ingest 61.8 s (72.3 s before; idle 51 s); the ingest 516 s and the same 1,354 chunks. Parity: 28 FinanceBench filings (every third cached one) parse to identical sections and warnings in the child and in process; every existing PDF test passes through the child. Gates: 2,878 passed + 3 expected failures (1 Linux-only skip), and 2,887 after the review's fixes (PDF parity rechecked on 5 filings), lint clean, fuzz 72/72, models 7/7 also offline, and the verification, FactCG, golden-retrieval (PASS) and answerability benchmarks with 0 differences from round 16.
- **Remaining contention**: after the parse, ingest embeds every chunk in one call on the shared two-thread pool, and torch's threads share the CPU cores with the question's: retrieval 1.3–1.4 s and NLI validation 15–22 s (10–17 s idle) while it runs. That is CPU sharing, not a stall; on the 2-OCPU host it is the next thing to measure (H2 stays open for the host itself).
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py` (19 + 1 Linux-only, which CI runs: the parse runs in another process; sections and warnings equal an in-process parse for ruled multi-page, borderless statement, partly scanned and legal PDFs; encrypted, corrupt and scanned files raise the same error with the same message; a missing file is still FileNotFoundError; a parser that dies fails its upload once, not retried, and the next upload parses; another upload's hostile file does not fail this one; a parse that never ends times out and the next parses; the timeout setting; the child's failed optional steps are counted in the API; shutdown stops a parse in progress; ingest goes through the child; the child's oom_score_adj is 1000), and every existing PDF test, which now exercises the child.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py (new), src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py (new)

## Decision 111: The re-ingestion report names documents whose table rows lost their column order
- **Date**: Session 25
- **Context**: Decision 108 left server indexes written before it with header-named table rows that a gRPC read returns shuffled, and the re-ingestion report did not flag them. A version bump would flag every older document with tables, most of which read correctly: rows whose columns are placeholders ("Column N") sort by number, and a row with one cell has no order to lose.
- **Change**: `numerical_registry.column_order_known(row)` — beside `row_cells`, sharing its test for a usable `columns` list — is False only for a row with two or more header-named cells and no `columns`. `reingest_report` reads `structured_rows`, counts each document's table chunks with such a row (`unordered_table_chunks`), and plans `reingest` with the reason "N table chunk(s) store rows without their column order".
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_table_column_order.py::test_column_order_known` (7 cases), `tests/test_reingest.py` (header-named rows without `columns` → reingest; with `columns`, placeholders, one cell or no rows → nothing; the report reads the rows).
- **Impact**: src/utils/numerical_registry.py, src/vector_db/reingest_report.py, tests/test_table_column_order.py, tests/test_reingest.py

## Decision 112: An answer that says the documents searched do not contain what was asked ends as "not_found", shown with the refusals
- **Date**: Session 26
- **Context**: Decision 109 removed the known cause of round 16's wrong "the provided documents do not contain" the top-ten customer share (47.8%, in a schedule a category filter hid), but such an answer was still labelled `answered`, could be badged Verified, and carried a confidence score: its absence statements can be confirmed by the judge only against the passages retrieved, true of those and not necessarily of the data room. Owner's choice (2026-11-24): option (a), a distinct outcome shown like a refusal, over badging the statements.
