- **Change**: `claims.not_found_lead(answer)` reads the answer's opening section (its direct answer: after any leading headings, up to the next heading or rule) and recognises a decline of the whole question: no citation and no figure in the section; its first sentence says the evidence as a whole lacks the answer (`NOT_FOUND`: "the provided documents/context/data room do not contain…", "cannot be calculated", "not possible to determine", "based on the provided documents, X is not available") — narrower than `DECLINES_TO_ANSWER`, so "the merger agreement does not contain a financing condition", a finding about a named contract, stays an answer; every other sentence declines too. `run_outcome` returns `not_found` after `synthesis_failed` and `refused` and before `answered_unverified` (the validation status still travels with the response). The API documents the value and counts it under its own metric label; `is_refusal` stays false, so a client that predates the value keeps showing the text as an answer, as before. Both consoles show a refusal-styled banner, "Not found in the passages searched", saying the check covered the passages retrieved, not every document, with what to try; the model's explanation and what it did find follow; the verdict reads "Not found" (never Verified; a failed validation still reads Failed) and no confidence is shown.
- **Measured**: on the 123 recorded live answers of the committed end-to-end runs (`eval/answer_replay_answers.json`) and the 14 of rounds 16–17, the rule picks 9, each read: the eight answers that decline a question the room cannot answer (churn against competitors, FY2025 projections, Q1–Q2 FY2024 revenue, an EV/EBITDA multiple from inputs the answer could not find) and round 16's top-ten answer; it leaves the free-cash-flow answer that declines a total and then answers from the components, a partially disclosed closing-cash answer, round 17's 47.8% answer and the 125 others. Forced refusals were already `refused`.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_not_found.py` (whole declines recognised, partial/cited/figured/named-contract answers not; the replay set picks exactly the eight reviewed ids; outcome precedence), `tests/test_platform_lifecycle.py::test_a_not_found_answer_is_reported_and_counted_as_one` (both routes, metric label), `tests/test_streamlit_truthfulness.py::TestNotFound`, `web/tests/verification.test.ts` and `web/tests/page.test.tsx` (banner, explanation, no Verified, no confidence).
- **Found in review and changed before the merge**: an independent review showed findings that read like gaps: "The transaction documents contain no financing condition" (a bare "documents" after another noun), "In the context of the merger, … is not provided", and agentive or reported impossibilities ("The target could not identify a successor"; and, found while checking, "The outcome of the litigation cannot be determined at this time", "The seller provided insufficient information to the regulator"). A bare noun now counts only as "the documents"/"the context" (not followed by "of"), and an impossibility only in a sentence that names the evidence searched ("… cannot be calculated from the provided documents"); the five are negative tests, and the replay set still picks exactly the eight reviewed answers.
- **Not covered**: a decline worded outside `NOT_FOUND` stays `answered` (the less safe reading, as before this change); a model that writes no opening decline but buries it later is not read. The rule reads wording, so a new model's phrasing should be checked on its first recorded answers.
- **Impact**: src/verification/claims.py, src/workflow/orchestrator.py, api/routes/query.py, api/models/response_models.py, src/utils/metrics.py, app/components/verification.py, app/components/answer_display.py, app/streamlit_app.py, web/lib/{types,normalize,verification}.ts, web/components/{NotFoundPanel,AnswerPanel}.tsx, web/app/page.tsx, tests

## Decision 113: A synthesis context too long for the local model's window is cut to fit and answered, not failed
- **Date**: Session 26
- **Context**: Decision 106 made Ollama refuse a prompt longer than its window (counted with the model's own tokenizer) instead of silently dropping the prompt's start, and a refused synthesis ended the run as `synthesis_failed`. A full synthesis context (MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS, 64,000 characters) does not fit the default 16,384-token window, so on any deal larger than the sample one the local rung — the last rung of the ladder, and the whole ladder with no cloud key — failed every question it was asked.
- **Change**: `LocalContextExceeded` carries Ollama's two counts (prompt tokens, window), read from its refusal (`failures.context_refusal_sizes`), on the blocking and now also the streamed path (which used to raise Ollama's raw error). On that refusal `synthesize_answer` cuts the evidence by what did not fit — the tokens over a target of the window less a reply reserve (a quarter of the window, or the output budget if smaller; with shift=false a reply cannot push the context out), at this prompt's own characters per token, plus a tenth (`fitted_context_chars`) — rebuilds the prompt with that budget (best-ranked evidence first, the same selection as always) and asks the same local model again. At most two fits; the run still fails with Ollama's count when the counts are missing, after two fits, or when under 4,000 characters of evidence would remain. Cloud rungs keep the full context. Each fit (window, counts, evidence before and after) is in the synthesizer's trace entry (`context_fits`, admin trace only).
- **Verified live** (local qwen2.5:14b, Ollama 0.34.4, no cloud key, every call checked local before it was sent): a FinanceBench 10-K (3M 2018) parsed by the production parser, 64,000 characters of evidence, "What were 3M's total net sales in 2018, and how did they compare with 2017?". At 16,384: refused at 19,387 tokens, cut to 35,252 characters, answered "$32,765 million, compared to $31,657 million in 2017", cited, 33.6 s. At 8,192: cut to 10,400 characters, the same figures, cited, 13.9 s. One fit each.
- **Found by the gates and changed before the merge**: the first version widened the ladder loop by the two fits, which also let a provider-wide outage walk two more rungs (`test_exhausted_provider_degrades_to_an_explicit_refusal`: 10 synthesis calls against a bound of 8). A fit's retry now gives its hop back instead, so an outage is bounded as before.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_local_context_fit.py` (the counts read from both message forms; the budget arithmetic; a refused context cut and answered by the same model with the best-ranked passages kept in order; a second refusal cut again, a third fails with the count; a window too small for any evidence fails at once; the streamed path raises the same error with its counts and is fitted too), and `tests/test_local_context_window.py` unchanged.
- **Not covered**: what is cut is not read by the model, and the answer does not say so; a re-synthesis after a failed validation starts from the full context again and pays one more refused call (each refusal is a tokenization, not a generation); the validator still checks the answer against the full retrieved context. The cloud path (no hosted call, by rule).
- **Impact**: src/llm/failures.py, src/llm/litellm_wrapper.py, src/agents/answer_synthesizer.py, tests/test_local_context_fit.py

## Decision 114: DOCX, PPTX and XLSX parse in the child process too
- **Date**: Session 26
- **Context**: Decision 110 moved the PDF parse out of the API's process after measuring it stall questions, and left the other formats in place, unmeasured (outstanding item 10).
- **Measured** (development machine, the method of Decision 110: a question's model work in retrieval — embed three texts, rerank twenty passages — timed repeatedly while ingest's `extract_sections` runs on `asyncio.to_thread`; idle 0.06 s): a 5.9 MB, 12-sheet workbook (48,000 rows) extracts in 23.9 s, 21.3 s of it openpyxl, and put one 2.54 s pause into the question's work (median unchanged); a 2,500-clause contract with tables (DOCX) extracts in 5.0 s, 4.4 s of it the parser, and ran the question's work at a median 0.79 s, 13 times idle; a 250-slide deck (PPTX) 0.8 s, at most 0.20 s. Smaller than the PDF's 33 s, and on the two slower cores of the ARM host each parse takes longer.
- **Change**: `parse_isolation.parse_office(extension, path, doc_id, table_id_prefix)` runs the format's processor — exactly the calls ingest made — in the same child (spawn, one at a time, a fresh process per document, raised OOM score, the timeout) and returns its output; its failed optional steps are counted in the API, its errors cross unchanged, and a parser that dies or times out fails that upload with "The DOCX/PPTX/XLSX could not be parsed: …". `_counting_failed_steps` is shared with the PDF path. A test that patches a processor uses the new `parse_in_process` fixture, since the child imports its own modules.
- **Result** (same files, isolated): the workbook's pause 0.10 s at most (2.54 s), the contract's median 0.06 s (0.79 s; at most 0.54 s, while the API builds its table sections), the deck unchanged; the same sections (24,012, 2,565, 1,000). The workbook's extraction takes 28.9 s instead of 23.9 s: the child's start and 24,012 sections copied back.
- **Found in review and changed before the merge**: an exception crosses back to the API by pickle, and lxml's XMLSyntaxError (python-docx on a malformed part) does not pickle: the client still got "The .docx file could not be parsed", but the API's log recorded "cannot pickle 'lxml.etree._ListErrorLog' object" in place of the cause. The child now returns an exception that does not survive pickling as a RuntimeError naming its type and message (`_crossable`); the pool was never affected.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py` (10 more, among them a malformed DOCX part whose error names XMLSyntaxError while the client's message is unchanged; DOCX, PPTX and XLSX output equal to an in-process parse; an encrypted workbook's and a body-less DOCX's errors cross with the same type and message; a dying parser fails its upload naming the format, and the next parses; ingest goes through the child for each format), and the existing format and robustness tests, which now exercise the child.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py, src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, tests/conftest.py, tests/test_ingestion_formats.py, tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py

## Decision 115: A parser child that dies while idle no longer stops every later upload
- **Date**: Session 26
- **Context**: the first CI run on main after round 18's merge (run 45286375011, Linux, Python 3.11.18) failed 20 tests in streaks with "The PDF/XLSX/PPTX could not be parsed: the parser stopped"; the rerun of the same commit passed, as had the PR's two runs. In the failures the child never ran its task (a patched parser that records each run left no record), and the BrokenProcessPool came from `ProcessPoolExecutor.submit`, which raises it when the pool is already broken.
- **Cause**: with `max_tasks_per_child=1`, CPython starts the replacement child as soon as a parse's child exits (`_adjust_process_count`), and it waits idle with the OOM killer's highest score. When that idle child died, the pool was broken before the next upload; `_run` called `submit` outside its `try`, so the broken pool was never discarded, and every later parse in the process failed — on the development machine, five calls in a row after one kill, the same pool each time. In production that is every upload until the API restarts. A pool race was ruled out first: 300 parses each with Python 3.11.18 and 3.12.16 in Linux containers (2 CPUs, 1 GB), idle and with every core busy, with this pool and with a fresh pool per parse: no break in 1,800.
- **Change**: `parse_isolation._submit` replaces a pool whose `submit` raises BrokenProcessPool and submits to a fresh one, logging "Parser pool broke between parses". `submit` raises before queueing anything, so the upload never reached a child; a file that killed the parser is still not retried.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py::test_a_child_that_dies_between_parses_does_not_fail_the_next_upload` (one parse, the idle child killed, the pool seen broken; the next two uploads parse, equal to an in-process parse). It failed before the change with CI's error.
- **Not covered**: what killed the idle child in that CI run is not known (memory pressure on the runner is one candidate: the child is the OOM killer's first choice by design; Decision 117 found a likelier one in the test suite itself). A child that dies after `submit` has queued an upload but before it reads it still fails that one upload "the parser stopped"; the pool is replaced and the next upload parses.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py, tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py

## Decision 116: The upload panels say where document text goes, before the upload; Dependabot is on
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Context**: Decision 107 proposed a line on the upload panels saying uploaded text goes to a third-party AI service on a free tier, its wording the owner's. The owner, 2026-11-25: draft it and put it in. The terms (aistudio.google.com/docs/terms, effective 2026-03-23, read 2026-11-25), for Unpaid Services: Google uses what is submitted "to provide, improve, and develop Google products and services and machine learning technologies"; "human reviewers may read, annotate, and process your API input and output"; "Do not submit sensitive, confidential, or personal information to the Unpaid Services." (In the EEA, Switzerland and the UK the paid terms apply to unpaid use too.)
- **Change**: `GET /api/v1/data-handling` (public, read rate limit) returns `hosted_llm` and `upload_notice`: with any Gemini key configured (the running tracker's keys, else the environment's), "Google Gemini API" and the notice; with none, both null — every agent then runs on the local model and nothing leaves the server. The draft wording: "Passages from uploaded documents, and your questions, are sent to Google's Gemini API to write and check answers. This deployment uses Gemini's free tier: Google may use what is sent to improve its products, and people at Google may read it. Do not upload confidential, client or personal documents." `UPLOAD_NOTICE` replaces it (shown only while a hosted model is configured). Both upload panels show it before the file picker (web: a note in the upload panel; Streamlit: a warning above the uploader). When the endpoint cannot be read — unreachable, an older server, a malformed reply — they say "Could not confirm whether this deployment sends document text to an outside AI service. Assume it does: …", never nothing; while it loads, and with no hosted model, nothing.
- **Dependabot**: alerts and security updates switched on (repository settings, 2026-11-25), and `.github/dependabot.example.yml` renamed `.github/dependabot.yml` (npm weekly, GitHub Actions monthly, 14-day cooldown; pip still left out, Decision 104). A pip security pull request can still arrive, since security updates read requirements*.txt: its version goes into constraints.txt with scripts/update_constraints.py rather than being merged as it is.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_platform_access.py` (with a key the notice and provider, and no key in the body; with none, nulls even with `UPLOAD_NOTICE` set; the owner's wording; the running tracker's keys decide), `tests/test_console_upload_notice.py` (the server's wording, nothing without a hosted model, the cautious wording for an old, malformed or unreachable server; shown above the file picker), `web/tests/uploadNotice.test.tsx` (the same three states in the panel; `getDataHandling` keeps only text and is null on failure).
- **Not covered**: the notice is shown, not acknowledged — nothing stops an upload, and the API's `/ingest` does not check it. A question typed into the console goes to the same service and has no notice of its own beyond the upload panel's.
- **Impact**: src/llm/data_handling.py (new), api/routes/ingest.py, app/components/document_uploader.py, web/lib/{api,types}.ts, web/components/UploadPanel.tsx, web/app/page.tsx, .env.example, .github/dependabot.yml, tests

## Decision 117: A console test no longer leaves its Streamlit script as the test process's `__main__`
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Found by**: Decision 116's first full suite: 55 failures and 21 errors, every parse "the parser stopped", after its new console test was added.
- **Cause**: Streamlit's AppTest runs a script as `__main__` and leaves it installed. A spawned process runs its parent's main file before its task (`multiprocessing.spawn._fixup_main_from_path`), so after any console test every parser child (Decisions 110, 114) first ran `app/streamlit_app.py` — the whole console, in bare mode — or AppTest's generated script for `from_function`, on which it died at once (`NameError: name '__args' is not defined`). Which one depended on the last console test to run: `test_console_images` alone breaks every later parse; `test_console_tie_out`, last in name order until Decision 116 added `test_console_upload_notice`, left `streamlit_app.py`, which children survived — so the suite passed while each parser child ran the console first. CI installs Streamlit and runs the console tests at 14–15% of the suite, before every ingestion test: the likely trigger of the CI run of Decision 115, whose log keeps no child's stderr to confirm it.
- **Change**: an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` (`_main_module_is_restored`) puts the test process's `__main__` back after each test. Parser children now import pytest's own main, which does nothing when imported. The suite runs 18 s faster on the development machine (229.8 s, 247.7 s before), with no child running the console first.
- **Production**: the API runs `python run_api.py`, whose top level only imports and defines (`main()` is behind `if __name__ == "__main__"`); a parser child imports it and stops there. The Streamlit console parses nothing.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_console_upload_notice.py::test_a_parser_child_still_starts_after_an_app_test` (runs after an AppTest in the same file; fails without the fixture).
- **Impact**: tests/conftest.py, tests/test_console_upload_notice.py

## Decision 118: An answer written from part of the retrieved evidence says so
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Context**: Decision 113 answers from the best-ranked evidence that fits when the local model's window does not hold the full context, and recorded each fit in the admin trace only; its "Not covered" named the gap: the reader is not told that lower-ranked passages were left out. On the 3M 10-K the answer was written from 35,252 of 64,000 characters at 16,384 tokens, and from 10,400 at 8,192.
- **Change**: `answer_synthesizer.evidence_cut(chunks, fitted_chars)` counts the passages and evidence characters the answer was written from against what the full context carries, and the synthesizer returns it as `evidence_cut` when the local model answered from a fitted context (None otherwise, and on a refusal or a failure). `QueryResponse.evidence_cut` carries it on both routes; the synthesis stage's summary ends "from N of M passages (the local model's window)", and its detail keeps the counts (public: counts only; the fits' windows and token counts stay in the admin trace). Both consoles say under the answer: "Written from 6 of the 14 passages retrieved (about 47% of their text): the local model's window could not hold them all. A detail found only in the others is not in this answer." A not-found says too that it "was judged on only 6 of the 14 passages retrieved, so the rest may hold it." The verdict is otherwise unchanged: the claims are still checked against everything retrieved, and what is left out is an omission, not a false statement. The consoles keep the value only when it says something was left out (fewer passages, or less of their text); a server that predates it shows nothing, as before.
- **Verified live** (local qwen2.5:14b, Ollama 0.34.4, no cloud key, every call checked local before it was sent; the 3M 2018 10-K and question of Decision 113, the passages each prompt carried counted from the prompt itself): at 16,384 the prompts carried 47 passages (refused), then 29 and 29, and the reader was told "Written from 29 of the 47 passages retrieved (about 55% of their text) …"; at 8,192, 47 (refused) then 10, and "10 of the 47 … (about 16% of their text)". Both answered "$32,765 million … $31,657 million in 2017", cited. At 16,384 a third request with the same 29 passages followed (63.4 s, against 33.6 s in round 18) on the two runs made right after Ollama started, and not on two later runs with the model loaded ([47, 29], one prose call each in the log): most likely a retry after the cold model load, not the fit; not confirmed.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_local_context_fit.py` (a fitted answer's counts equal the passages in the two prompts; none from the full context or on a failure), `tests/test_platform_lifecycle.py::test_what_a_fitted_context_left_out_reaches_a_public_caller` (both routes, the stage summary and detail; the fits stay admin-only), `tests/test_streamlit_truthfulness.py::TestEvidenceCut`, `web/tests/evidenceCut.test.tsx`.
- **Not covered**: the count is of passages, and a passage can be shorter in the fitted context than in the full one (its own text instead of its parent section), hence the share of text beside it. A revision synthesis starts from the full context again (Decision 113) and reports its own cut.
- **Impact**: src/agents/answer_synthesizer.py, src/workflow/{state_definitions,orchestrator}.py, api/models/response_models.py, api/routes/query.py, api/security.py, app/components/{verification,answer_display}.py, app/streamlit_app.py, web/lib/{types,normalize,verification}.ts, web/components/AnswerPanel.tsx, tests

## Decision 119: The web toolchain is tested on Node 24; Dependabot's first pull requests triaged
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Context**: Dependabot's version updates, switched on by Decision 116, opened ten pull requests within the hour (#61–#70). Reviewing them showed CI testing the web app on Node 20, which reached end of life on 2026-04-30, while the development machine runs Node 24.16 and `@types/node` was on the 22 line; and Vitest 5 (#68) requires Node 22, though its CI run on Node 20 passed.
- **Change**: CI's web job and the dependency audit use Node 24; `@types/node` ^24.19.2 (2026-10-09; with it undici-types 7.24.6, 2026-03-25). `dependabot.yml` ignores the semver-major updates of eslint (ESLint 10 is outside eslint-config-next 15's peer range: `npm ci` fails, #67), typescript (TypeScript 7 rejects the side-effect CSS import in app/layout.tsx, TS2882, #69) — both move with the Next.js upgrade — and @types/node (it follows the Node runtime, not the newest Node; #70 proposed 26).
- **The pull requests, for the owner** (merging them was not authorised in this session): #61–#65 (actions/checkout 7, setup-python 7, setup-node 7, upload-artifact 7, cache 6) and #66 (React 19.3.1, Tailwind 4.3.5, their types) pass CI; the Actions majors move their runtime to Node 24 and their packages to ESM, with no input this repository uses changed and GitHub-hosted runners above the 2.327.1 minimum; upload-artifact and the second cache step run only in the scheduled retrieval eval, which a manual run exercises. #68 (Vitest 5.1) passes CI and fits once this change has moved CI to Node 24. #67, #69 and #70 are superseded by the ignores above.
- **Not covered**: which Node the Vercel deployment runs is set in the Vercel project, outside the repository; it should be 24.x to match.
- **Impact**: .github/workflows/ci.yml, .github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml, .github/dependabot.yml, web/package.json, web/package-lock.json

## Decision 120: A negative finding is no longer read as "not found", and the not-found wording no longer claims a check
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Found by**: an independent review of Decisions 112–118 (one agent, read-only, given the code range and not the author's conclusions), each finding reproduced before it was fixed.
- **Defect 1**: `NOT_FOUND` let the sentence's subject change between the evidence noun and the negation. "The documents show that Acme did not provide audited financial statements to the buyer before signing", "Based on the provided documents, the revolving credit facility was not available for drawdown after the covenant breach" and "The sources confirm that the seller does not provide any indemnity beyond the cap" — findings that answer a question — ended as `not_found`: Verified withheld, confidence hidden, the reader told the information "may still be there". Checking a sibling found one more: "Based on the provided documents, the target was unable to locate the original title deed" (anyone could be "unable to").
- **Change 1**: between the evidence noun and "do not …" / "contains no", nothing that opens another clause — no "that", no reporting verb (show, confirm, indicate, reveal, state, suggest, note, establish, demonstrate, reflect, record); commas and "which" clauses still pass ("The provided documents, including the 10-K and the merger agreement, do not specify …"). "… is not available/disclosed/…" after "based on the documents" must end the clause or name where or for when ("in the data room", "for Q1 FY2024"). "Unable to" is a decline only when the writer is unable ("I/we were unable to", or a sentence that opens "Unable to determine …"). The recorded live answers still give exactly the eight reviewed declines.
- **Defect 2**: the not-found detail said the absence "was checked against the passages retrieved for this question". An absence statement stays unverified unless the judge reads the passages, and nothing was checked when the validator was unavailable. **Change 2**: both consoles say the answer "was written from the passages retrieved for this question, not from every document in the data room, so the information may still be there".
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_not_found.py` (the six findings above are not declines; four phrasings at the edges of the change still are; the replay pin unchanged), `tests/test_streamlit_truthfulness.py::TestNotFound`, `web/tests/verification.test.ts`.
- **Not covered**: wording is still the signal (Decision 112): a finding phrased as a decline of the evidence ("The provided documents do not show any audited financials being delivered") is still read as one.
- **Impact**: src/verification/claims.py, app/components/verification.py, web/lib/verification.ts, tests

## Decision 121: The classifier's sample of a PDF, DOCX or PPTX is read in the parser child
- **Date**: Session 27
- **Found by**: the same review. Without a category override — every web upload sends none — ingest classified the document from a sample read in the API's process before the isolated parse: python-docx built the whole package (`docx.Document`, every paragraph's proxy), python-pptx the whole presentation, PyMuPDF opened the PDF. A file that hangs or crashes a parser did so in the API's process, outside the timeout and the OOM setup, undoing what Decisions 110 and 114 claim for every upload but a categorised one.
- **Change**: the sample (`src/data_processing/content_sample.py`, which imports only the parsers: 78 ms, against ~6 s for ingest_pipeline) is read by `parse_isolation.content_sample` in the child, under the same timeout. A parser's error still gives "" (classification falls back to the file name, as before); a child that dies or times out on the sample fails the upload ("The DOCX could not be parsed: the parser stopped …") before the parse is tried. A text file's sample is still read in the process (no parser). An upload with a category reads no sample, as before.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py` (the sample read in the child equals an in-process read for a PDF, a DOCX and a PPTX; a sampler that dies fails the upload and the parse is not attempted; ingest reads the sample in the child when it classifies, and not with an override).
- **Measured** (development machine, medians of 10, the child already warm as in the API): the sample read in the child costs 0.19–0.27 s against 0.01–0.06 s in the process — the 2,500-clause contract (DOCX) 0.204 s (0.014 s), the 250-slide deck (PPTX) 0.270 s (0.057 s; at most 0.350 s), the 3M and Activision 10-Ks (PDF) 0.192 s and 0.197 s (0.008 s) — the same text every time (40 of 40). The contract's whole classified extraction, ~101 s, was 1.7 s slower than with a category, within its run-to-run spread.
- **Not covered**: not measured on the ARM host, whose two slower cores make each child start longer.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/content_sample.py (new), src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py, src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py

## Decision 122: A file queued to a parser child that was already dead goes to a fresh child, once
- **Date**: Session 28
- **Context**: Decision 115 left one case open: the waiting child dies, the upload's `submit` succeeds because the pool has not noticed yet, and the pool then fails the queued file without any child having read it — "the parser stopped" for an upload that was never parsed. Measured on `main` (scratchpad `r20/race.py`: kill the waiting child, parse at once): the first round failed that way. Decision 115's path (the submit itself sees the broken pool) was taken in 0 of 20 rounds; this one is the common case, not a corner.
- **Change**: each pool shares a flag (`multiprocessing` RawValue, passed to its children through the initializer) that the child sets before it runs the file. `_run` clears it at submit. A BrokenProcessPool with the flag still clear means no child began the file: the pool is replaced and the file submitted once more, within the time left of the same timeout, logging "Parser child stopped before it began this file". A file the child had begun is still not retried — it may be the file that killed it — and a second loss fails the upload as before.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py`: the flag set in the child is read in the API's process; a file queued to a broken pool before start parses on a fresh one; lost twice, the upload fails "the parser stopped"; the real race three times; the existing hostile-file test still counts one run (not retried).
- **Measured** (development machine, `r20/race.py`, 20 rounds): 20 of 20 parsed, equal to an in-process parse, all 20 through the new path; on `main` the first round failed.
- **Also fixed** (found by the new race test in the full suite): a discarded pool was shut down with `wait=False`, which clears the pool's process table at once while its manager thread may still be starting a replacement child from it — a `TypeError` in that thread (`object of type 'NoneType' has no len()`), and the child it was starting left waiting with nothing to stop it. Since Decisions 110 and 115 every discard did this. The shutdown now waits for the manager thread, on a thread of its own so the upload is not held up. The full suite passes with unhandled thread exceptions turned into errors (2,973 passed).
- **Not covered**: a child killed between setting the flag and finishing the file (for any reason — the OOM killer, an operator) is indistinguishable from a file that killed it and still fails its upload.
- **Impact**: src/data_processing/parse_isolation.py, tests/test_pdf_parse_isolation.py

## Decision 123: No cross-process upload lock; the one other writer is pinned never to stage
- **Date**: Session 28
- **Context**: outstanding issue 6 (from Decision 101): uploads of the same bytes are serialised by an in-process lock, so "two processes uploading the same bytes at once are not serialised". Checked which second process can exist before building a lock across processes: the API refuses a second worker at startup unless `ALLOW_MULTIPLE_WORKERS=1` (Decision 58's guard, `api/security.py`; eval scripts write to their own in-memory or on-disk stores), and the only other process that writes to the index is the re-ingest run (`reingest_run`, run as a separate container in DEPLOYMENT.md). It re-indexes committed documents in place — `plan_reingest` takes only report rows marked `reingest`, and a staged, never-committed upload is listed apart, not as a document — so it never writes a staged point and cannot race a first upload's staging.
- **Decision**: no cross-process lock. A file lock would need a volume both containers share (they share none today), and a Postgres advisory lock would make every upload depend on Postgres, which ingest does not touch now; both guard a case the shipped configuration cannot reach.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_staged_ingest.py::TestTheOtherWriter` — with a committed old document and a cut-short first upload of another in the same deal, the re-ingest run re-indexes only the committed one, every point it writes carries `deal_id` and no staging marker, and the staged points are left as they were.
- **Documented**: DEPLOYMENT.md's single-worker paragraph now says `ALLOW_MULTIPLE_WORKERS=1` also drops the serialisation of same-file uploads.
- **Impact**: tests/test_staged_ingest.py, DEPLOYMENT.md, PROGRESS.md

## Decision 124: An upload's embedding waits for a question's model work
- **Date**: Session 28
- **Context**: outstanding issue 10. Decision 110 measured, on the whole development machine, a question's retrieval at 1.3–1.4 s (0.3 s idle) while ingest embedded a 10-K's chunks in one call on the two-thread pool questions share, and left the host to measure. Measured now on an emulated two-core host (Decision 71's setup: two cores by affinity, two torch threads, no GPU, the MiniLM reranker; scratchpad `r20/embed_lag.py`), with a question's retrieval model work (embedding three texts, reranking twenty passages) repeated every 0.2 s while 48 chunks of the 3M 10-K were embedded: 0.33 s idle; 6.5–9.5 s at the median and 15–20 s at most during the one-call embedding (three runs). A call already running cannot be paused; the CPU is simply shared.
- **Change**:
  - `src/utils/model_priority.py`: a process-wide count of a question's model calls in progress. `embed_texts_async`, `rerank_pairs_async` (and so `rerank_async`) and `nli.ascore_pairs` run inside `foreground()`.
  - `reranker.embed_texts_background`, ingest's default `embed_fn`: on the CPU one text a call, in the length order `encode()` itself sorts by, and before each call it waits while a question's call is running, at most `INGEST_YIELD_MAX_WAIT_S` (default 10 s) so questions back to back cannot hold an upload back for good. On a GPU, all texts in one call as before (batching is what makes it fast there). `INGEST_EMBED_SLICE` overrides the texts per call (0 = one call).
- **Measured** (same setup, the production function):

  | | question's model work, median / p90 / max | 48 chunks embedded in |
  |---|---|---|
  | one call (main) | 9.5 / 14.1 / 15.0 s | 99 s |
  | one text a call, yielding | 1.0 / 1.7 / 3.0 s | 89 s |

  Slices of 2 and 4 texts gave 2.4 s and 4.6 s at the median (the slice already running when a question arrives is what remains). On the whole development machine (24 threads, no GPU, 192 chunks, two runs each): 0.23 s against 0.38–0.44 s at the median, and the embedding took 69–72 s against 78–82 s — one text a call is not slower on the CPU, as batches of 32 pad every text to the longest.
- **Vectors**: a vector embedded alone differs from the same text's vector in a batch of 32 by float rounding — at most 1.2e-6 per component over 192 chunks (a one-call embedding repeats exactly, run to run). Stored vectors written on the CPU therefore change at that level; on a GPU they do not change. The gates below compare retrieval on the CPU both ways.
- **Guarded by**: `tests/test_model_priority.py` (15): the count nests and is released on error; the wait ends when the question's call ends and at the cap; one text a call, longest first, results back in input order; one call on a GPU; `INGEST_EMBED_SLICE`; a slice starts only after a question's call ends, and never while one runs; back-to-back questions hold it at most the cap per slice; a question's embedding, rerank and NLI calls are marked; ingest embeds through the yielding path by default.
- **Gates** (with Decisions 122 and 123): offline suite 2,988 passed, 3 xfailed (one test of this decision first failed in the full suite only: another test replaces the reranker module in `sys.modules`, so it now patches by path); ruff clean; `-m models` 7/7, also with the Hub offline; fuzz 72/72; the verification gate, FactCG, the golden set (PASS) and answerability on the GPU identical to round 19 (0 differences each — on a GPU ingest still embeds in one call); PDF parity 28/28. On the CPU, the golden set and answerability run with one text a call and with one call (`INGEST_EMBED_SLICE=0`) are identical field for field (0 differences; both PASS): the rounding in the stored vectors moved no ranking.
- **Not covered**: an upload's other model work (Laya classification, PII and risk scans, sparse vectors) still shares the CPU unmarked; a slice already running when a question arrives (one chunk, ~1.4 s on the emulated host) is not interrupted; LLM calls are not model work here, so an upload proceeds while a question waits on its LLM. Not measured on the ARM host (H2).
- **Impact**: src/utils/model_priority.py (new), src/vector_db/reranker.py, src/verification/nli.py, src/data_processing/ingest_pipeline.py, tests/test_model_priority.py, .env.example, PROGRESS.md
